420. **The narrow form of the margin index** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20): on one column, does a rail entry render immediately above its own clause in the document flow (recommended — "beside" is a claim about adjacency in document order, and a phone's only axis of adjacency is vertical), or do clauses carry marks only, with entries reachable from them? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §4 (2026-08-23): the second — marks in the gutter, the pinned population in a bottom dock (Q657).*

421. **The fold triangle is invisible until hover** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20). On touch it must always be visible — should it then always be visible on desktop too, rather than the surface having two different answers? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §4 (2026-08-23): visible under `(hover: none)`, hover-revealed elsewhere — one rule, two renderings (Q658).*

422. **Judging offline** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20): offline is read-only on the blindness argument — a judgment cast against a stale pair is a judgment on a question that no longer exists. Is a *queued* judgment ever acceptable if the client re-validates the pair on reconnect and discards silently when stale? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §3.e (2026-08-23): no — the radio position survives and is restored if the same pair is still served; the member presses ✓.*

423. **Does `room-pulse` need a third state on a phone?** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20). It says *the room is moving*, and a backgrounded PWA cannot say anything at all — is silence distinguishable from stillness, and should it be? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23, against the recommendation: **no pulse in the mobile app** — `design/MOBILE.md` §3.f; the offline word stays on the clock line.*

424. **Multi-block drafting on touch** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20). A selection spanning blocks is one candidate on desktop; the caret proxy (prose `contenteditable="false"` on touch, since mobile IMEs do not honour `preventDefault()` on `beforeinput`) has no obvious gesture for it. *Deferred by `design/MOBILE.md` (2026-08-23) to stage 6; §1.7 names the seam — the door is the lane's ✏️ and a long-press on a clause, never the caret.*

425. **Do lanes collapse or scroll on narrow** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20) when a card carries two candidates side by side? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §4 (2026-08-23): closes — every candidate pair is already a stacked `.propblock`; asserted as no card overflow at 360px.*

426. **One PWA icon or many** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20) — the icon identifies the app, but a member may be in several documents. *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §3.b (2026-08-23): one icon art, a manifest per document, many installs (Q669).*

427. **`--border` contrast at phone brightness** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20) — several hairlines are below 3:1 and load-bearing (the incumbent's dashed edge in the sealed record, for one). *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §4 (2026-08-23): a `--border-strong` token for the load-bearing edges, users named in the selector, surface-wide (Q659).*

428. **Where do push subscriptions live** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20), given the log is append-only and a subscription is deletable per-device state — exactly the kind of thing the `people` table (Q436's schema) exists for? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23 as recommended in `design/MOBILE.md` §3.d (2026-08-23): the `people` table is adopted but unbuilt — a `push_subscriptions` table now with a nullable `person_id`, both stores, never the log (Q673).*

429. **How big is the offline shell allowed to be** (mobile plan, 2026-08-20) before caching the surface needs its own budget? *Answered by Ed 2026-08-23, against the recommendation: **no budget** — `design/MOBILE.md` §3.c.*





463. **The close is undesigned, and by this session's own logic it needs 🍾's treatment** (raised 2026-08-20). Everything decided today rests on two rules — **a thing that happens should be a thing somebody does**, and **no power arrives without acknowledgment**. Both have an unexamined mirror at the other end of a document's life. A windowed document reaches its closing date; a perpetual one freezes when active plus holding falls below quorum. Neither moment has been designed at all, and both are larger than 🍾: the close is when the document becomes **final**, which is the only irreversible thing in the mechanism. **Ed, 2026-08-22 (spec pass 1, item 567): the communication of the closing ceremony has not been designed at all — put it in the backlog.** What a member is told at the close beyond the 🥂 card and the one mail (SURFACE.md E24) is this item's.

    What the symmetry asks:
    (a) **Is there a closing act, and whose?** 🍾 is the founder's, because before the start they are the only one who can act. At the close there is a whole room, so a founder-pressed ending would be the one unilateral power nobody granted them — which argues the close is either automatic (the clock) or a consensus act (🏛️). *Ending the document at all* is already constitutional, so the machinery exists.
    (b) **Do powers leave with an acknowledgment?** By 453's rule they arrived with one. Unspent ✏️s become meaningless at the close; a 🏛️ in flight has nowhere to land. Something should say so, and the wallet emptying is the obvious place — the inverse of the pencil-storm.
    (c) **What does the last hour look like?** A rising bar means late changes need near-unanimity, and a member who wants one last fix should be able to see that the window is closing. Nothing on the surface says how long is left.
    (d) **What happens to live races at the close?** They degrade to backlog, per the spec — but a member watching their own proposal die in a race that ran out of time is a real moment with no design.

464. **The record is undesigned, and it is what the machine produces** (raised 2026-08-20). `record-builder` has been in the glossary since the start — *final text plus the record: rankings, camps, graveyard, care map, minority map, backlog, audit log* — and no surface has ever been drawn for it. The per-decision `sealed record` card exists and is good, but that is one clause's history; the **document-level** artifact, the thing a convention hands to the people who were not there, does not exist as a design.

    It is worth raising now rather than later because two things this month bear directly on it. The **constitution-as-document-text** work means the finished artifact already has a natural shape — the rules, then the charter — and the record is the third part. And **the stranger's door** (456) is the first surface built for somebody outside the room, which is exactly the record's audience.

    (a) Is the record a **separate document**, or the same document read in a closed state — the redacted page's opposite, everything open?
    (b) Does it have its own URL, and is it the one people share?
    (c) How much of the graveyard and the minority map is public, given authorship is sealed by default and the record is where seals come off (§3.4)?

    **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): *ratify what is built — same document, same URL, read closed — and treat (c) as the only live question.*** So **(a) and (b) are settled by ratification and are spent**: the record is not a separate document and has no URL of its own; it is this document read in its closed state, which is what `closedBlocks` and `.doc.closedpage` already build — every clause grey, every tab filed, the `sealed-speaker` revealed, wallets and pulse gone, the document ending bracketed as constitution · charter · backlog with the signatures beneath. Nothing is owed for either half; the tree is the answer and this records that it was chosen rather than drifted into. **(c) is the only live question**, and it is the one that needs a mechanism rather than a page: how much of the graveyard and the minority map a **stranger** may read, given §3.4's seals come off at the close and §3.5a's judgments ladder now has a per-decision reveal rung coming (**996**). Those two interact — a document that reveals judgments as each decision settles has already published most of what (c) is about — so (c) should be answered **after** 996 is built, not before. **(a) and (b) spent; (c) open.**

465. **Notifications are the surface nobody has designed and everything depends on** (raised 2026-08-20). A convention runs over days or weeks and the participants are not sitting in the page. `router` has carried *notification digests* as a named part since the beginning, with no design and no copy. Every other decision this month assumes people come back — the drip accrues, the bar rises, lapse warns before it bites, a 👑 question waits, a motion needs everybody — and all of it is inert if the mail is wrong.

    (a) What is the default cadence, and is it a **setting** (in which case, whose — and is it constitutional by the test, since it changes who effectively participates)?
    (b) What does a digest contain, given the blind discipline? Counts and your own tasks are safe; anything about standings is not (§3.5).
    (c) Does a 🔥 — *the question you are being asked next* — ever justify an immediate mail, or is everything batched?
    (d) The lapse warning and the freeze alarm already have to be mails. They are the two that exist by necessity, so they are the place the vocabulary gets set.

    **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): *take the whole thing as one design pass with its own plan.*** So (a)–(d) are not four questions to be answered one at a time in the register — they are the agenda of a design pass, and the pass is what answers them. That is the same shape as **1009**'s extraction and **464**'s (c): a commissioning, not a resolution, and the item stays open until the pass has run. **What the plan inherits**, so it does not start from nothing: the mails that already exist are the founding invitation, the magic link, the §9.7a save notice and the close (`MAILS` on the page, `mail-fold` in `packages/server`), `design/STYLE.md` §6 already holds the mail copy rules, and **the lapse warning and the freeze alarm are the two the mechanism obliges** — (d) is therefore where the pass should start rather than where it ends. **Answered as a commission; the design pass is owed and unwritten.**

490. **Does the alpha's usability come before Postgres?** (raised in housekeeping, 2026-08-20, reading PRODUCTION.md's stage order against what docs.vote can actually do today.) Two stages stand between *live* and *a friend can use it*, and both sit behind stage 6's 2–3 weeks of database work. **Stage 9** (Resend on mail.docs.vote) is the plan's own "the product actually works": until it lands the sandbox sender delivers only to Ed's own address, so an invitation goes nowhere and a document cannot have a second member. **Stage 8** (the surface merge) fills the hole the plan itself names — `session-view.html` is never served, so a constituted document has **no drafting surface**: docs.vote today can found a constitution and then cannot draft the thing the constitution is about. Meanwhile the case for Postgres-first is real and not to be waved away — it is durability, and every day of JSONL is a day where a disk incident loses documents that were somebody's actual constitution, with no backups until stage 11. The tension is that the current order optimises for *not losing* data the alpha is not yet generating, because the alpha cannot yet generate it. Three shapes: **(a)** as written — durability first, on the reasoning that the moment there is anything to lose it is too late to be building the store; **(b)** 9 then 6 — mail is 2–3 days and turns a demo into a product with real users, and those users are what makes stage 6 urgent rather than theoretical; **(c)** 9, then 8, then 6 — the whole alpha experience first, accepting that a JSONL disk holds a handful of Ed-and-friends documents for a month, with a nightly copy off the disk as the cheap interim (an hour's work, not stage 11's drill). My recommendation is **(c) with the interim backup**, and the reason is what the alpha is *for*: the thing that is scarce right now is not durability, it is finding out whether the mechanism survives contact with five real people, and neither of the two stages that lets it be tried is expensive. If it is (a), the honest consequence to accept is that docs.vote stays a founding demo for a month, and the alpha flag should probably say so.

500. **The legal drafts' decisions.** (Stage 12, 2026-08-20; the drafts are `docs/legal/PRIVACY.md` and `TERMS.md`, placeholders marked, not in force.) The eight decisions that have to be made before either can be linked, from `docs/legal/README.md`: **(a)** who the legal entity is; **(b)** controller for everything, or controller for account data and processor for each document on the founder's behalf — the drafts assume the latter; **(c)** publish now with erasure done by hand, or wait for the `person_id` mechanism; **(d)** keep the operator-notification mail carrying the founder's address, or reduce it to title-and-URL; **(e)** retention for a closed document — a period, or each group setting its own as a rule; **(f)** whether the Terms promise a moderation process there is no mechanism for; **(g)** self-host the web font (deletes a third-party disclosure and a CSP exception); **(h)** whether member emails staying visible to other members is intended. The drafting agent's two independent findings ride (g) and (h): Google Fonts is currently the only third party beyond the three providers, and most members will assume their address is private. — **Parked by Ed, 2026-08-29: *park the whole item until go-live is actually scheduled.*** All eight, not a subset — the answers turn on facts nobody has (the entity, the launch date, who the first real members are), and eight guesses written into a document that is not in force is worse than eight blanks. **Parked, not spent**, the way **46** is: nothing here is decided and nothing is owed until there is a date. Two things ride along and should be re-read at that point rather than now, because both drifted while this sat: **(d)** is the same decision as **482**, which Ed has since answered — the operator mail keeps the founder's address — so (d) inherits that unless the legal reading overrides it; and **436**'s `person_id` is a stage 5/6 schema decision that (c) depends on and does not control. `PRODUCTION.md`'s stage-12 line carries the park.

504. **The setup-probe's founding scenario is half dead.** (Found re-freezing the references, 2026-08-21.) Its selector map predates the surface: step 6 looks for `[data-act="sendverify"]`, and the page has sent the verification on the card's ✓ (`[data-confirm]` while 📧 is open) since the commit-row grammar landed — so `click-magic-link`, `open-policy`, `choose-holder`, `choose-join`, `open-membership`, `paste-invites`, `send-invites` and `motions:close-card` all miss, identically on both sides. The guard is real for steps 1–5 and for whatever later steps still find their targets, and empty after that. Fixing it means re-deriving the founding flow (send on ✓, the policy card's birth, the invite box), not swapping selectors. Related: the `pre-constitution` reference had drifted 200 diffs from HEAD before stage 8 noticed, so **a probe that nobody runs rots silently** — worth a CI job that serves `design/` and runs both probes headless (Q-size: a day). **Ed, 2026-08-21: (b) — the CI job. Built.** **(a) closed the same day**, on the back of the one-at-a-time founding: the scenario was re-derived against the surface as it now runs (the commit *is* the send; the settings arrive one at a time, so a step can only reach the card the one before it opened), the reference was re-frozen, and the run is **36 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed, no dead steps**. CI's probe job is `--strict` from now on, so a step whose target goes missing fails rather than warns. The drive itself is `scripts/founding-walk.mjs`, which walks a blank arrival to a settled constitution and prints every card's strings — the audit tool the STYLE passes now read.

505. *Spent in chat (2026-08-21) on a misreading of Ed's 🪶→📍→📧 note; the matter is Q460. Kept so the sequence stays honest.*

507. **The power lines' other halves** (from Ed's rewording, 2026-08-21: *The Founder may change this at will.* / *Decisions from the membership require assent.*). Applied as asked, and the two negatives made symmetric on my own judgment — *The Founder does not change this at will.* / *Decisions from the membership apply by themselves.* — for Ed to veto. Open: whether the per-setting variants follow the new shape — membership (*The Founder invites and removes directly* / *An application the members carry comes to the Founder as a 👑 question*), the Text (*The Founder edits the text directly* / *A change to the text that carries waits on the Founder's OK before it lands*) — and the option labels under the power cards, which still say *directly* and *the Founder's OK*. (a) Rewrite them all in the new voice (*at will*, *require assent*); (b) keep the specific ones specific, since they name the act.

508. **Is the membership list readable at the stranger's door?** (Server half of item 7, 2026-08-21.) The rule built is *the rules are public while the text is private*: a stranger reads every standing value, holder and power, and the founder's name and picture (the Founded line is part of the rules), but **not the members' names** — only how many have arrived — though the Members list is, on the page, a section of the constitution. (a) Serve the names (and pictures) too: a stranger deciding whether to knock may want to know who is in the room, and the list is constitutional text; (b) keep them private: a member's name is how they appear *in the room* (§9.0c), and a stranger is not in it; (c) serve them only where 🌍 is at least *link*. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (c).** Built both halves: the door serves the arrived members’ names and pictures exactly where it serves the text, and the count alone where it does not — the Members list is a section of the very constitution the door is showing, so it rides 🌍 with the rest of it. Pinned by a server test.

509. **Joining by arrival under an *open* join policy** (stranger's door, 2026-08-21). The policy's fourth rung is *open* — anyone with the link may join — and the door's card says *Join*; but `/api/d/:slug/apply` starts an *application* under every non-invite rung, so under *open* the Join card sends an apply mail and the applicant is admitted by the ordinary route rather than simply arriving. (a) Under *open*, the login mail for a new address creates the member at the click — arrival is joining, as the rung says; (b) keep the application path and let *open* mean "applications are admitted without a race". **Ed, 2026-08-21: (a).** **Not yet built** — the one answer of that batch that is a feature rather than a wording or a pacing change: it mints a membership from a login link, touching auth, the register and the module’s invite/arrive pair, and it wants its own tests. Next session’s first job. Until then the door is the truthful **Log In** card that states the policy and promises no mail it cannot send. **Interim shipped 2026-08-21** (review #2 found the door promising a mail it cannot send: the card said *Join*, told the reader *the link it sends is the login*, and then sat on *Check your inbox* forever, because `/login` deliberately mails a non-member nothing). Until this is answered the door states the policy — *Anyone with the link may join* — and offers the login it can actually perform; the *Join* affordance comes back with (a).

510. **Does the member surface's lockline tell the truth about who set a value?** (Review #2, 2026-08-21.) The shared `readBody` — what anybody sees on a settled card they did not set — has always opened with *Set by the founder when the document was made.*, whatever actually settled it. On a **member's** surface the half underneath corrects it (a question the room answered appends `watchBody`, *The members decide*), so the line has been half-wrong rather than wrong. At the **stranger's door** there is no second half, so the door was attributing every ceremony- and motion-settled rule to the founder; the body now takes its sentence from the caller (STYLE §5) and the door says *Decided by the members.* (a) Give the member surface the same truthful line, so one body says one thing everywhere — costs a reference re-freeze, since it changes settled cards on the frozen page; (b) leave it: on a member's surface the pair of lines together is accurate, and *set by the founder when the document was made* is doing a second job — saying **when**, which the members' line does not. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (a).** Built: *Decided by the members.* where the room settled it, *Set by the founder when the document was made.* where the founder did — so the *when* survives exactly where it is true.
511. **Should the founder meet a delegation default at all?** (From Ed's *remove the defaults*, 2026-08-21.) Implemented as written: nothing arrives chosen — not the value, not the number, and **not the holder** — because only that produces the sentence he asked for, *The Founder is deciding [x]*, on every clause. A setting that arrived delegated would read *The Founder has delegated to members to decide x — waiting for members*, which states an answer the founder never gave. But it retires a documented rule with a real argument behind it (CLAUDE.md, the two kinds of decision, 4): **constitutional settings default to the members, ordinary ones to the founder**, on the reasoning that the room can take an ordinary one back cheaply and cannot take a constitutional one back at all. Two consequences on the surface: a founder now keeps everything unless they act, and three cards (⏱️ ✒️ 👥) open on a lone *I set it* rung with no visible alternative, since delegation lives on the ✒️/🛡️ tabs by design. **(a)** As built — no defaults anywhere, delegation is a click. **(b)** Keep the value and the number empty but let constitutional settings arrive delegated, accepting the delegated sentence on those clauses. **(c)** As built, plus a line under *I set it* pointing at the tabs — the cheapest fix for the lone-rung problem without restoring a default. My recommendation is **(a) with (c)'s line**, but the rule it retires is yours. **Ed, 2026-08-21: delegation is an option on the decision card** — *Delegate to the membership*, *which is essentially the same as “skip this and go to the next one”, because it means “don't decide now”*. Built: a rung after the values (it is not one of them), rendered once for whatever card is open, on every delegable setting and no other, and pre-start only. This retires CLAUDE.md's *“The members decide” appears in no value list* — the ✒️/🛡️ tabs still perform the same act, but a founder walking the constitution meets it where the question is. **CLOSED as (a), 2026-08-21, and now true in the module rather than only on the fixture.** Ed, walking a real document on docs.vote: *constitutional clauses are appearing a whole section at a time, rather than a clause at a time. They're also appearing already delegated, whereas they should begin undecided.* Both symptoms were one cause, and it was never the page: `document-opened` folded every delegable constitutional setting to `holder: 'members'` with `collecting: true`, so ten blind questions were open at the instant of creation; the live page's `hydrateS` faithfully marked each members-held setting *seen*, `settled()` treats a members-held setting as settled by construction, and `orderReady` — which asks only whether everything above it has settled — therefore released the whole constitution at once. The fixture never reaches `hydrateS`, which is why the founding walk and both probes had been honestly green on a path that did not exercise it. **SPEC §9.0a is amended (v0.63)**: nothing arrives delegated, every setting is born convenor-held with both powers and its question shut, and delegation is the act that opens a blind question. A holder radio now reports an act rather than a default — delegated reads *roster*, held-and-set reads *founder*, held-and-unset stays the undecided blank — because after the change `holder === 'convenor'` no longer distinguishes *kept* from *untouched*.

513. **Is the Text last what you want?** (Consequence of the constitution-order pacing, 2026-08-21.) 📄 is the last clause of the constitution, so under the new rule the founder answers **eighteen** questions before being asked for the document's own words. Nothing stops them typing into the prose column earlier — it stands under the constitution the whole time — but the *task* comes last, and the 💡 Proposing gate opens only when it settles. **(a)** As built: set the rules, then write the thing. **(b)** 📄 moves to just after the birth, on the argument that the text is what the founder came to write and the rules are about it. **(c)** 📄 stays last but the prose column carries an invitation to start writing at any time. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (c)**, *think about the best point in the walk for it to appear* — **the save**, the first moment there is anywhere to put the words and the moment the column becomes the founder's. From there the empty column says *Start writing whenever you like*, in grey, asking nothing; at 📄's own turn it becomes the paste-well it always was. (Rejected: after the first setting settles, which would make the invitation a reward for answering something, and it is not one.)

514. **Is a grant a task?** (Same walk.) Ed's rule is one task at a time; a **grant** — ✒️ at the save, 🏛️ at arrival — is news rather than a question, and stands in the rail alongside whatever is being asked, so the rail briefly holds two entries. It cannot be sequenced like a question (it announces a power that has already arrived, and staging it would mean handing somebody a pen and not saying so). **(a)** As built — a grant sits beside the current question. **(b)** A grant takes its turn like everything else, arriving only when the rail is otherwise empty. **(c)** A grant is not a rail entry at all: the wallet animates and the constitution states it, with no acknowledgment asked. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (a), agreed.** A grant announces a fact rather than asking a question, and holding it back would mean handing somebody a pen without saying so.
515. **What is the keyboard's one-second hold?** (From Ed's report that 🪶 feels instant, 2026-08-21.) A wallet commit — 🪶, ✒️, ✏️, 🏛️ — is held: the glyph flies from the wallet and the act lands when it arrives. A **keyboard** activation has no pointer behind it, so it acts at once, which is how it has always worked and is the only reason a held commit can be reached without a mouse at all. But it means the lesson the hold exists to teach (an act costs something) is skipped by anybody pressing Enter, and on the birth's own card that is the first thing many people will do. **(a)** Leave it: a click with no pointer behind it acts at once, and accessibility beats the lesson. **(b)** Hold **Space** — the browser's own repeat on a focused button — with the same flight and the same release-cancels rule; Enter still acts at once, so nothing becomes unreachable. **(c)** Enter arms it and a second Enter confirms, which is a hold in two acts rather than one. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (a)** — a click with no pointer behind it acts at once, and accessibility beats the lesson; built already, nothing to change. (My recommendation had been (b): it is the same gesture in the same place, and it leaves the escape hatch open. Related, unreproduced: Ed saw an instant commit *changing a title during the birth*; a 150ms press commits nothing in every test I could write (title, re-titled title, link, email, and a post-save ✒️), so if it is not the keyboard I need to know what the gesture was.
516. **The 🛡️ wording, and whether 🍾 has a wallet.** Two threads from Ed, 2026-08-21.
    **(i) 🛡️'s sentence.** *at will* is settled for ✒️ and every line now uses it; *requires assent* is "ok" but not loved. The suggestion that follows Ed's own description of the pair — *a philosophical sword and shield: one writes, one only refuses* — is to make the two clauses mirror each other: **The Founder may change this at will.** / **The Founder may refuse a change the membership makes.** *May change* against *may refuse* says the asymmetry in the verbs themselves, needs no term of art, and reads as one sentence of a document rather than as a rule about a mechanism. Alternatives: *Nothing the membership decides takes effect until the Founder accepts it* (longest, clearest about the consequence); *The Founder has a veto over what the membership decides* (shortest, but *veto* is a borrowed word this vocabulary has avoided); or keep *requires assent*, which is the only one that survives being said about a **setting** as well as about a person. Whichever wins, the negatives follow it: *does not change this at will* / *may not refuse a change the membership makes*.
    **(ii) Does 🍾 have a wallet?** My answer is **no** — and the reason is the family's own test. The four wallets are the four verbs, and the four verbs are a ladder of **who has to agree**: 🪶 nobody (nothing exists yet), ✒️ nobody (you hold the power), ✏️ enough of the room, 🏛️ everybody. Each is a *capacity you hold* — spent and refilled, or held permanently. 🍾 is not a level of authority and not a capacity; it is a **moment**, and it happens once. A wallet holding one thing once is a button with a balance drawn on it. It would also beg a 🥂 wallet for the mirror act, and the close is precisely the act nobody performs — the clock does it. So: not overboard on the four, but 🍾 is where the family stops. **What is worth fixing** is what prompted the question: 🍾's commit currently reads **✒️ Begin**, borrowing the drafting pen for an act that is not drafting — beginning is what *ends* the founding. Options: **(a)** the button becomes **🍾 Begin** and the hold flies the 🍾 itself from the button into the document (a pop, not a spend) — the hold machinery keys on the glyph, so this is a real change rather than a relabel; **(b)** leave it as ✒️ Begin, on the grounds that the founder's last act with the pen is laying it down. I recommend (a).
517. **Does the same rule cover an *ordinary* delegated setting?** (From Ed's ⏳ note, 2026-08-21, which said **constitutional cards only**.) Built as asked: a constitutional card waiting on the room keeps its subject glyph, greys, and leaves the rail. But ⏱️ the proposal rate and 🤖 AI proposals are **ordinary** and delegable too, and a member watching one of those can act on it no more than they can act on a constitutional one — the same *grey means nothing is being asked of you*. The asymmetry that would justify keeping ⏳ on them: an ordinary setting can be re-proposed by anybody at the threshold at any time, so a waiting one is arguably still *live* to you in a way a constitutional one is not. **(a)** Extend the rule to every delegated setting, ordinary included; **(b)** keep it constitutional-only as asked, and let ⏱️ and 🤖 keep their ⏳ entries. I lean (a) — the reason the rail entry was noise is the same in both cases — but it is your line, and (b) is what you asked for. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): *extend the rule to every delegated setting, ordinary included* — option (a).** So the predicate is *delegated and waiting on the room*, and the kind stops entering into it: what makes the rail entry noise is that nothing is being asked of **you**, which is equally true whichever route the setting takes. **Folded 2026-08-29 by the batch AD pre-flight** into SURFACE §6's `wait` row, which now says *a delegated card waiting on the room* where it said *a constitutional card*. **What is owed is two predicates and nothing else**: `design/session-view.html` `~L3469` (`.filter((c) => !(c.kind === 'constitutional' && stateOf(c, ctx) === 'wait'))` — the rail's own filter) and `design/setup.js` `~L190` (`if (st === 'wait' && c.kind === 'constitutional') return c.g;` — the tab's glyph), each losing its `kind` test, with the comment above each rewritten off *constitutional*. **Which cards this actually reaches**: the ordinary delegable settings are exactly `rate` (⏱️) and `machines` (🤖), and 🤖 has had no card since entry 251 — so ⏱️ is the only card a member will see change, plus a replayed document that delegated 🤖 before the retirement. **Answered, owed, not built.**
518. **Should the empty document be pre-filled with something to edit?** (Ed, 2026-08-21, on Q513(c): *I am tempted to pre-fill it with something like "We the people…", to invite an edit.*) The appeal is real: an empty column is the hardest thing in any editor to start, and a first line somebody wants to change is the fastest way to teach that the text is editable at all. Against it, two things this surface has already decided. The column is where the **answer** goes, and *Untitled* left the title heading that same morning on exactly that ground — helper text standing where the answer goes reads as an answer somebody else gave. And a charter that opens *We the people* arrives with a voice and a tradition already in it, which is a strong thing to put in a room’s mouth before it has said anything. Three shapes: **(a)** leave it empty with the grey invitation that is there now (built); **(b)** pre-fill real prose as **document text**, which the founder edits or deletes; **(c)** pre-fill it as a **placeholder** — grey, not in the document, gone at the first keystroke — so the shape of a charter is suggested without anything being said on the room’s behalf. **(c)** is the one that gets the teaching without the ventriloquism, and my recommendation. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): *pre-fill as a grey placeholder — not document text, gone at the first keystroke* — option (c), as recommended.** So the empty column suggests the shape of a charter without the room having said anything: the words are `--muted`, they are drawn out of the text flow the way every other placeholder on this surface is, `proseText()` never sees them, and the first keystroke takes them away for good. **Where it lands, and the one thing that has to be settled with it**: the column's grey invitation today is `.prose.empty.writeready::before` (`design/session-view.html` `~L118`, *Start writing whenever you like…*) with `.prose.empty.pasteready::before` beside it (`~L123`), both keyed on `visible(card('text'))` at `~L5545–5547` — **and plan 129 (backlog 204) removes that key**, 📄's task being retired. So (c)'s placeholder must be keyed on the column being empty and writable rather than on 📄's turn, and whoever builds it should read plan 129's part 1 first; if 129 lands first there is no `writeready` left to hang it on. The empty document's **one empty clause** `L0` and its own `.doc p.editable.blank` placeholder (`design/system.css` `~L755`) are a **different** placeholder from this one and must not be conflated — that one says the block is empty, this one says what a charter looks like. Copy is owed through `design/STYLE.md` §8, and `card-copy.golden.json` re-freezes with it. **Answered, owed, not built.**

519. **Does a re-send kill the earlier link?** (Raised by Ed's QA of 2026-08-21: *when I click 📨 I'm taken back to link*.) The bounce is fixed — a resend now proves the address is its own with the `pendingId` the first send returned, so it keeps its reservation, its stash and anything pasted into it, and just mails a fresh link. That leaves the founder's inbox holding **two live links to one creation**, which nothing in the design had considered before, and the fix had to say what the older one does. As built: whichever is clicked first creates the document; the older one **logs the founder in** if the document stands at the address it promised, and is **refused as spent** if the address moved between the sends (its creation has already happened somewhere else). Nothing can make a twin. **(a)** As built — every link the founder was ever mailed does something sensible, and none of them founds a second document. **(b)** A resend **invalidates** the previous link outright, the way most magic-link senders behave: one live link at a time, the older one saying it has expired. This is the tighter story and the one a security reviewer would expect, but it costs a schema change — the stash would have to remember the token hash it last minted, in both stores — and it makes the common case (*the first mail was slow, I pressed 📨, then the first one arrived*) into an error page. I recommend (a) and would only move to (b) if the two-live-links window bothers you. **Ed, 2026-08-21: they all stay live — the first one followed creates the document and the rest forward to what was created.** Built: the save no longer deletes the stash, it *claims* it with the document id (the text is folded into the document and the reservation ends, since from then on the document holds the address), so every later link reads the claim and forwards there, logging the founder in. This holds however the address moved between the sends, because the claim is on the **creation** rather than on a name — which is what the earlier design could not do, and why it had been refusing the older link instead. Nothing can found a twin. Postgres schema 3 adds the column.
520. **✋ and 🖼️ clear where every other bin puts back.** (From the card review of 2026-08-21.) Their 🗑️ is the only one whose act is destructive: *Clear the name* / *Remove the picture*, emptying the value, where every other bin restores what the card stood as when you opened it. That was harmless while the bin greyed out unless there was something to throw away; it is not harmless now that the bin is always live **and** is how you leave a card — on a document where your name is set, opening ✋ and pressing 🗑️ to get out of it wipes the field. **(a)** They revert like everything else, and *removing* a picture becomes a choice inside the picker (a *no picture* option) rather than a control on the commit row — one glyph, one meaning, and the destructive act is where the other picture choices are. **(b)** Leave them clearing, and accept that 🗑️ means *put it back* on twenty cards and *delete this* on two. **(c)** Revert on the bin, plus a labelled *Remove* in the card's body. I recommend (a): the commit row is a grammar, and the moment one glyph has two meanings the row stops being readable. **Ed, 2026-08-21: the bin only clears un-actioned inputs on cards; if they have been set already, the bin does not affect the set state.** Built as (a) in effect: ✋ and 🖼️ take the shared bin, which restores what stands, so a saved name and a chosen picture are safe from it. Emptying a name is still done by emptying the field, and a picture is removed by choosing initials — the destructive act stayed where the other picture choices are.

521. **Two words for leaving a card.** (Same review.) Six cards have no bin and carry **Close** instead — 🪪 Membership and 📄 Text (their values live elsewhere, so there is nothing to put back), 🍾 Begin, and the news/gate cards, which carry *Close* beside *OK*. Now that 🗑️ is *put it back as it stands, and close*, the two controls do exactly the same thing wherever there is nothing to put back, and the reader meets two different shapes for one act. **(a)** 🗑️ everywhere; *Close* retired from the surface. **(b)** Keep the documented rule — Close survives where the card holds nothing to put back — and accept the two shapes. **(c)** Close survives only where a card's acts have **already committed**, which is 🪪 alone: invitations commit as you send them, and a 🗑️ there would falsely promise to take them back. Everywhere else the bin. I recommend (c) — it is the reading that keeps 🗑️ honest, and it leaves exactly one card wearing the other word, for a reason a reader can feel. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (a).** *Close* has left the surface: 🪪, 📄, 🍾 and the news cards take the bin, which on them has nothing to put back and is the close it always was. (The applicant surface keeps one ✓ that commits by closing — a commit, not a *Close* button.)

522. **🎩 still commits by a control of its own.** (Same review.) Every setting commits through one path; 🎩 has `data-hatgo`, which is why it once did not close on commit and needed its own fix. Its bin is now the shared one and this is the last bespoke piece. Folding it into the standard commit costs nothing and removes the only card that can drift away from the commit grammar again. (Same family, cosmetic: 📨 is the only commit-row control keyed by `data-act` rather than naming its own act.) I recommend folding both unless you want 🎩 kept separate for a reason I have not seen. **Ed, 2026-08-21: yes.** 🎩 commits through the one commit control, which also gives it the pen-hold every other ✒️ has; 📨 names its own act. The setup-probe step that drove 🎩 by its old attribute was updated with it — a probe that presses a control by name is part of the rename.

523. **Does a clause tab go when its rail entry does?** (From the acknowledgment build of 2026-08-21.) Ed's rule — *you shouldn't see queue-cards for tasks you haven't acknowledged the right to do* — is built in the rail: a 💡/🔥 entry waits for the ⚖️ acknowledgment, while ⏳ and filed entries stay, because hot wants something from you and cold reports. The **gutter** was left alone, so a clause still shows its 💡 tab and still opens a readable, locked card. **(a)** Leave it: the document's own marks are information *about the document*, and the card behind them is readable — only the rail claims to be a list of your tasks. **(b)** Filter the gutter to match, so an unacknowledged reader sees an unmarked charter and the marks arrive with the grant. **(c)** Filter both by dropping the items before they reach the charter at all, which is (b) plus no card. I recommend (a) — but (b) is the literal reading of *don't show me actions I can't do*, and it makes the grant's arrival a visible event. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (b)** — and with 525 (c) beside it that is (c) in effect: no rail entry, no gutter tab, no contents-rail mark and no card. Built by filtering at ingest in session.js's `bindData`, so the three columns agree by construction rather than each restating the rule. The marks now arrive as one batch when the grant is pressed, which makes the acknowledgment a visible event.

524. **Two grant attributions are derived rather than known.** (Same build.) A grant card now says who conferred the power. 💡 and ⚖️ are certain (both open at 🍾, always the founder's own press), and the founder's own ✒️ at the birth is certain. Two are not: **(a)** 🏛️ for an arriving member — the page says *the membership admitted you* when the roster is members-held and *the Founder brought you into the membership* otherwise, but what it wants is how **this** member got in (invited, or admitted on an application), which the page does not track per row. **(b)** ✒️ after a post-start `reserve` motion — the card says *the membership returned the pen to you*, the only other road to a founder's pen, but it is inferred from *not at the birth* rather than read off the motion. Both want a per-member arrival record; worth building, or is the office-level sentence enough? **Ed, 2026-08-21: worth building!** Built: `MemberRecord.arrival` ({via: founding | invitation | application, by: convenor | members | null}) and `SettingState.powerFrom` per power ({founding | motion | null}), both folded from events the log already carried — `member-invited` has carried `viaMotion` from the start — so **no event shape changed and the hash chain is untouched** (the golden log passed unedited). Served on the register rows and the setting rows; the grant card reads them. Residual at 526.

525. **A card locked because you may not judge yet says nothing about why.** (Same build.) Judgment cards go `locked` for a reader who has not acknowledged ⚖️ — radios disabled, no ✓ — which is the state cards.js already draws, and its two existing locked cases each carry a sentence explaining themselves (*settled*, *the ground shifted*). The capability case carries none, so the reader meets dead controls with no reason on the card. **(a)** Give it a sentence of its own, as the other two have. **(b)** Leave it: the ⚖️ task is standing in the rail beside it, green and asking to be pressed, and a card explaining what the rail is already asking for is the design explaining itself. **(c)** Don't render those cards at all for such a reader, which is Q523(c) and makes this moot. Leaning (b), and it depends on 523. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (c)** — *I shouldn't see cards where I don't have the right to do the action.* Built with 523; the locked-card sentence is moot, since the card is not rendered.

526. **A grant is acknowledged once, so a power restored later is never announced.** (From Q524's build.) `grant-pen` opens on `holdsPenAnywhere()` and its OK persists per document and seat, so a founder who relinquishes every pen and is later re-crowned by a carried `reserve` motion gets the pen back **silently** — no task, no acknowledgment, and the card's own *The membership returned the pen to you* sentence (now true, and read off `powerFrom`) never renders. Same shape for 🏛️ if a membership is ever restored. This is the acknowledgment rule's own logic pointing at a hole: *no power arrives without acknowledgment* should not quietly mean *no power arrives twice*. **(a)** A grant re-asks whenever the power arrives from a source it has not been acknowledged for — key the OK by `{grant, source}` rather than by grant alone, so the birth's pen and a motion's pen are two acknowledgments. **(b)** Re-ask whenever the power goes from not-held to held, however it got there. **(c)** Leave it: being re-crowned is a thing you voted on and watched happen, so it needs no receipt. I recommend (a) — it is the smallest change that keeps the rule true, and it is what makes the sentence Q524 built worth having. **Ed, 2026-08-21: (b)** — re-ask whenever the power goes from not-held to held, however it got there. Built as `syncGrantAcks`: an acknowledgment covers *this* holding, so losing the power drops its OK and regaining it asks again. **It watches a transition and never reads an absolute** — before the module hydrates every gate reports shut, so a rule of the form *gate closed, therefore drop the OK* would wipe acknowledgments on every page load and silently hand back powers already accepted; a drop needs the page to have seen the power held and then not held, in that order. Keyed by seat as well as by grant, since the dev dropdown changes who is sitting there and a seat change is not a transition. Verified: the OK survives a reload and a fresh navigation, and a founder→member→founder seat round-trip keeps the pen. **Not verified end to end: the drop itself** — driving a founder to zero pens needs every setting's pen laid down one at a time, and the harness managed 4 of 14 before it became more scaffolding than the check was worth. The rule is six lines and both adjacent hazards are proven; worth a real test when the reserve-motion path is next touched. Known gap, inherited: the module holds no grant-OK state, so a power relinquished in another browser is not seen here — this is a per-device approximation of a per-member fact.

527. **Does *public* leave the catalogue as well as the surface?** (From Ed's instruction of 2026-08-22, *we can remove the option "public" from "Visibility"*.) Built as a surface removal: 🌍 offers *Members only* and *Anyone with the link*, and the rung is gone from the founder's radios, a member's answer ladder and the motion composer — but `packages/constitution/src/catalogue.ts` still holds `['closed', 'link', 'public']`, and every map that *reads* a rung back still knows the word, so a document that took it before today still states its own rule rather than printing a raw value. Two reasons that is the right first move and one reason it may not be the last. It keeps the value domain a module fact rather than a surface fact, so no existing log becomes unreplayable; and narrowing a *constitutional* setting's domain is a change to what rooms may consent to, which is a spec amendment rather than a copy edit. **(a)** Leave the module as it is: the domain is what the mechanism can express, the surface is what we currently offer, and the two are allowed to differ. **(b)** Remove `public` from the catalogue too, with a spec amendment and a version bump, and decide what happens to any document already holding it (there are none in production that I know of — worth checking before, not after). **(c)** Keep it in the module *and* give it a road back to the surface — a listed-and-public document is a plausible thing to want later, and this may be a *not yet* rather than a *no*. I recommend (a) for now and would want to know which of *no* and *not yet* you meant before doing (b). **Ed, 2026-08-22: (c), and the *not yet* reading** — *no point thinking about this until we know whether we even have a public catalogue.* Which is the right test and names the thing the rung actually depends on: *public* means **listed** as well as readable, and nothing lists documents today. So the module keeps it, the surface offers it nowhere, and the question reopens if and when docs.vote grows a directory — at which point *public* is a feature with a home rather than a rung with nothing behind it.

528. **The arrival record is served on everybody's register row, not only your own.** (Flagged in chat when Q524 was built, 2026-08-21, and never ruled on — so it is claimed here.) `MemberRecord.arrival` says how each member got in (founding · invitation · application, and whether by the convenor or the membership), and the register serves it for **every** row. It is not a judgment or an answer, so §3.5's blindness is untouched, and *how the room was assembled* is arguably constitutional fact of exactly the kind the constitution publishes. But it is also, per member, the sentence *this person applied and was voted in* — which somebody may not want standing beside their name for the life of the document, and which nobody consented to publish. **(a)** As built: arrival is public register fact, like the membership itself. **(b)** Your own row only, with the office-level sentence for everybody else — the grant card needs no more than that. **(c)** Public for the founding and invitation routes, private for **application**, which is the only one that says something about a person having asked and been judged. Yours to veto; (c) is where I would land if it were mine, and (a) is what is live. **Ed, 2026-08-22: (a)** — public, as built. How the room was assembled is constitutional fact, like the membership itself; no judgment or answer is exposed, so §3.5 is untouched. No change.

529. **The Proposals preamble and both gate clauses still land together.** (Raised three times during the single-file build, 2026-08-21, unanswered.) The founding runs one task at a time in document order, and `ORDER` puts 💡 and ⚖️ in their document places — but a gate is *settled* only once it has been OK'd, and it cannot be OK'd until the constitution is decided, so both are skipped as blockers and their clauses arrive the moment the founder crosses into the Proposals section: the preamble, 💡 and ⚖️ in one frame, three paragraphs where every other step of the founding has delivered one. (The same rule is what the pen has now been excepted from — see the `blocksOrder` note in session-view.html — because ✒️ *can* be settled the moment it arrives.) **(a)** Leave it: the two gates genuinely do follow from the others, so arriving together is honest. **(b)** Hold both gate clauses until 🍾, where they are actually decided, so the Proposals section fills one clause at a time like every other. **(c)** Let each gate's clause arrive when its own conditions are met rather than when its position is reached, which is the wave graph the single file retired and I would not go back to. I recommend (b). **Ed, 2026-08-22: (a)** — *let's try that.* Built: 💡 and ⚖️ take `hide: () => !constituted()`, so their clauses arrive at the press that opens them rather than when their position in the order is reached. Measured over the whole founding afterwards: **every one of the sixteen steps now delivers exactly one clause**, and the only multi-clause moment left is 🍾 itself — 💡, ⚖️ and 🏛️ together, which is the batch 🍾's own card already states. This supersedes the ⏳-tab-in-the-band waiting state for these two gates; what the founder is owed instead — *what is still stopping judging* — is 🍾's readiness readout, which is where they were going to read it anyway.

626. **A delegated question blocks the start, gate or not.** Raised 2026-08-22 from a live investigation and answered by Ed in the same brief: **(a)** the spec wins. Since spec pass 1 the two halves of §9 disagreed — §9.7.1's note said a delegated question on **any** setting blocks the start, §9.0b said only a judge-gate does — and the code followed §9.0b, so a founder could delegate ⏰/⏱️/🚪/🤝/🤖 and press 🍾 over it. The question then survived the start unresolvable (the consent rule refuses under two voices), unreclaimable (pre-start only) and unsettable (the members' now), while the adapter ran on a fallback nobody chose. A clerk founder who delegated 🤝 to an empty room sealed the document for ever: nobody could be invited, nobody could apply, and four questions could never resolve. Folded: SPEC v0.66 §9.0b, R-045, `waitingOn` in the constitution module, three tests in `begin.test.ts`.

627. **The `setSetting` dependency gate.** Answered by Ed the same day: **leave it**. A founder can still set ✒️ *66% rising from 55%* while ⏰ is delegated and unanswered, and the adapter would run it flat over a perpetual document — but with 626 in place ⏰ can never be unresolved at the start, so the contradiction is unreachable. No deps check on `setSetting`.


629. **The sim-harness founding walk has been broken since Q511 and nothing noticed.** `npm run founding -w @draft/sim-harness` — one of the walks CLAUDE.md names as verification — fails at its first assertion, *constitutional settings default to the members (§9.7)*, which Ed retired on 2026-08-21 with the no-defaults instruction; it then calls `answer` on a setting nobody delegated and aborts. It is not in CI, which is why it went unnoticed for a day. Raised 2026-08-22. **Answered by Ed, 2026-08-26: (a) — *Update the walk to the no-defaults world and put it in CI.*** **Owed, not built.** It is node-only (no browser), so its CI home is the `ci` job beside `npm run spec-check`, not the `probe` job — which is also why this does not contradict 625 above: 625 moves the *browser* golden out of the push, this puts a *headless module* walk into it.

630. **🍾's waiting line joins with ' and '.** With 626 in place the readout can name several waiting questions at once, and three of them read *A and B and C*. Cosmetic; noted while building 626 rather than fixed. Raised 2026-08-22. **Answered by Ed, 2026-08-26: *Add one shared list-joiner and use it at every site that names several settings.*** So it is not a fix to 🍾's line but **one helper**, and every site that names several settings adopts it — 🍾's waiting readout, the readiness holds, the begin batch, and any clause or motion sentence that lists more than one. **Built 2026-08-27 (plan-queue 74); the detail is below.** Two things for whoever lands it: the joiner is member-visible copy, so it belongs to STYLE.md §1's vocabulary and wants a rule there (Oxford comma or not is the decision inside the decision, and nobody has made it); and it moves strings on cards, so if plan-queue **39**'s copy golden has been frozen by then the commit ends with `npm run copy-freeze` and its diff **is** the list of sites it reached.

    **Built 2026-08-27 (plan-queue 74).** The helper is `listOf(items)` in `design/setup.js`, beside `BLINDNOTE`, exported on `window.SETUP` and destructured by the page: it drops empties, gives the item for one, *A and B* for two, and *A, B and C* for three or more. **The comma decision is made, and it is STYLE.md §1's** — commas and a final *and*, **no serial comma**, because that is the register these documents already write in and a rule that said one thing about copy and another about the documents governing it would be two rules. Seven sites in `design/session-view.html`, not the four this item was raised over: `proseLine`'s count line, `oneVoiceAsk`'s three (its own stuck list and the two halves of entry 69's *waiting on … in turn* sentence), and `beginBody`'s three (the hold line, the one-voice line and entry 69's dependency line). Entry 69 landed the same night and wrote three of them; whichever of the two had landed second owed the other its sites, and this is the second. **The guard is `checkListJoiner` in `scripts/spec-check.mjs`**: no `.join(' and ')` survives in any of the four files a member reads from, `listOf` is defined and exported, and the helper's own source is lifted by regex and run in a `vm` against all five cases — which is the **only** place the comma is asserted, since every `card-audit` walk founds a document of one and no walk can reach a list of three. `npm run copy-check` was green, as expected: one item and two come out of the helper byte-identical to the join they replace, so the golden did not move and nothing was frozen. Nothing on these sentences moved but the punctuation between three of them. **Spent.**

631. **"When the session begins" is the only "session" a member can read.** The new Proposals preamble is Ed's own wording (2026-08-22), and I have built it verbatim — but every other member-visible string on the surface says **document**, including the sentence this one replaces (*The document has not begun.* / *The document began at…*), the 🍾 card's own body (*Only the Founder can begin the document*), the hold line (*The document cannot begin while…*) and the stranger's door. A scan of `session-view.html`, `session.js` and `cards.js` finds "session" in comments and file names only, never in copy. So the preamble now says *session* two paragraphs above a card that says *document* about the same act. **(a)** Keep *session* and leave the inconsistency — a convention **is** a session, and this is the one sentence about the room's life rather than about the text. **(b)** Keep *session* and sweep the rest of the surface to match, making it the vocabulary. **(c)** Say *When the document begins, members may propose changes to rules…*, which costs nothing and matches every neighbouring sentence. I recommend **(c)** unless *session* is deliberate; it is a one-word change either way, and STYLE.md's job is one vocabulary. Raised 2026-08-22.



638. **One string the ✍️/👤 rename did not reach.** (**(i) closed 2026-08-25 by Q768**, the other way round from the option offered here: ✍️’s `everybody` went rather than 👤’s `public`, which keeps the ladder `authorshipVisibility` needs and still deletes the contradiction. **(ii) is open.**) (Left by the rename of 2026-08-22, which Ed scoped to the titles and the order.) Both are copy, neither is behaviour. **(i)** 👤 *Are Anonymous Proposers Revealed?* keeps three rungs, and the third — *Names from the start* — does not answer the new title: a proposer named as they propose was never anonymous, so there is nothing to reveal. The rung is also the same fact as ✍️ `everybody` (every proposal signed), which is what makes 👤 `anonymous` + ✍️ `everybody` a reachable contradiction (634 iii). Dropping it would leave never · at-the-close, the shape 👁️ judgments already has, with the catalogue and `authorshipVisibility` in engine-core keeping three exactly as 🌍 kept Public (Q527). **(ii)** `PW_NOUN` still names the two settings *signing* and *proposer naming* in the power clause (*The Founder has a veto over signing*), nouns neither card now uses. **(a)** Fix (ii) only — a two-string change to *anonymous proposals* and *revealing proposers*. **(b)** Fix both, dropping the rung. **(c)** Leave both. I recommend **(a)** now and **(b)** when the anonymity family is next opened: (ii) is a plain leftover, where (i) removes a member-visible option and is a decision rather than a tidy-up. Raised 2026-08-22.


900. **The admit judgment has no way in.** (Found 2026-08-26 in the entry-94/97 documentation sweep.) Entry 94 re-typed 🪪 from the register card into the price of admission; the register's list moved to the Members paragraph and **the judging did not move with it**. `rosterClause` in `design/session-view.html` has no callers, and it is `liveApplicantsHtml`'s only one — so the lanes, the ✏️ *Propose them* button and the admit race are unreachable, while SURFACE F21 and E21 both assert they live on 🪪's clause. It also still reads `value.joinPolicy`, which entry 94 replaced, so it could not be revived where it stands. **Ed, 2026-08-26: (a)** — re-home it. The question offered ✉️ without having read plan-queue entries 95 and 96, which he wrote the same day and which put the admit judgment on an *Applicants* subsection and take every control off 🪪; told that, he ruled for those — *resolve this according to [96], and we will have applications land in applicants*. **Built 2026-08-26** (`a200872`, `85e2fa9`): entry 95's five subsections, then entry 96's doors standing by their results, with the judgment a new `adm:<applicant>` card family taking the form 🪪's price gives it. `rosterClause` and `liveApplicantsHtml` are deleted rather than revived — the second also read `value.joinPolicy`, which entry 94 replaced. **Closed the same day, guard and all.** `admit-view.test.ts` pins the module data on all three rungs, and `npm run applicants-walk` is the browser half — the walk no harness had: it founds a document, knocks as a stranger, and asserts the rail task naming the applicant, the *Applicants* row and the card's form at each price. It fails on the pre-entry-96 page at *the application raised no entry in the rail*. Two of its own bugs are worth keeping: a second page in the same browser context takes the founder's seat (one cookie per document), and the magic link's origin is the server's `baseUrl`, so navigating back to the host the walk was **started** with arrives seatless and reads as the product losing the founder's seat.

901. **Three acts have no communication designed.** (Same sweep.) Entry 94 added exile at will and resignation, and entry 97 added the refusal of an application verified before 🤝 shut — three acts with real audiences and no row in SURFACE's event matrix, which is meant to be complete. **Ed, 2026-08-26: add them, and put designing them on the backlog.** Added as **E31–E33**, each marked *undesigned and unbuilt* so `spec-check` counts them beside E28–E30; the design itself is plan-queue backlog **98**. What each audience is told — and above all whether an exiled member is told anything at all, and by what channel — is the design work this holds open. **Built 2026-08-27 (plan-queue 69):** E31–E33 designed and built — see SURFACE §2 and `design/DECISIONS.md` *Three acts nobody was told about*. Exile mails the member (the document's address, no login link) and their dead seat's door says why; resignation gets the door sentence and no mail; both leave a grey departure line under *Members*; the refused application gets Y25's sentence before and after the press, Submit dark. Two things stay open, unnumbered here: whether the room is owed an OK for a departure (the plan builds the sentence and not the OK), and what a member removed by a *carried* motion is told.

902. **A 💤 change names the members it returned.** (Same sweep, off entry 97.) Turning 💤 off, or lengthening it past somebody's quiet, returns lapsed members at once — a fact about people that today rides E5's change line as though it were only a fact about the rule. **Ed, 2026-08-26: agreed — the change to the lapse setting is the news, and it should include a list of the lapsed members that have now been made active.** Written as SURFACE **Y26**, marked unbuilt.

903. **`roster` means three things in one file.** (Same sweep.) 🪪 is titled *Admissions* and prices admission; its catalogue id is `membership` and its page key is `roster`. 🤝 is titled *Applications*; its page key is `policy`. **Ed, 2026-08-26: (b) — rename.** The measurement the question did not have when it was asked, and which changes the size of the job: in `design/session-view.html` the string `'roster'` carries **three** meanings — the 🪪 card key (~40 sites), the holder sentinel for *delegated to the membership* (27 sites), and `.roster`, the members array (49 sites) — so no sweep of it is mechanical. Beyond the page: 42 references to the catalogue id across four packages, the two current golden logs and `founding.state.json` (re-freeze), `design/tools/founding-walk.golden.json` (re-freeze), and the committed bundle. `founding-v0.jsonl` is frozen deliberately as a pre-versioning log, so the rename needs a legacy fold at load — the module already does exactly this for 🤝's `joinPolicy` (`session.ts`) — rather than a regenerated fixture, which would defeat what that file is for. **Planned, not queued** (Ed, 2026-08-26): `~/.claude/plans/rename-admission-and-applications.md`, which carries the three-meanings inventory, the legacy fold and the order module → page → goldens. It must run **after** entries 95 and 96, which rewrote the region holding most of the ambiguous sites.

    **Built 2026-08-27 (plan-queue 40), and spent.** The catalogue id is `admission` and the page keys are `admission` and `applications`, so `MID` and `PKEY` carry neither pair and SURFACE §4's table has two rows fewer of asymmetry. **The two other senses of `'roster'` are untouched by design** — the delegation sentinel and `S.roster` — and the closing check is the one the plan asked for: every surviving `'roster'` in `design/session-view.html` is one of those two. **Three adjustments the plan did not foresee, all recorded rather than asked.** (i) `founding-v0.jsonl` **is** regenerated, because since Q767 (2026-08-25) `freeze.ts` derives it from today's walk with `schemaVersion` stripped and `golden-log.test.ts` asserts it chains to *today's* rolling hash — so it can only ever prove the versioning fold, never an id fold, and holding its old bytes would fail an assertion the fold cannot reach. What proves the id fold instead is a purpose-built test that re-chains the golden walk under the legacy id and replays it. (ii) `design/setup.js` is in scope after all: its `ANSWER` bodies are keyed by page card key, and a delegated 🪪 or 🤝 would have had no answer body. (iii) The card key reaches five harnesses and the frozen twin — `setup-probe.js`, `journey-walk.mjs`, `slider-walk.mjs`, `pic-measure.mjs`, `spec-check.mjs`'s composable filter, and `design/reference/`, re-frozen so the probe compares like with like. `design/tools/card-copy.golden.json` (plan-queue 39, landed the same morning) re-freezes too: two of its top-level keys are card keys.

910. **The setup-probe has been walking past four cards, and reports green while doing it.** (Found 2026-08-26 while re-freezing the reference under Q900's work; the dead steps themselves are older and were filed as Q504(a).) Its founding scenario has **13 dead steps in one run** — `open-applications`, `choose-join`, `confirm-applications`, `open-hat`, `choose-hat`, `confirm-hat`, `open-name`, `type-name`, `confirm-name`, `open-picture`, `search-emoji`, `choose-emoji`, `confirm-picture` — so 🤝, 🎩, ✋ and 🖼️ have **no probe coverage at all**. A dead step is a warning rather than a failure (`--strict` is off in CI, deliberately), so the run says *46 steps, 0 diffs* and looks like proof. This is the same species as Q900: a hole in a guard that reads as a green guard, and it survived the re-freeze untouched. Two ways. **(a)** Fix the scenario so the four steps land again — the selectors name `data-card="policy"` and `data-set="joinBy"`, which still exist, so the likely cause is the walk arriving at those cards in a state the founding order no longer produces; the honest first move is to find out *why* each one misses rather than to re-point the selectors. **(b)** Turn `--strict` on in CI, which makes the next dead step fail loudly but fails today's 13 first, so it has to follow (a). I would do (a) and then (b), because (b) without (a) is a red build nobody can act on, and (a) without (b) rots again in a fortnight.

    **(a) built 2026-08-26, and the thirteen had one cause between them.** Not four cards in a state the founding no longer produces — one card the scenario had never heard of. Entry 94 moved **🪪 Admissions above 🤝** in `ORDER` the same morning (commit *Entry 94 QA: the founder's 🥾 radios speak the price scale; 🪪 is served before 🤝*), and since **`ORDER` is the dependency list**, an unanswered 🪪 kept 🤝, 🎩, ✋ and 🖼️ from ever being born; every step below it missed as one. Driving the scenario to the stall point and surveying the rail showed it in one reading — the rail held *🪪 Admissions* and *Your Shield*, and `#doc` carried no `policy` tab at all. Three steps (`open-admissions` · `choose-admission` at `data-set="admission"` `assembly` · `confirm-admissions`) restore the entire tail: **49 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed**, no step dead. Nothing else was re-pointed, which is the evidence that nothing else was broken.

    **(b) rests on a premise that was four days stale.** `--strict` **is** on in CI, and has been since 2026-08-21 — `ci.yml`'s `probe` job runs `npm run probe -- --strict`. What made a red probe read as a green run is the job's own **`continue-on-error: true`**, *advisory until it has run green for a week*, set 2026-08-21 and never lifted. It has swallowed a real failure on every push since 2026-08-25: first **136 diffs** (the reference stale after the plan-queue batch), then Q910's thirteen dead steps. `scripts/probe.mjs`'s header asserted the opposite in prose, which is how this question came to be written against it; the header now points at `ci.yml`, which owns the flag. **Still open, and reframed:** promoting the job cannot gate the deploy, because the deploy is a *step* of the parallel `ci` job — so it buys a red X on the commit and nothing more. Gating would mean restructuring the deploy into its own job with `needs: [ci, probe]`. Ed's call, because a non-advisory probe reddens `main` on every intentional page change until the reference is re-frozen.

    **Ed, 2026-08-26: (1) — *let's see if what it picks up is useful*.** `continue-on-error` is off the `probe` job. It buys a red X on the commit and nothing else, the deploy being a step of the parallel `ci` job; lifting the deploy into its own job with `needs: [ci, probe]` stays unbuilt and unasked. **The thing to watch is the false-positive rate**: a deliberate page change reddens the job until `design/reference/` is re-frozen and tagged, so the question this trial answers is whether the reds are findings or bookkeeping. A scenario change needs no re-freeze — only a page change does (Q914). Q910 closes on (a) built and (b) tried.

911. **A walk can run against a server another session left listening, and read as a product failure.** (Met in the wild 2026-08-26; plan-queue backlog **65** is the same hazard, filed from the queue's side.) Two servers were up on the default ports, 8140 and 8199, and the one on 8140 was old enough to still know the **`signing`** setting that spec v0.70 retired — so the first run of the new applicants walk produced a page of confident, entirely fictitious failures (*ending: endsAtMs must be…*, *'membership' is not set this way*, *the document cannot begin while 'signing' is still being decided*). Nothing in the harness noticed it was talking to a stranger. `applicants-walk` takes its base URL as an argument, which is a mitigation and not a fix. Options: **(a)** every walk asserts the server's build before its first step — the version, or the catalogue's own setting ids, against the tree it was started from; a mismatch stops the run rather than reporting nonsense. **(b)** Walks default to an ephemeral port and start their own server, which is what `founding-walk` already does for the design tree. **(c)** Leave it and rely on the argument. I recommend **(a)**: it is cheap, it catches the case (b) does not — a stale *tree*, not just a stale process — and the failure it prevents is the expensive kind, an hour spent debugging the product against a ghost.

    **Ed, 2026-08-26: (a), on all four attaching walks, and the field may be public. Built.** The investigation sharpened the diagnosis, and the sharper version is what the guard is aimed at: **a running server holds two copies of the work at different ages.** `design/` is served from disk, so the page and the browser bundle are *always current* — edit `session-view.html` and the running process serves the edit. The mechanism is loaded at boot (`tsx src/main.ts`, no watch), so the catalogue, the engine and the server logic are frozen at whenever it started. **A stale server therefore serves today's page over a week-old engine**, which is exactly why the failures read as product bugs rather than as an environment problem: the surface was current and the thing behind it was not. It also rules out the cheap fix — nothing fetched off disk can detect this, so only the running process can be asked.

    So `/healthz` gains `catalogue`, the setting ids from the process's own memory, and `scripts/lib/assert-server.mjs` compares them against the tree's before a walk's first step. **Ids rather than a hash** so the failure names what differs: *it has, and your tree does not: signing* ends the hour, where *fingerprint mismatch* does not. The tree's side is read from `design/constitution.js` the way `spec-check` reads it, `bundle-fresh.test.ts` guaranteeing the bundle matches source. Wired into the four walks that attach to a running server — `applicants-walk`, `journey-walk`, `ladder-walk`, `slug-walk`; `founding-walk` and `slider-walk` start their own server on an ephemeral port and are structurally immune. A server predating the field is refused by name rather than passed, since silence is how this happened.

    **Verified against the real thing.** Three stale servers were still listening on this machine while the fix was written — 8140 (3.8 days old), 8199 (4.2 days, the one that cost the hour) and 8211 (old enough that `/healthz` 404s). The guard refuses all three, each with its own message, and passes a server started from the tree. `applicants-walk`, `slug-walk` and `ladder-walk` run green through it.

912. **Two calls inside the admit card's news form, both unmade.** (Raised by the entry-96 build, 2026-08-26.) At 🪪 *pen* an application is news rather than a task (Ed's own ruling the same day), and two details went in undecided. **(a) The OK is session-local.** It is not in `ACK_KEYS`, so unlike every acknowledgement that confers a power it is not persisted per document and seat — reload and the news comes back. W6 says *every acknowledgement that confers a power persists*, and this one confers nothing, so the rule does not reach it; but C8's *a decision you had no say in is owed an OK; reading is not enough* reads oddly against news that returns. **(b) It names them by their email address.** At *pen* opening the link **is** the joining (Q894–Q896), so there is no application to fill in and no name to use — the card says *rowan@example.org Has Joined*. That is honest and slightly cold, and the alternative is to wait for a name that may never come. Both are defensible as they stand; neither has been ruled on.

    **(a) Ed, 2026-08-26: persist the OK. Built.** **C8 governs, not W6** — an acknowledgement that confers no power is still owed, and an OK that comes back after every reload asks the same member to acknowledge the same joiner for ever. `adm:<applicant>` cannot be a literal in `ACK_KEYS`, there being one per joiner, so the page asks **`ackPersists`** — `ACK_KEYS.includes(k) || /^adm:/.test(k)` — at the two sites that persist, and `ACK_KEYS` stays the literal list `spec-check` reads out of the file. The load side needed nothing: `loadGrants()` restores unfiltered. `syncGrantAcks` still iterates `ACK_KEYS` alone and should, being the drop-on-transition rule for gates, which an admitted applicant has no analogue of. **Rule: SURFACE W6a. Guard: `npm run applicants-walk -- --price=pen`**, which presses the OK, reloads, and asserts the news stays gone — it fails on the pre-fix page at *the news came back after a reload*, which is the only reason to trust it.

    **(b) Ed, 2026-08-26: leave it — the address is the name.** No code change; the ruling is recorded in SURFACE §9's `adm:` row. At *pen* opening the link **is** the joining, so the person is a member before they have told anybody who they are and the address is genuinely all that is known — the announcement says so rather than waiting for a name that may never come, or inventing *Anonymous* for a card whose whole job is *somebody new is here*. It is the one place the surface volunteers an address unprompted, and that is not a disclosure question: §9.0c makes the membership public, and 👤 seals authorship rather than who is in the room. **912 closes.**

913. **One guard's failure hides the next one entirely.** (Found 2026-08-26 under Q910.) `ci.yml`'s `probe` job runs two guards as consecutive steps — `npm run probe -- --strict`, then `npm run founding-golden` — so when the probe fails the job stops and **the golden founding walk never runs at all**. That is what happened here: behind the advisory flag the probe had been red since 2026-08-25, and `founding-golden` had therefore not executed in CI for days. It was **52 differences stale** when finally run by hand — 🪪 from step 26 to step 14, the eight steps between it shifted by two, entry 94's rewording of the 🪪 and 🤝 clauses, and Q891–Q893's `/d/` address. Every difference was accounted for by a decision already taken, so the golden was regenerated (55 steps, matching) and this question is only about the arrangement. **(a)** Give each guard its own job, so neither can mask the other. **(b)** Keep one job and add `if: always()` to the later steps, which is one line and keeps the browser cache shared. **(c)** Leave it. I recommend **(b)**: the two guards share an expensive Chromium install, and the only property worth buying is that a red first step cannot silence a second — which `always()` buys exactly. Note this compounds with Q910 (b): the arrangement was harmless while the job gated nothing and somebody read the log, and neither of those was true.

914. **🛡️ Your Shield sits unacknowledged through the whole setup-probe run.** (Found 2026-08-26 under Q910, and deliberately not fixed with it.) The founding scenario OKs 🪶 the pen (`open-the-pen` / `ok-the-pen`) and never touches the shield, which stands in the rail from the save onward — the survey at the 🪪 stall point showed it there, *Your Shield · You hold the shield*, still asking. It is **not** a dead step, because `grant-shield` is a grant and `blocksOrder` lets a grant pass, so nothing below it stalls and `--strict` will never report it. That is precisely what makes it the same species as Q910: a card with no probe coverage, invisible to the guard that exists to notice. Q732 caught 🖼️ in this exact shape after four days of green checks. **(a)** Add `open-the-shield` / `ok-the-shield` beside the pen's pair, three lines, and re-freeze. **(b)** Leave it, on the grounds that the shield's card is a grant like the pen's and the pen already walks that shape.
