I recommend **(a)** — the two grants are not interchangeable (STYLE T6–T9's 🛡️ radios name the setting they veto, and CLAUDE.md already records *the setup-probe never opens a 🛡️ card with live radios* as a known blind spot), and the cost is three lines. Worth asking whether the same audit should be run over every card in `ORDER` rather than one at a time.

    **Ed, 2026-08-26: (a) and the audit. Both built.** The shield's pair sits in its own `ORDER` position, behind the pen and before 🌍 — 51 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed, no step dead. **No re-freeze was needed and this is worth knowing:** the reference is a frozen *page*, and only the shared probe script changed, so both sides drove the new steps against their own unchanged copies. A re-freeze is for a page change, never for a scenario change. The audit is `npm run probe-coverage` (`scripts/probe-coverage.mjs`), and it measures **opening, not naming** — it runs the real probe and records every `data-setupcard` key that reaches the DOM, because Q910 was precisely a selector that named a card still present in the source and never reached. `EXEMPT` carries a reason for any `ORDER` card deliberately not driven.

915. **The setup-probe opens 12 of the founding's 25 cards, and ⏩ is the reason.** (The first run of `npm run probe-coverage`, 2026-08-26, Q914 (9).) Covered: 🪶 📍 📧 ✒️ 🛡️ 🌍 🪪 🤝 🎩 ✋ 🖼️, and 🌡️ from the `motions` scenario. Never opened, and it is exactly the tail of `ORDER` below 🖼️: **💤 🥾 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ ⏱️ 🤖 ⏰ 👥 👤 👁️ 📄 🍾** — thirteen cards whose rendering has never once been compared against the frozen reference. The cause is not drift: the scenario presses **`#devff` (⏩ settle the founding)** after 🖼️, which settles the whole tail by stagehand, and everything below it is therefore reached only as already-answered clauses in the band. Note what does *not* rescue this — `founding-golden` walks all 25 and asserts the founder's **experience** (order, rail, the first words of every clause), where the probe asserts **live-vs-frozen equality** of geometry and markup. A card only the golden walk opens is a card whose rendering nothing compares. **(a)** Drive the thirteen before ⏩: honest, ~26 steps, and it makes the probe's founding scenario the whole founding. **(b)** Open and close each one without answering it, then ⏩ as now — much cheaper, and it buys the rendering comparison, which is the thing the probe is for. **(c)** `EXEMPT` the thirteen with *settled by ⏩; covered by founding-golden*, which is at least honest about the boundary. I recommend **(b)**: the probe exists to catch contamination in how a card *draws*, and opening it is all that requires — answering it is `founding-golden`'s job and is already done there. Until one of these lands, `npm run probe-coverage` exits 1 by design and is **not wired into CI**; wiring it is the same decision as Q910 (b).

    **Ed, 2026-08-26: (a) — drive all thirteen fully. Built; coverage is 25 of 25** and `probe-coverage` exits 0. The founding scenario is now the whole founding: **96 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed**, no step dead, and the `motions` scenario still runs on what it leaves. **No re-freeze was needed** — only the scenario changed, not the page (Q914's rule). Three things the drive turned up. **💡 and ⚖️ arrive *after* 🍾**, not at their `ORDER` position: they take a place in the order but are only served once the constitution is decided, so a loop that tried them in sequence found nothing. **Every card that carries a number refuses its commit until the number is there** — 💤's `lapseDays`, ⏱️'s three (`grant`, `cap`, `dripMin`), 🤖's `auditorBudget`, 🌡️'s `tClose`, 👥's `quorumN` — and 🌡️ additionally refuses a value at or below the coin flip, which is the surface half of Q836's `MIN_CLOSENESS_SPAN`. And **⏰ takes `perpetual` deliberately**: a windowed document puts a live countdown in the topbar, and the two sides of a comparison are measured seconds apart, so `ends` would make the topbar hash differ whenever a tick fell between them — the *Founded at* flake this runner already retries for, made permanent. Values elsewhere are chosen to exercise each card's own fields rather than for what they say.

916. **`npm run journey` is broken, and CLAUDE.md still names it as the guard for five rules.** (Found 2026-08-26 while wiring Q911; **pre-existing**, and confirmed identical at HEAD with the Q911 changes stashed, so it is not that work's doing.) It dies in `inviteDoorPreBegin` waiting for `.setupcard [data-add]` — it opens **🪪 `roster`** and expects the invitation box to be on it. Entry 94 moved that box: 🪪 is the *price of admission* now, and the box belongs to **✉️ `invite`**, the door, which carries its own ✒️/🛡️ pair. The walk's own comment states the stale premise outright — *🪪 is a band tab, never a rail task — it is always settled, having no value to settle* — where 🪪 is today a constitutional setting with four rungs on `data-set="admission"`. **Why this one matters more than a broken script.** CLAUDE.md cites `npm run journey` as the named guard for Q812 (the invite door on the wide gate), Q846 (the two-press acknowledgement), Q645 (✋ and 🖼️ in the rail), Q835 (solo judgment) and the pen-hold fixes — and under the eviction rule a gotcha with a named guard is reduced to one line *because the guard holds it*. So five post-mortems are currently held up by a walk that does not run. **None of the walks are in CI** — the `ci` job runs lint, typecheck, spec-check, tests and build; the `probe` job runs the two design guards — which is why this could rot unseen, and is the same shape as Q910 one level out. **(a)** Repoint the door step at ✉️ and fix the stale comment; smallest thing that restores the guard. **(b)** (a), plus take the walks into CI behind a server the job starts itself — expensive (a real server, a data dir, playwright) but it is the only thing that stops the next one rotting. **(c)** Repoint it and accept that walks are run by hand. I recommend **(a) now, and (b) asked separately**, because (a) is small and five gotchas are waiting on it, while (b) is a real CI design decision that should not ride in on a bug fix. Note Q911's new guard does *not* cover this: it proves the server is your tree, not that the walk still describes the surface.

    **Ed, 2026-08-26: cover both doors. Built; the walk is green.** Three repairs, and **no product defect anywhere in it** — every failure was the harness describing a surface that had moved. **(i) The door step opens ✉️.** **(ii) The row reader was reading nothing at all.** `ROW_READ` queried `.roster.clauselist .rperson`, markup entry 95 deleted outright, so every row assertion in the walk had been matching an empty array. Membership is `.memrow` under one lvl-3 heading per status now, and two things follow that are easy to trip on: **`memSub` renders the card *instead of* its rows** while a card in that subsection's pile is open, so a row can only be read with the card shut; and **status is the heading, not a chip**, so an invitee is one by sitting under *Invitees*. `memRow` prints an address only until a name arrives — as its **local part** — so an invitee is matched by address and a joined member by name, never both. **(iii) Both doors, each on its own pen.** `doorShuts(k, glyph, directSel, label)` lays down `pw:u:<k>` and asserts the door loses its unilateral control — `[data-add]` on ✉️, `[data-exile]` on ❌ — and still says who decides instead. It reads **the whole card, not its `.why`**: once the pen is down the door draws a composer, and a missing `.why` would read as a shut door, which is the exact confusion the check exists to catch. It must run **after 🍾**, because pre-start `directInvite` and `directRemove` are both plain `amFounder()` (§9.6a) — a pen laid down before the start shuts nothing, and the check would pass while asserting nothing. Green twice in a row from a clean data dir.

918. **E9's audience cell, *the actor; every member*, is two readings joined by a semicolon, and the page has no key for it.** *(seat matrix, first run 2026-08-27.)* SURFACE §2 row **E9** *A power is laid down* — Audience cell, verbatim: *the actor; every member*; its Channel cell names the news entry as owed an OK under Q571. The step that triggers it is `lay-down`: `relinquish { setting: 'rate', power: 'unilateral' }` on the founder's pen, live epoch, after the knock and before the close. Seats stood at that step: **founder** (Ada Lovelace — a member founder on the `member` document, a clerk on the `clerk` one), **early** (Bo Marlowe, arrived before the post-start rule), **late** (Dee Latimer, arrived after it), **lapsed** (Cy Quiet, lapsed for real on the one-minute 💤), **stranger** (no seat), **applicant** (Rowan Vale, application submitted). What the harness reports, and why, on both sides: **page side**, the row carries `key: null` because the page files no news entry for a laid-down power and `relinquish` owes no OK — Q571 is unbuilt on both sides, so there is nothing in any rail to assert; **table side**, `AUDIENCE` has no predicate for the cell, because *the actor; every member* is two audiences joined by a semicolon and the harness reads a cell character for character. The reading the builder would take: *the actor* is the founder seat and *every member* is `isMember`, so a founder who is a member is in the audience once, and a clerk founder is in it as the actor only. **(a)** That reading — the union, the actor counted once — written into the cell as one audience (e.g. *every member, and the actor*), so the harness can read it. **(b)** The semicolon is separating **channels**, not audiences: the actor gets the power card's own confirmation, every member gets the news entry — two rows' worth of rule in one cell, which would want splitting so each half has one audience. **(c)** *Every member except the actor*, the founder having no news to read about their own act — the E5 shape. I recommend **(a)**. What settles it: a rule Ed writes into the cell, which is his to edit (entry 139), plus Q571's news entry on the page, which is a build — until then the harness carries the row as `filed: 'Q918'` and is green over it. **The build half landed 2026-08-29** (backlog entry 162, Q1013): `relinquish` post-start and 🍾 owe a release **batch**, and the page files one 👑 news entry per act, keyed `rel:<batch>`, so the *page side* sentence above is the first run's report rather than the state of the code — a re-run now has something in the rail to assert. That build takes **(b)** on the cell and **(c)** on the audience: the actor's channel is the power card's own confirmation, and the news entry goes to every arrived member but the actor. **The cell itself is still Ed's to write**, and reversing the reading is one predicate (`oweReleases`, `session.ts`). — **Written by Ed, 2026-08-29: *rewrite the cell as `every member but the actor`.*** That is option **(c)** on the audience, and it is what entry 162 already built, so no code moves and the drift closes in the direction of the tree. **Folded 2026-08-29 by the batch AD pre-flight**: SURFACE §2's E9 Audience cell now reads *every member but the actor*, one audience the harness can read character for character. The Channel cell is left as it is — the actor still gets the power card's own confirmation, which is (b), and a channel for somebody outside the audience is what a confirmation always is. **What survives, and is why this number stays open**: `scripts/seat-matrix.mjs`'s `AUDIENCE` table needs the predicate for the new cell (*every member but the actor* → `isMember && !isActor`), and the E9 row's `filed: 'Q918'` at `~L416` drops with it, along with the two header paragraphs at `~L51–52` and `~L69` that describe the cell as unreadable. Until that lands the harness is green over a row it is no longer entitled to be green over. **A build, and a small one.** — **Built 2026-09-01** (plan-queue 143): `AUDIENCE` carries the cell verbatim → `isMember(s) && s.name !== ctx.actorOf(ev)`, the `lay-down` row is keyed `rel:` (a prefix match, E34's `mail:` shape) with `filed: 'Q918'` dropped, and both header paragraphs say what is true. Measured on a member-hat run: at `lay-down` the founder — the actor — carries no `rel:` key in rail or band, `early` and `lapsed` carry `rel:rel-1` and `rel:rel-2`, and `late` carries `rel:rel-2` alone, so both branches of the cell are exercised in one snapshot and the row is asserted on every stood seat. Inverting the predicate turns all four seats red, which is the guard's own proof. `filed` is now empty for the whole run. **Nothing is owed here: the question is discharged and can be deleted at the next sweep.**

919. **💤 cannot be rebuilt from the module, and everything below it in the founding order vanishes for every member.** *(seat matrix, first run 2026-08-27 — a page finding the run made, recorded and not fixed; it is the one thing standing between the harness and exit 0.)* `hydrateFromModule`'s 💤 line writes the **day count** into `S.lapse` — the field the card's rung radio reads (`days` | `never`) — and never sets `S.lapseDays`, the number the *They lapse* rung carries; so on any document whose 💤 is a number of days, `CHOSEN.lapse` is false after every reload and every poll, 💤 reads as unsettled, and `orderReady` withholds every card below it in `ORDER` — 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ ⏱️ 🤖 ⏰ 🌡️ 👥 👤 👁️ 📄 — from every member's page and from the founder's own after a reload. The run showed it four ways: the founder reloaded at 🍾 was asked 💤 again (`rail ["lapse"]`); `early` and `lapsed` were owed 💡 and ⚖️ by the module and carried neither (E4, *the module owes it too: a page finding*); `late` was owed ⏰ at the close and carried nothing (E5 `ending`); and after the ladder's reload the founder's rail read `["lapse", …]` with 💤 long since set. The fixture never sees it because the fixture never rehydrates, and `journey` never sees it because its guest asserts nothing below 💤. The harness works round the founder's half only — 💤 answered on the card after the reload, then the minute over the wire — and the members' half cannot be worked round. A second, smaller half: `S.seen` and `nameSet`/`picSet` are rebuilt only by `hydrateS` at boot, so a page open through a founder's later act never learns the setting is settled until it reloads. **(a)** Fix the hydration line: the rung into `S.lapse` (`days`/`never`) and the count into `S.lapseDays` — two lines, and `journey` gains an assertion below 💤 on the guest. **(b)** (a), plus rebuild `S.seen` from the module on every poll, the way `okd` already is (`syncOwedOks`), so an open page learns of a settling it did not perform. **(c)** Leave it and let the seat matrix stay red on it. I recommend **(a) now and (b) as its own decision**, since (b) changes what a poll may do to the page the reader is looking at. Until (a) lands the seat matrix exits 1 with fifteen findings, every one of them this. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): (b)** — *Also rebuild the page's record of what has been settled on every poll.* So both halves, and the second is the one that was held back for its own decision: the hydration line splits (the rung into `S.lapse`, the day count into `S.lapseDays`), **and** `S.seen` is rebuilt from the module on every poll the way `okd` already is (`syncOwedOks`), so a page open through a founder's later act learns of a settling it did not perform. Ed has ruled on what (b) costs — a poll may now change what the reader is looking at — so the build does not re-ask it. **Answered, owed, not built**, and it stays here until a plan lands it; `journey` gains an assertion below 💤 on the guest, and the seat matrix stops exiting 1 on this.

920. **A clerk founder is served the voice they do not hold, and 🍾 waits on it for ever.** *(seat matrix, first run 2026-08-27, clerk hat — a fold finding, recorded and not fixed.)* `set-convenor-membership { isMember: false }` moves the founder off the roster and records the act (`membershipSet: true`), but the fold never touches `convenor.isMember` — the struct field set once at creation (`session.ts` L112, the `created` fold) — and `convenorRecord()` hands that struct back verbatim, so `view.convenor.isMember` stays `true` on a clerk's document and the page's `viewerIsMember()` reads the clerk as a member. On the clerk document the founder's rail carried `grant-voice` as news before 🍾 (`no begin card to hold · readiness {"ready":true,…} · rail ["grant-voice"]`), `beginOffered` held 🍾 shut behind the voice's OK — which the harness rightly refuses to give for a clerk — and the document never began, so every later row on that hat is the same finding cascading (E4, E5, E21, E24, the lapse wait, both ladder rungs). No walk founds as a clerk (`journey`, `ladder`, `founding-walk` all found a member), which is how it stayed invisible; 🎩's own case in `founding.test.ts` reads the roster, not the view. **(a)** `convenorRecord()` (or the view builder) reports `isMember` from the roster — `members.has(convenor.id)` — and the struct field is retired or kept in step by the fold; one case on the clerk's view. **(b)** Keep the field and update it in the `convenor-membership-set` fold. I recommend **(a)**: the roster is the fact (entry 94), and a second copy of it is what went stale. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): (a)** — *Report the fact from the membership list and retire the stale copy.* `convenorRecord()` (or the view builder) reports `isMember` as `members.has(convenor.id)`, and the struct field set once in the `created` fold is retired rather than kept in step, because keeping it in step is the second copy the ruling refuses. One case on the clerk's view, and the clerk hat of `seat-matrix` is what proves it. **Answered, owed, not built.**

930. **E11's audience cell, *whoever the router serves*, is not an audience the harness can read — and what the page actually does is something else.** *(seat matrix, the 🥾 promise-coverage row, 2026-08-27, plan-queue 44 / backlog entry 80.)* SURFACE §2 row **E11** *An ordinary motion is put — an invitation or removal too, where 🪪 / 🥾 stands at* proposal — Audience cell, verbatim: *whoever the router serves*; Keys cell `invite remove`. The step that triggers it is `remove-motion`: `open-motion { kind: 'remove', member: <late> }` put by the **early** seat, live epoch, after the knock, with 🥾 standing at `proposal` in the harness's `SETTINGS`. Seats stood at that step on the member document: **founder** (Ada Lovelace), **early** (Bo Marlowe, the mover), **late** (Dee Latimer, **the subject**), **lapsed** (Cy Quiet, lapsed for real), **stranger**, **applicant** (Rowan Vale). `AUDIENCE` has no predicate for the cell and cannot be given one: a predicate is `(seat, step, ctx, ev)` and never sees a snapshot, so the router's choice — which lives in each seat's own `view().raceCards` — is out of its reach by construction. **What the run showed**: the ❌ card stood in the rail of *every* member seat — `early`, `late` and `lapsed` all carried `remove` — because `liveMotionRec` puts a motion's own card in the rail whatever the route, and the router only orders the pairs *inside* the card. So the audience of the **task** is not the router's at all. **(a)** The cell states the audience of the task and the router is pacing inside it: rewrite it as *every member* (the E10 cell), and the predicate is `isMember`, which the harness already has. **(b)** (a), but narrower — *every active member*, since a lapsed member is out of E and may not judge, which would make the page's serving the card to `lapsed` a finding rather than the rule; note *every active member* is E10's own cell and has no predicate either. **(c)** The cell is right and the harness gains an oracle in the shape of `ORACLE` — a per-seat read of `view().raceCards` — so *served* is asserted rather than inferred; that is a harness build, not a cell edit. I recommend **(a)**, with (b) filed beside it as the lapsed question: the task standing in a rail is a fact about who may judge, and *served* is about order, not access. Until the cell is edited — which is Ed's (entry 139) — the row is reported as *no rule* and the run exits 3 over it; it cannot be carried as `filed: 'Q930'`, because the harness only honours that mark on a row with **no key**, and this row has one (`remove`). — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): (b)** — *Rewrite it as 'every active member', excluding anyone whose membership has gone quiet.* **The cell is folded**: SURFACE §2's E11 Audience cell now reads *every active member — not anyone whose membership has gone quiet (Q930, Ed 2026-08-29)*, which is E10's own cell. Two consequences the ruling carries with it, both for whoever next touches the harness: the page serving the ❌ card to `lapsed` is now a **finding** rather than the rule, since (b) is the narrower reading and a lapsed member is out of E and may not judge; and `AUDIENCE` still has no predicate for *every active member* — **E10 carries the same cell and has none either** — so `seat-matrix` exits 3 on both rows until one is written. **The cell edit is done; the predicate is owed, not built.**

982. **`npm run journey -- --delegate-all` has handed over nothing since Q967, and passes while testing nothing.** *(found by plan-queue 94's build, 2026-08-28, trying to run the mood as the guard for its own `nothingElseServed` question.)* The walk finds the delegate rung by its words — `isDel = (x) => /delegate/i.test(x.textContent)` (`scripts/journey-walk.mjs:266`), and `handedOver.push` is gated on `/delegate/i.test(chose)` at `:983`. Q967 relabelled the rung *Let the membership decide this*, which contains no *delegate*, so `wanted` is empty, `options()` falls back to the ordinary rungs, and the run answers every question the founder way: `delegated · handed over 0, asked back 0`. It then reports `ends at 🍾 · FAIL` — the founding correctly ends at a pressable 🍾, because nothing was ever delegated — so the mood is **red for the wrong reason**, which is worse than red. This is the guard `CLAUDE.md` names for Q826–Q830 and the only walk that reaches the `one-voice` dead end F18 and F19 exist for; it has been blind since Q967 landed. **(a)** The walk keys on the rung's **value**, `data-val === 'roster'`, not its text — which is Q620's own lesson, *filter the standing rule by value, never by label text*, applied to the harness that tests it. **(b)** Keep the text match and update the pattern to the new label, which breaks again at the next rename. **(c)** `spec-check` asserts that whatever `delegateRung` writes is what the walks look for, so a rename cannot silently blind a walk again. I recommend **(a)**, with **(c)** filed beside it: a walk that goes quiet on a copy change is the same failure as a card audit that never opens a card. Not fixed by 94's build, whose plan edits one assertion in this file and no other.

924. **Gate 1 merges at the paragraph, so two typo fixes in one paragraph become a rivalry** (raised 2026-08-27, from Ed's question *does git teach us anything about overlapping proposals?*; **Ed: good idea**). `blocksOf` makes one engine line one paragraph, so every footprint is a whole paragraph and two members editing different sentences of the same one fail the three-way merge (`compose.ts`) and escalate to a race the room must judge — the opposite of *most of a session should feel like approving typo fixes*. Git's lesson is that the granularity of conflict detection sets the collision rate, and that most conflicts are adjacent edits that a finer diff composes. Proposed: **Gate 1 composes at word or sentence level inside a paragraph** while the footprint stays paragraph-keyed for anchoring, so the page's *one block, one hunk* simplicity survives and only the merge gets finer. **Before deciding, the sim measures it**: rivalry rate at paragraph vs sentence granularity over the existing sweeps, since the whole case is that most paragraph collisions are accidental. SPEC §2.2 rule 1 is the sentence that changes. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): make the change now — merge at sentence level inside a paragraph.** So the measurement is not a precondition of the decision; it may still be worth running as evidence of how much the change bought, but nothing waits on it. Gate 1 composes at **sentence** granularity (not word — Ed named the sentence) inside a paragraph, while the footprint stays paragraph-keyed for anchoring, so `blocksOf`'s *one block, one hunk* survives and only `compose.ts` gets finer. **SPEC §2.2 rule 1 changes with the build, not ahead of it** — a spec sentence describing a granularity the engine does not have is the drift the spec pass exists to stop, and this is one commit's worth of both. **Answered, owed, not built.**

1015. **What is a member who is not the Founder told while a text adoption sits awaiting assent?** *(raised by the R-056 build, 2026-08-29, backlog entry 159.)* Under 🛡️ on the Text a race that clears the bar now **parks**: the version does not move, no rival is rebased, and the candidate leaves every feed. What the room sees is the race disappearing from the rail and the text not changing — and nothing anywhere says why, or that anybody has been asked. The build adds nothing deliberately: the surface says nothing about it today, and inventing copy for it would be inventing a rule. The readings: **(a)** nothing — the room is told outcomes, and this is not one yet (SPEC §3.5, and the *no cleared-and-cooling state* rule in §4.2 is the same argument: saying *this has won and is waiting* is a standings reveal); **(b)** a mark on the clause and nothing more — *this clause is waiting on the Founder*, no wording, no author, no confidence; **(c)** the whole thing on the record when it resolves, and silence until then, which is what happens today by construction. I recommend **(a)** or **(c)** — they are the same behaviour, differing only in whether the silence is a rule or an accident, and (b) is the one that needs a ruling because it leaks *something cleared* in real time. What settles it: Ed. The founder's own card is built (SURFACE §9's *👑 question (the Text)* row); this is the other side of it. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): (b), and he wrote the card** — *It should appear as a ⏳ whose card says something like "Awaiting assent from the Founder 🛡️" [OK].* So the room **is** told, and told in the alphabet it already reads: the parked clause carries a ⏳ mark, its card says who is being waited on and asks nothing but to have been seen. Ed has overruled the standings-reveal objection (a) and (c) rested on — *this has won and is waiting* is news the room may have — so the build states it and does not re-argue it. **Owed, not built**, and it needs a SURFACE §2 event row of its own before it is: the audience, the channel, the close and the persistence of that ⏳ are exactly the columns nobody has filled in, and Ed's sentence gives the Ask and the Close (*[OK]*) but not the other three.

1016. **The seat-matrix row for a shielded Text is deferred to entry 158.** *(raised by the R-056 build, 2026-08-29.)* `npm run seat-matrix` needs a running server and a whole membership unanimously carrying a `reserve` motion on the Text to reach the parked state at all — a walk of its own. Entry 158's Begin-card power switches make the state reachable in one press, so the row belongs with that build. Until then the founder-facing text 👑 card has no end-to-end walk; its guard is `packages/constitution/test/text-powers.test.ts`'s *🛡️ on the Text parks the adoption (R-056)* block for the mechanism, and a DOM measurement of the card for the surface. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): fold it into whatever next touches the harness, rather than as its own job.** So the row is not a build of its own and no plan is owed for it; it is a **rider** on the next piece of work that opens `scripts/seat-matrix.mjs`, which is why this stays in the register rather than being spent — it is a standing instruction with nothing yet to attach to. Two other riders are now waiting on the same file and should ride with it: **Q930**'s missing `AUDIENCE` predicate for *every active member* (E10 and E11 both), and **Q919**'s fifteen findings, which are one bug.

1017. Released, unspent (claimed with 1015–1016 for backlog entry 159).

1021. **Answered by Ed, 2026-08-29 (decision D47): (b) — a news card beside the amended clause.** *(raised by the R-058 build, 2026-08-29, backlog entry 160.)* The question was where a member acknowledges a text amendment they had no say in, and the R-058 build took **(a)**, 📄's own key `startingText` through `oweOks` — cheap and true, collapsing a run of amendments into one owed OK reading *the Founder changed the text*. Ed's ruling reverses it to the richer reading it named and scoped out: **a news card beside the amended clause**, in the charter rail against the paragraph that moved, one card and one OK **per amendment**, so two amendments at two clauses are two cards. Built 2026-08-29 as the `amendment-owed` / `amendment-ok` pair and `oweAmendment` / `ackAmendment` — a new owed kind on entry 162's model, without entry 162's batching — with 📄's owed OK **removed**, since the card is a second home for the one fact the moment it exists (STYLE **T36**). SPEC §9.7 rule 8 and SURFACE **E35** state it; `R-058` records the reversal. See **1034**.

1022. Released, unspent (claimed with 1020–1021 for backlog entry 160).

1031. Released, unspent (claimed with 1030 for backlog entry 173).

1033. **What becomes of a *motion-backed* 👑 question standing when the convenor's seat is vacated?** *(raised by the vacancy auto-pass build, 2026-08-29, plan 115.)* Ed ruled that a parked **text** adoption whose convenor has left auto-passes, the way a lapse does, and that is what is built: `crownSeatVacated` loops the pending questions and auto-passes the ones carrying `text`. The lapse loop it copies does **more** — it auto-passes motion-backed questions too, and calls `settleCarriedEffects` for each — and the build deliberately narrows away from that, because a carried invitation or removal **applying** without assent because the convenor left is a governance consequence nobody has ruled on. The asymmetry that decides it: a parked text adoption blocks every text adoption in the document for the rest of its life, and a standing motion-backed question blocks nothing at all. The readings: **(a)** auto-pass them too, matching the lapse loop exactly, on the ground that a seat nobody occupies cannot refuse anything and a motion the room carried should land; **(b)** leave them standing, and let the close resolve them the way §4.6 resolves a text question — the motion fails closed, having never been assented to. I recommend **(b)**, weakly: (a) applies acts nobody assented to, and its only argument is symmetry with a loop whose other half is about a crown that may still wake up. What settles it: Ed. Neither is implemented; the questions simply stand today. The wider version of the same question — every other crown power at a vacant seat, `crowned()`, `holdsAnythingReserved()`, `reservedTarget()` and the doors' pairs all still reading the seat as held — is gestured at here and is not claimed as a number of its own. The spend it was held for — whether 📨 widens past the founder where 🪪 stands at ✒️ and any member may invite — is not raised: E34 says *the founder's ✉️ row* and the build simply obeys it, `founderOnly` on `resend-invite`. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): (a)** — *Auto-pass them too, exactly as the lapse case does.* Against the recommendation, and the reason to record is Ed's own ground for (a) rather than mine for (b): a seat nobody occupies cannot refuse anything, and a motion the room carried should land. So `crownSeatVacated` stops narrowing away from the lapse loop and becomes it — every pending question auto-passes, motion-backed ones included, with `settleCarriedEffects` called for each, so a carried invitation or removal applies. **The consequence to build carefully**: this is the point at which a governance act lands with nobody having assented to it, so the record must say the seat was vacant, not that the convenor agreed. **Answered, owed, not built.** The wider version — every other crown power at a vacant seat, `crowned()`, `holdsAnythingReserved()`, `reservedTarget()` and the doors' pairs all still reading the seat as held — is still not claimed as a number of its own and is still not answered by this.

## Backlog (provisioned, build later)

1009. **SURFACE.md is over its size budget and only grows** (Ed, 2026-08-28, answering the batch-Q run report; **SURFACE first**, before 1010). 77,151 bytes against a 70,000 budget, and it is the append-only surface inventory: every batch adds rows and nothing takes any out unless somebody decides to. What the refactor is *for* is the question to answer first — the file is read by `spec-check` table by table and by a session looking one rule up, and neither wants the whole of it. Candidates the measurements already point at: §8.1's twenty-two prose rules (**1006**, its own pass and its own plan), the exceptions paragraphs that restate their tables, and the event matrix's longest cells (**1005** rules that a cell stays self-contained, so those cannot be shortened by a pointer). Precedence: the extraction proposal at `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md` is written and unperformed, and **1004** and **1005** are its two answered items — read it before writing anything new. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): perform the written extraction and raise the limit to a number it can meet.** Both halves matter: the extraction proposal at `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md` is **performed** rather than re-argued, and the budget moves to whatever the performed extraction lands on rather than the extraction being cut to reach a number — which is **1051**'s rule (*the byte target is a consequence, never a criterion*) applied to this file by name. **This needs reading against 1050 before a plan is written**, and a session that finds them incompatible should stop and ask rather than pick: 1050 redefined the SURFACE work owed as the **§8.1 tabulation** (**1006**) rather than a whole-file cut, and said the byte target *is not itself the object*; this answer commissions the written extraction as well. The reading that holds both is that the tabulation and the extraction are two pieces of one job and the limit follows them, but nobody has said so. Standing facts for whoever takes it: SURFACE.md is **82,303 bytes** against a 70,000 budget (it was 77,151 when this was raised, and has grown by every batch since), **143 stands** — refactor, not cull, every row and cell of every table stays — and the limit itself lives in plan-queue's watch, not in this repository, so *raising it* is an edit outside this tree. **Answered, owed, not built**; plan 118 is blocked and 1039–1042 stay parked with it. — **The tension above is resolved by Ed, 2026-08-29: *do both, in order: perform the written extraction first, then §8.1 as a table as its own plan.*** So the reading that holds both answers is the one this item guessed at and would not take on its own: the written extraction at `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md` is **performed first**, as its own piece of work, and **1006**'s §8.1 tabulation follows it as a **second plan** — two pieces, in that order, and neither folded into the other. The extraction proposal is still in the tree and still a proposal (its own opening line says nothing in `SURFACE.md` changed in the build that wrote it); the pass plan 118 ran was **entry 191 / 1036**, a different one, and it is not this. **1050** stands unamended: the byte target is not the object, and **1051**'s rule governs the limit — it moves to whatever the two pieces land on. Standing fact at `f9a5c66`: SURFACE.md is **83,309 bytes**, having grown by a further 1,006 since this was raised. **Answered, owed, not built**, now as two plans rather than one. — **The extraction half was already built when the sentence above was written** (plan 139, 2026-09-01, which checked all 26 change-items of `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md` against the tree one at a time, by the sentence each quotes, and found every one applied — six retires gone, fourteen reduces ending in their proposed pointers, the one move verbatim in `design/DECISIONS.md`, five points reduced to cross-references, none overtaken). The pass that did it is commit **`18710a9`** — *SURFACE.md: the extraction pass applied, and Ed's quotations moved (entry 191, Q1036)* — which is entry 191 / **1036** itself, so this item is wrong where it calls that pass *a different one*: plan 118 applied the extraction and **then** blocked on the budget question, and the register recorded the block rather than the landing. Plan 139 therefore folded nothing into `SURFACE.md`; it marked the proposal performed and left the file alone. The real arithmetic: **87,617 B** before that commit, **82,301 B** after (**−5,316**, −6.1%), and **95,917 B** today against the 70,000 watch — the 13,616 B since being new rules from batches Z, AA, AC and AD, which is what an append-only inventory does between passes. **The remaining half is 1006's §8.1 tabulation**, and the limit still moves to whatever that lands on (**1051**). **Half built, half owed.**

1003. **The governance rules `SPEC.md` already states leave `CLAUDE.md`** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the CLAUDE.md audit's C7 — *move them — pointers only, SPEC is the source*). Seven items — 23, 26, 28, 29, 34, 202, 211, some 4,915 bytes — restate rules `SPEC.md` or `SURFACE.md` already state in the same words, against the file's own admission rule. Each leaves a residual pointer naming its section, and `spec-check` must still resolve every one. Two constraints on when: it is a large edit to the one file every session reads whole, so it must not land while a batch's plans are editing `CLAUDE.md` for their own reasons; and it must not take **1001**'s card-lifecycle lines or **1002**'s *One card per decision*, both of which Ed kept in the same sitting. The known cost, and the argument that was put against it: SPEC.md is not loaded per session, so a session that needs the two kinds of decision has to open it, and one that does not know it needs to will not.

1006. **A SURFACE §8.1 tabulation pass** (Ed, 2026-08-28, on the extraction pass's own reading — *yes, file a backlog entry*). §8.1 is 13,548 bytes of unbroken prose in twenty-two rules F1–F22, three of them past 1,100 bytes each. §9's card kinds were prose once and are a table now; a pass that tabulated F1–F22 the same way would do more for the file's size and readability than every item in the extraction proposal put together. It needs its own plan, its own line-by-line approvals and its own question number when it is written, because F1–F22 are cited by number from `SURFACE.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and the plans, and a tabulation that renumbers anything breaks every citation. Feeds **1009**. **Confirmed by Ed, 2026-08-29**, put to him again while batch X's plans were being written: *yes — file it. The tabulation gets its own plan, its own line-by-line approvals and its own question number when it is written, and it must not renumber F1–F22.* In the same sitting he ruled that **entry 191 becomes this pass** (see **1050**), so the SURFACE work owed is no longer a whole-file cut but the §8.1 tabulation; the item stays here because it is owed a plan, not because anything about it is still undecided.

996. **A per-decision reveal rung for 👁️** (Ed, 2026-08-28 — *file a backlog entry for the per-decision rung*). The 👁️ judgments ladder has two rungs, *never revealed* and *revealed at the end of the document* (§3.5a, **994**). Ed's note asked for a third: names shown once the decision they contributed to closes, decision by decision, rather than at the end of everything. It is a SPEC §3.5a change with record semantics inside it — what the sealed record shows, and when — and the batch-O plan deliberately did not take it. What has to be settled before it can be built: whether a per-decision reveal names judges on the sealed record only or in the live view of a settled race; what it does to a race that resolves and is later re-opened by a ground shift; and whether the rung is elective per member or the room's alone, since every other rung on the ladder is the room's. — **Answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): build it, and show the names in the live document too as each decision settles.** That answers the first of the three open sub-questions outright — **not the sealed record only**: a settled race shows its judges in the live view, as it settles, not merely in the record at the close. The other two are still open and a plan must settle them before it builds: what a **re-opened** race does with names already shown (a ground shift re-opens a settled race, and a reveal cannot be taken back), and whether the rung is **elective per member or the room's alone** (every other rung on the ladder is the room's, and per-member election would make one document's names half-shown). SPEC §3.5a is the section, and the ladder gains a third rung between *never revealed* and *revealed at the end of the document*. **Answered, owed, not built** — and note it is the reveal-cannot-be-taken-back half that makes this dearer than a rung label. — **The other two are answered 2026-08-29 (Ed): *a re-opened race hides its names again until it re-settles; the rung stays the room's.*** So a reveal **is** taken back, and the rule is stated positively: names show while a race stands **settled** and are withdrawn the moment a ground shift re-opens it, returning when it settles again — the reveal is a property of the settled state rather than an event that fires once. And the rung is **the room's alone**, not elective per member, which keeps every rung on the 👁️ ladder the same kind of thing and rules out a document whose names are half-shown. **All three sub-questions are now answered and nothing is left to decide** — what remains is entirely a build, and it is one plan's: SPEC §3.5a's judgments sentence gains the third rung, **and §9.7.1's `judgments` row must move with it** (its ladder cell reads `never · after` today and `spec-check` asserts that table against `packages/constitution/src/catalogue.ts`, so the spec sentence, the table row and the catalogue values are one commit or a red build). The pre-flight of 2026-08-29 deliberately did **not** fold the rung into SPEC for that reason. **Answered, owed, not built.**

1012. **A removed invitee who was never here** (Ed, 2026-08-28, on the batch-O review's one left-alone finding — *keep the mail and also record the departure*). The `member-removed` mail arm in `packages/server/src/server.ts` has no arrival check, so somebody who never turned up and is then removed via `remove` is told *You are no longer a member of …*; the session's own departure fold deliberately excludes people who never arrived, so the register says nothing about the same act. Ed keeps the mail and fixes the other half: **the departure fold records a removal whether or not the person ever arrived.** Unreachable from the page today — invitees are routed to `uninvite` — so nothing a member sees is wrong yet, and the mail's own guard (the `uninvite` precedent) is deliberately **not** added.

530. **Even a ✒️ decision should offer a rationale, and a change should announce itself.** (Ed, 2026-08-22, backlogged as he raised it.) Two halves of one idea. **(a)** The pen commits with no rationale lane, where every motion carries one — so the one route that changes a rule *without asking anybody* is also the only one that leaves no reason behind, which is exactly backwards: a change nobody got to argue about is the one most worth explaining. **(b)** A change should generate an acknowledgement — *The Founder has changed the visibility from x to y* — presumably the news card the surface already has, pinned until OK, which is the same object an unacknowledged constitutional decision uses. **The exception is the founder deciding something for the first time**, which is not a change at all: at the founding a setting goes from unanswered to answered, there is no *from x* to state, and a room being told that the founder has decided fifteen things they were never asked about is noise rather than news. So the trigger is *a settled value being re-set*, not *a value being set* — which is a distinction §9.6a already draws, since before the start nothing is amended, only set. Open inside it: whether the rationale is required or offered (offered, on the surface's own rule that a blank rationale is a real proposal); whether the acknowledgement goes to every member or only to those who had a say; and whether it stands in the rail as news or only in the record.

    **Investigated 2026-08-22 (Ed: *investigate this change*).** Findings, in the order they change the shape of the work.

    **Half of (b) is already built, and nobody noticed.** `setSetting` ends with `if (CONSTITUTIONAL.has(setting)) this.oweOks(t, setting)`, and the fold deletes every member's `okGiven` for that setting — so a founder changing a **constitutional** setting already owes every other member an acknowledgement, *and an OK given before the change is dropped, so it is owed again*. That is the news card pinned until OK, the object this asks for. What is genuinely missing is narrower than the note implies: **ordinary settings owe nothing at all** (⏱️ the rate, 🤖 machines, 📈 the ramp), so a founder re-setting the proposal rate tells nobody; and **the card states the rule, not the change** — `headFor` serves the clause's own sentence, so it reads *Only members can see the document* where Ed wants *The Founder has changed the visibility from x to y*.

    **The "from x" costs no event change.** Nothing anywhere projects a previous value, but every `setting-set` is already in the log, so the previous value folds into `SettingState` exactly as `arrival` and `powerFrom` folded in Q524 — a projection over events that already exist, hash chain untouched, golden log unedited.

    **And the exception is already implemented, by a different rule.** The page withholds *all* news until the document begins (`news()`'s `!constituted() ? false`), on §9.6a's ground that the founder re-sets freely until the start — which covers "the founder deciding for the first time" for everything decided during the founding. It does **not** cover a setting first decided *after* the start (delegated, never resolved, reclaimed, set), where a true first-time test wants `previousValue === null`. So (b) and the exception are the same piece of work: the fold that gives you *from x* is also what tells a first decision from a change.

    **(a) the rationale needs one optional field, and it is hash-safe.** `setting-set` carries `{type, t, setting, value, by}` and no reason. `stableStringify` **drops undefined keys**, so adding `why?: string` leaves an event without a rationale serialising exactly as it does today: every existing log replays bit-identically. The precedent is in the same file — `motion-opened` has carried `why?: string` since it existed.

    **One real design question inside (a), and it is not a small one.** The rationale lane the surface already has (`whyLane`) draws the `sealed-speaker` — the featureless disc — because authorship is sealed until the record (§3.4). A ✒️ act is attributed **by construction**: only the founder can perform it, and the acknowledgement names them. So a founder's rationale must not wear the sealed disc; it is the one voice on this surface speaking in its own name. Either a named speaker treatment (the founder's own avatar, which exists) or no speaker at all — but the sealed disc would be a lie the first time it was drawn.

    Sizing, then: the acknowledgement half is one fold field, one view field and copy, and it brings the first-time exception with it. The rationale half is one optional event field, one lane, and a decision about whose face is on it.

    **Built 2026-08-22** (Ed: *let's do 530; the rationale should have the founder's name and avatar on it, and 👑 so people know that it was a founder action, even if they have no name or avatar*). Module: `setting-set` gains an **optional** `why?`, hash-safe because `stableStringify` drops undefined keys; `SettingState.previousValue` and `setWhy` fold from events the log already carried, so **the golden log passed unedited**. The acknowledgement widens by one clause — constitutional settings owe an OK on any set as before, ordinary ones only when the value **changed** — which is Ed's exception stated in the module rather than in copy. Server: `set-setting` takes `why`, capped like every other rationale. Page: `WORDS` (a typed value in words, its own map because `RESULT` reads the current value out of `S` and `MVAL` only knows what a motion may offer), `changeHalf` (the founder's lane, or the change note and the reason), `founderSpeaker` / `founderSpeakerLane` (avatar, name, 👑 — never the sealed disc, since a ✒️ act is attributed by construction), and `syncOwedOks`, which makes the module the truth about what is owed on the fixture path as `hydrateS` now does on the live one — it had only ever *added*, so an acknowledgement could never be taken back and a change could not announce itself. 13 new module tests; verified end to end on the fixture: a first decision shows neither half, a change shows the lane to the founder and the note to a member, and the member's OK clears it. **Still open inside it**, and unbuilt: whether the acknowledgement reaches every member or only those who had a say (it reaches every member), and whether the reason belongs in the record as well as in the rail.

    **The two residuals closed 2026-08-22, and the second reframed the first half of the build.** Ed: **(a)** *owe the lapsed too* — E excludes them and for deciding that is right, but an acknowledgement is not a decision; it is owed to somebody about a document they are still a member of, and the person who was under the old rule and went quiet is exactly the one a change ought to find. Removed members and never-arrived members stay excluded, each for its own reason. **(b)** *both clause and record* — the reason stops vanishing at the OK: the clause keeps a dated **Last amended** block permanently, and the closed record grows an **Amendments** section. Then, mid-build: ***a unilateral rule change by the founder is still just a kind of amendment and so should be treated in the same way in terms of how it's communicated and reported*** — which retired the bespoke change list this had been growing. A pen change now **joins the motions**: `MotionRoute` gains `'pen'` (the four verbs' ladder of who had to agree), `RaisedRoute` narrows anything that raises one, and the record is **folded from the `setting-set` event** rather than emitted, so the hash chain is untouched and the golden log unedited. The dividend was immediate and is the point of his framing: the clause's history line now serves **both routes** with one shape, naming the founder where the pen is attributed by construction and the membership where the mover is sealed (§3.4).

    **Answered and built 2026-08-22.** Ed chose **the lean** (of glow · ring · spin · lean), **🪶 ✒️ ✏️ only** (🍾 and 🏛️ deferred), and **his two mechanisms alone** — no empty track, no button-side fill — *then look*. Then, unprompted, the two proposals themselves: *when you mouse over a 🪶✒️✏️🏛️ button, the token in the topbar that will be spent is somehow highlighted*, and *when someone just clicks and doesn't hold, the token jumps 1/4 the way towards the button, so that it's hard to miss that you're making something happen*. Both put the teaching on **the thing being spent** rather than on the button, which is more on-metaphor than anything on the original list: the price is an object, so the object reacts. Named `spend-preview` and `short-press-nudge`; see the glossary.

    **What the ground survey found, which changed the job.** (a) **There was no return flight on ✒️ or 🪶 at all** — `penRelease` called `flight.cancel()`, which removes the glyph outright — so a tap on the two buttons a founder meets *first* made a feather appear near the wallet and vanish. The nudge is those buttons' first fly-home, not an adjustment to one. (b) **Every wallet token's animation was being destroyed on a timer**: the ✏️ row rebuilds its innerHTML every second on the drip, the power wallets on every render, and only `#quill` had an idempotent write guard — so no transition on a ✏️ or ✒️ token could ever run to completion. Latent, and the lean walked straight into it. (c) `flyGlyph`'s safety timeout owns its traveller, so a release at 990ms of a 1000ms flight would have had the glyph deleted in mid-air at 1080ms while it was still rewinding; it now hands ownership over (`disarm`).

    **Three things settled during the build.** **"A quarter of the way" is ambiguous by 60%** — the pen flies `linear` but the pencil flies `cubic-bezier(.45, .05, .3, 1)`, whose slow start makes a quarter of the *duration* only 18% of the *arc*. The floor is **distance**, because there is no track on the surface and nobody can perceive a fraction of a duration: 250ms of 1000 for the pen, 864ms of 3000 for the pencil. **The throw needs a minimum rate, not a fixed duration** — measured at 0.94x on the pen, i.e. the token drifting forward *slower* than the hold moves it, which is the exact opposite of the point; a 1.8x floor means the flick is always a speed the hold cannot produce, which is what makes the exaggeration read as momentum rather than as progress. And **the preview must not key on `:hover`**: the first version self-healed by testing `leanBtn.matches(':hover')`, which is **false in headless Chromium with the pointer parked on the button** — so every harness would have been blind to the feature, and correctness would have rested on a paint state. It tracks the pointer from the events instead (a `pointerover` on anything that does not spend is itself the proof the pointer has left), with `isConnected` as the structural self-heal for the case that actually happens: a card committing and closing under the cursor.

    **Honesty note, since the floor over-reports.** Position on an arc is only a progress *reading* if there is a scale to read it against, and Ed's own answer forbade the things that would supply one. What a viewer can perceive is departure, distance and return — not "this is 25%". The lie is confined to releases below a quarter, where the truthful alternative is showing nothing, which is the bug. A floor on *time in the air* instead would be unimpeachable and useless: 100ms of the pencil's easing is 3% of the arc, under 20px, invisible at the corner of the eye.

    **Verified**: session-probe IDENTICAL (43 cards, all geometry) — the hover and nudge work is entirely invisible to it, by design; setup-probe 42 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed after the re-freeze the ✒️∞ markup required; `npm run journey` exit 0 with `errors · none` and `refused · none`; lint, typecheck and 480 tests green. Directly measured, since no harness covers either mechanism: the lean's keyframes point at the button, its `startTime` survives repeated wallet rebuilds unchanged, moving away leaves zero animations running, the throw runs at 1.8x, the ghost clears only when the token lands, and under reduced motion the lean is pure opacity and the nudge is a quarter of a fade.

    **Left open.** (a) **The empty track at rest** is still the only signal that works before the user has done anything and the only one that works on touch, where neither of these exists — held in reserve pending Ed watching testers meet the built version. (b) **🍾 and 🏛️** keep their deferral for the *preview*; note the **nudge reached 🍾 anyway** (its cork now flies back to the button on a short press rather than vanishing), since it rides `penRelease` — which is what Ed asked for in *clearly the others should also fly back*. 🏛️'s assembly-press is untouched and still shows nothing for a tap. (c) The two flight paths **disagree about reduced motion** and always have: `flyStart` fades out at the *source* and never appears at the destination, `flyGlyph` fades in at the *destination* and never appears at the source, and the comment above the first describes a third behaviour it does not have (*"fades at the wallet and arrives at the button"*). Pre-existing, deliberately left alone rather than changed in a batch about something else.

532. **The topbar as a toolbar: every wallet visible from the start, empty ones shown empty.** (Ed, 2026-08-22, mid-build on Q531: *perhaps we should give all of the wallets to all users at the beginning, and explicitly show that empty ones are empty; the topbar is a toolbar, and tools are added and taken away depending on what's happening; I hope this makes it clearer to users why we're going to such effort making a distinction between them. More evidence for 🛡️ as a tool perhaps.*) Raised, not built — it **reverses a rule from the day before** and deserves its own decision rather than being folded into a build about holds.

    **The rule it meets.** *A power is not held until it has been acknowledged* (2026-08-21) and its consequences: **an offer disappears**, and **a question you may not answer is not shown at all** — Ed's own words, *I shouldn't be shown actions that I can't do*. `.navbar .powerwallet:empty { display: none; }` is that rule in CSS, and the grant machinery is built on the wallet *appearing*: each grant is a task, acknowledged, and only then does the glyph fly into a wallet that did not exist a moment earlier.

    **Why it may not be a reversal at all, which is the interesting part.** That rule is about **actions and questions** — things you could press, answers you could give. An **empty slot is neither**: it asks nothing, offers nothing and cannot be pressed. And the gain Ed names is real and is not available any other way: the four wallets are **the four verbs**, a ladder of *who has to agree*, and a member who only ever sees the ones they hold never sees the ladder — so the distinction the whole design is built on is invisible to exactly the people it governs. There is a second, unlooked-for gain: the pen currently flies to a wallet that **does not exist until it lands**, which is why `renderPowerWallets` has to conjure it mid-flight; a permanent slot makes the destination visible *before* the flight, which is the same argument Q531 just made about the button. So the grant moment probably gets **stronger**, not weaker: the topbar stops changing shape, but the flight gains a target you can see it aiming at.

    **Three things need Ed's answer before it can be built.** **(a) Who sees the empty slots?** Members plainly. A **stranger** and an **applicant** are not in the room and three empty slots would be noise about a document they cannot act in. A **clerk** is the hard one: by design they have no ✏️ and no 🏛️ for the life of the document (*a clerk's topbar holds nothing but 🪶*), so a permanent empty pair is either honest information about what a clerk is or a standing reproach on every screen. **(b) What does empty look like?** The surface already has both halves of the answer and they disagree: grey means *nothing is being asked of you*, and `.gone` is a slot that keeps its width at `visibility: hidden`. A dimmed glyph is probably right and a dashed box certainly wrong, but an empty slot must not read as a control that is merely disabled. **(c) Does 🛡️ join now or later?** It is coherent — ✒️ and 🛡️ are the two powers, held per setting, *one pen many locks* — and it would make the toolbar the complete statement of what you hold. It is also an ∞ wallet like the pen, since a veto is never spent; it acts by existing. Ed's parenthetical suggests he already thinks so.

    **One consequence to fix rather than ask about.** `holdWallet` tests **the existence of `#penwallet i`** as its proxy for *you hold a pen*, and the guard that swallows a hold's trailing click reads `isPenCommit(held) && …`. If an empty wallet renders a token, that test silently becomes always-true — and Q531's `spend-preview` inherits it, since the preview is gated on the same function. Both must re-base on `mayPen()` / `mayVoice()` rather than on DOM presence, in the same change.

    Related: this is also a partial answer to **531(a)**, the empty track at rest — an always-visible wallet is an at-rest affordance for the *powers*, though not for the *hold*.

    **Answered and built 2026-08-22.** **(a) Everyone** — *a row of empty bars explains to you what your role can do, and "nothing" is a useful answer.* **(b)** each wallet is **a pill with an inner shadow**, a depression saying *something goes here*; an unheld tool is drawn in its socket **with a diagonal red line across it**; and **clicking a socket opens a speech bubble** explaining what it means. **(c) 🛡️ goes in** — *let's put it in and see how it looks.*

    **The distinction the build added, which Ed's phrasing collapsed.** *Empty* is two states and only one of them is a slash. A member who holds ✏️ and has spent them all still **holds the power** — the drip is running and the tray already says when the next lands — and that is `.empty` plus a countdown, as it always was. A clerk, a stranger, an applicant, a member before 🍾 or before accepting the grant **does not hold it at all**, and that is `.notheld`, struck through. A slash across both would tell a member they had lost a power they still have. So `walletHeld` is its own piece of wallet render state beside `walletShow` and `walletGhost`, never a class the page toggles from outside — `renderWallet` rewrites `className` wholesale and would wipe it.

    **The red is built as asked and is the only red on the surface**, which is worth keeping in view: the palette is three hues and a grey, and 🗑️ was deliberately *not* red on the argument that discarding is an ordinary thing to do rather than a danger. Not holding a power is the same kind of fact — the shape of your role, not an error — and a clerk wears two of these all session. **`?slash=muted`** renders the identical toolbar with the strike in the surface's own muted ink, the same shape as the stranger's door's `?bars=black`: an experiment you can look at rather than an argument.

    **Three things found in the building.** **The slash came out grey**, because a `::after` on the `<i>` is inside the box carrying `filter: grayscale(1)` — the one colour decision in the rule, quietly undone by the line above it. It hangs on the socket now. **`holdWallet` was inferring the pen from the DOM**: it tested `document.querySelector('#penwallet i')`, a true proxy for *you hold a pen* only while an unheld wallet rendered nothing — with every socket now drawing its tool that test would be permanently true, so an unacknowledged pen would have become a working hold, and the guard that swallows a hold's trailing click reads `isPenCommit(held)`. It asks `mayPen()` now, which was always the question. And **the navbar's height is load-bearing**: it is sticky and everything below is measured against it, so a socket taller than the 26px the avatar already sets would have moved every card on the surface. 24px, measured at 48.5px navbar before and after.

    **🛡️ needed nothing from the module.** `pwPair(k)` is the single place a setting becomes a `{u, a}` pair, covers every `PW_KEYS` entry including the Text, and reads the same off the blind projection in live mode — so `PW_KEYS.some(k => pwPair(k).a)` is the exact mirror of the fold the pen was already doing. It takes the ∞ too, being equally perpetual.

    **Verified**: navbar 48.5px unchanged in every state; session-probe IDENTICAL (43 cards, all geometry); setup-probe 42 steps, 0 diffs, 0 allowed after the re-freeze; journey exit 0, `errors · none`, `refused · none`; lint, typecheck and 480 tests green.

    **Residuals.** (a) **The closed page keeps its old answer** — the farewell flies the tools out and the sockets go with them (`.gonewallet`). Ed's rule arguably reaches there too, and rather well: the tools *were* taken away, which is exactly what a row of empty sockets says. Not done, because it changes a finished ceremony rather than a live toolbar, and four red strikes on a grey filed page is a different aesthetic question. (b) **🛡️ rides `grant-pen`'s acknowledgment** rather than having a grant of its own. Defensible — both powers arrive in the founder's hands by one act at one moment, and the save is when that becomes real — but the purer reading of *no power arrives without acknowledgment* gives it its own card, its own flight and its own place in the founding order. (c) **The quill's link now lives inside its bubble**, since a bubble over the logo would otherwise swallow the one capability it carries. That is a real improvement hiding in a side effect: a founder mid-birth who clicked the mark used to be navigated away from the document they were making.

    **The three residuals answered 2026-08-22, and two of them built.** **(a) The closed page keeps hiding its sockets** — Ed: *agree, the sockets only make sense when the document is in session.* No change; `.gonewallet` is the rule. **(b) The strike is dark red** (`#8e2116`), chosen with both versions on screen: at full saturation four strikes read as four errors, where the fact being stated is only that this is not your tool. `?slash=muted` stays reachable for comparison. **(c) 🛡️ has its own acknowledgment** — Ed: *a founder should have to acknowledge both separately; it's very important for them to understand what they can and can't do.* Its own card (`grant-shield`, 🛡️ *Your Shield*), its own key in `GRANT_KEYS`, its own flight into its own socket, its own clause. It stands **immediately after ✒️** in `ORDER`, so the pen is met, accepted, and then the shield is met alone — and it **does not block** the queue, keeping 🏛️'s rule rather than the pen's exception, because you cannot commit anything with a shield. That also leaves the rail at the save holding the pen alone, which the journey walk asserts. Measured on the live path: `at save · rail ["grant-pen"]`, then `ok · grant-pen`, then `ok · grant-shield`.

    **The bubble says the symbol and the verb** (Ed, 2026-08-22: *don't name the item — just have the symbol, larger than in the wallet, and then explain what it is using the verb; keep the language as clear and functional as possible, using verbs and nouns that relate to concrete things on the page*). So no *"The shield."* opening: naming a thing is not explaining it, and the glyph has already said which one it is. Each sentence starts with **who can do what**, and every noun in it is something the reader can point at — the text, a setting, a ✒️ tab, the membership. **Held and not-held differ by subject**: holding it, the sentence is about *you* and says what it spends and what comes back; not holding it, it names *who can*, which is the question a struck-through tool actually raises. One table (`SAY`) feeds both the bubble and the tooltip, so the two channels cannot drift — which needed a seam in session.js (`setWalletTitle`), since the ✏️ socket is rendered there and the drip rewrote its title every second.

    **Residuals now.** (a) The grant flight's destination table replaced a pen-or-voice ternary pair; three of them agreeing by hand was one place two would drift. (b) 🛡️'s clause and card copy have not been through a STYLE.md pass with the rest of the founding — `npm run founding` prints them in order and is the tool for it.

533. **`design/setup.notes.md`'s dependency tree describes a surface that no longer exists.** (Found during housekeeping, 2026-08-22; claimed here rather than fixed, because it is a rewrite rather than a correction.) CLAUDE.md lists the notes as *kept current at Ed's ask — the shared task grammar and the founding dependency tree*, and the tree is the half that has rotted: it is the **wave graph**, retired on 2026-08-21 when the founding went single file, and `ORDER` in session-view.html has been the dependency list ever since. It also predates the glyph batch (🏷️ and 🔗 for what are now 🪶 and 📍) and every grant that has since taken a place in the order. Marked *superseded, kept as history* at its head for now, which is honest but leaves a load-bearing document half-true. **(a)** Rewrite it as a description of the single file, which is mostly a matter of transcribing `ORDER` and saying what each card genuinely needs from the ones above it. **(b)** Delete the tree and let `ORDER` plus the CLAUDE.md entry be the whole answer — the reasoning about *literal* dependencies (the mail names the document; nothing is saved before the address is proven) would need somewhere to go first. **(c)** Leave the marker and let it rot as history. I would do (a): the literal-versus-pacing distinction it records is the reason the single file is safe to reorder, and that argument exists nowhere else.

534. **Should the address 📍 pre-fills be free by construction?** (From the fix of 2026-08-22, below.) 📍 is born pre-filled with `slugify(title)` and the card now asks the server about it on arrival, so a founder whose title collides with an existing document meets a task that is refused the moment it opens — correct, honest, and still a small unpleasantness at the third step of the birth. The server already computes the nearest free address twice (for the 409 and now for the check), and `uniqueSlugAsync` is exactly the function that would answer this. **(a)** As built: suggest the plain address, check it, and let the founder read *taken* and take the offered alternative. It is one extra press, and it shows them the address is a real, contested thing rather than a slot the machine fills — which is arguably worth teaching at the birth. **(b)** Ask the server for the suggestion at title-time, so the pre-filled address is free when it arrives and the refused state is only ever reached by typing. Costs a request per title, and the suggestion can still go stale between the fill and the send, so the check and the 409 both stay regardless. **(c)** (b) plus a note saying why the address is not simply the title — *docs.vote/charter is taken, so this one is charter-2* — which is the honest version of (b), since silently handing somebody a suffixed address is the machine making a decision without saying so. I lean (a): the refusal is rare, it is now legible and actionable, and (b) buys a smoother path at the cost of a request on every title keystroke's settle and a suggestion that can lie by the time it matters. Worth your call because it is a founding-experience question rather than a correctness one. **Ed, 2026-08-22: (c)** — the address moves, and the move says so. Built on the distinction the fix already needed: **the address is the machine's until you touch it**. A *suggested* address that comes back taken moves to the nearest free one before the founder meets it; a *typed* one never moves, because there the address is theirs and a collision is a refusal to read and correct. `S.slugAuto` is the whole of it, set in `fillSuggestedSlug` — the one place a suggestion is applied, where there had been three copies of the same three lines — and cleared by the one act that can make the address yours. The note names both addresses (*Suggested from the title — docs.vote/charter is taken, so this one is docs.vote/charter-2*), which is what makes it (c) rather than (b). No new endpoint and no request per keystroke: the `suggestion` the check already carries is what it moves to, so the cost worried about in (b) never arrives. Two guards: the address it moves **to** is asked about in its own right on the next render rather than trusted from the response that offered it, and `autoSlugMoves` caps the chain at three, past which the collision is simply shown — a server that kept offering an address it then refused would otherwise be a request storm.

535. **Does `scripts/slug-walk.mjs` stay?** (Raised with the same fix.) It drives a live birth onto an address that is already reserved and asserts 📍 refuses it *there* — the commit dark, the note naming the nearest free address, nothing sent at the wire — then corrects the address and walks the birth through clean. It is the walk that fails on the pre-fix page, which is the only reason to trust it. It exists because `npm run journey` births with a unique title and so can never meet a taken address, and both probes drive the fixture, where the check is synchronous and the live path is never touched. **(a)** Keep it as its own script with an npm entry beside `journey`, which is what it is: a second live-path walk, for the birth's refusals rather than its happy path. **(b)** Fold its assertions into `journey-walk.mjs` — it would have to reserve an address first, which makes the journey no longer a single straight line but keeps one live harness rather than two. **(c)** Drop it; the server test pins the endpoint and the surface rule is written down in CLAUDE.md. I recommend (a): this is the second bug in two days that ended at a correct 409 reading as the page malfunctioning, and neither existing harness could see either of them. It is committed with the fix so it is not an unexplained untracked file; say the word and it goes. **Ed, 2026-08-22: (a)** — kept, with `npm run slug-walk` beside `npm run journey`. Q534 (c) changed what it walks, and for the better: it now covers **both** halves of the rule rather than one — that a *suggested* address moves off a collision, naming both addresses, and that a *typed* one is refused and stays refused with nothing sent at the wire. It fails on the pre-fix page at *the commit is dark on a typed address that is taken*. It needs a reserved address to walk against, so its header carries the one-line `curl` that makes one.

315. **The ranking model as a document-creation setting: approval · Bradley–Terry · Crowd-BT** (Ed, 2026-08-17, parked as a note for later). *There are situations where each is appropriate and I think much of our machinery still applies for each.* Crowd-BT is Chen, Bennett, Collins-Thompson & Horvitz, *Pairwise Ranking Aggregation in a Crowdsourced Setting*, WSDM 2013 (10.1145/2433396.2433420) — Bradley–Terry with a **per-judge reliability parameter** estimated jointly with the item scores, plus an active-learning scheme that chooses which pair to serve *and whom to serve it to*. Details want checking against the paper before anything is built on them.

Ed is right that most of the machinery is indifferent to the choice. The `patch-engine`, `overlap-gates` and `dedup-gate` are text machinery and never touch the model. The `adoption-threshold` and its ramp are stated as P(challenger beats incumbent), and all three produce something that plays that role. Quorum, adoption floors, the record, the gazette, and the whole of `session-view` are unaffected — a card asks for a judgment either way.

What genuinely changes is worth listing, because it is more than the estimator. **Active sampling largely disappears under approval**: everybody can rate everything, so the `router` stops *selecting* and only *orders*, which would make the queue dramatically shorter and the `evidence-meter` mean something different. **Deadlock (§8.3) is a Bradley–Terry construct** — *close relative to the marginal value of further sampling* — and under approval it becomes two candidates with similar approval rates, which is detectable but is not the same claim. **Indifference** has no separate existence under approval; approving both *is* indifference. And the **`bounty score`** rests on resolvable disagreement, which is model-specific.

Two things worth having written down before this is picked up.

**The choice is constitutional, not statistical.** Crowd-BT's reliability parameter says a judge is *often wrong*, which presupposes there is a right answer to be wrong about. This project's §3.3 says the opposite in as many words — *preference is not measurement*. If Hollis prefers the shorter clause and thirteen people prefer the longer one, Hollis is not unreliable; Hollis disagrees. Down-weighting a judge is **disenfranchising them by an algorithm nobody in the room can audit**, which is a serious thing to do to a member of a constitutional convention. But there are documents where it is exactly right — a technical standard, a specification being cleaned up, any domain where some readings are simply mistakes — and those are the same documents where a large roster contains people of very different expertise. So the real question a convenor is answering is **does this document treat disagreement as difference or as error**, which is the kind of question that belongs at `document-creation` or the `founding-ceremony` beside quorum and the bar, and not in a settings menu. Under the §9.0a logic it is arguably the roster's to settle rather than the convenor's: being told your judgments count for less is a thing done *to* you.

**And the sophisticated model is probably wrong at v1 scale.** Crowd-BT spends data on extra per-judge parameters, and CLAUDE.md tunes v1 for rosters of 5–10 where data-efficiency beats throughput; on fourteen judges it may be strictly worse than plain Bradley–Terry, which is itself chosen over approval *because* it extracts more per judgment. Crowd-BT's home is hundreds of annotators. Which puts its threshold at roughly the roster size where you stop being able to know everyone — the same point at which a room stops being able to vouch for each other's good faith. Those two thresholds coinciding is not a coincidence, and it is the strongest argument that this is a real setting rather than a knob: **the model should change when the room changes kind.**

One reversal to expect if approval is built: it produces the **more legible record**. The `sealed record`'s best sentence — *these two beat what we had and those three did not* — is currently a Bradley–Terry construction in which every number is a probability against the incumbent. Approval counts are less informative and far easier for a lay reader to trust. The more sophisticated model makes the harder-to-explain record, which matters for a document whose legitimacy rests on people believing the record.

42. **Spectator commentary view** (Ed, 2026-08-14): an optional commentating view for people watching a convention — like the sim's commentator, but consuming **public data only**: gazette, live candidate texts/rationales, bounty board, document state. Never individual judgments, never standings, authorship only per the session's visibility setting. Provision: expose a `spectator-api` in engine-core (a strictly-public projection, sibling of the participant-api) and have every spectator surface — commentary LLM included — consume only that, so privacy holds by construction rather than by prompt discipline. Note the sim's own commentator is deliberately different: it is omniscient (sees temperaments and hidden agendas) because it narrates a fiction for the experimenter; the product spectator box narrates real people and gets none of that.

55. **Participant-api composer surface** (sim evidence 2026-08-14): `ParticipantApi` has no composer-opening call — the sim runner invokes `Session.openComposer` directly (same precedent as its dedup co-sign path). The product composer needs that surface, including the §3.3 forfeit semantics, before the UI can exist.

56. **Orphan refunds credit departed ledgers** (sim evidence 2026-08-14): when an author-departed candidate is adopted, its refund credits the removed member's ledger — harmless (tokens are worthless at close and E excludes them), but the UI must not render it as an invitation to act.
