244. **A flow entry can overlap a pinned one** (found 2026-08-16 while verifying 222; pre-existing, not caused by it). Flow entries are supposed to step around the pinned pile rather than hide under it (112), and `freeFor` re-checks every candidate position against the pinned blocks — yet one case still renders overlapping. Reproduce: open `quick-cellar` from the top of the document and `quick-probation` (a judged line) lands inside `quick-attendance`. The same shape occurs on the previous build with a different pair (`diag-quorum-keys` over `quick-notice`), so it predates the claim-absolute change. Not chased further because it is bounded and cosmetic, but the likely suspects are a height measured before a re-render or the `prev + QGAP` adjustment running against stale blocks.

248. **The ⌈E/3⌉ has never been calibrated** (raised 2026-08-16). It arrived with spec v0.5 in the initial commit and has never been the subject of a study, unlike its neighbours — the hot set went 6 → 3 on a sweep, the economy was checked at rosters 5–14, the ramp was argued out. §8.2 justifies it as "statistical sufficiency", which is the wrong account: sufficiency depends on effect size and noise, not on how many people are in the room, and the adoption threshold is already doing the confidence work. What the floor actually does is **legitimacy** — the claim that enough of the room looked for the outcome to be theirs. That is a political quantity, and political quantities are argued rather than derived, which is why a third feels arbitrary: it is. The one relevant piece of evidence is finding 10 of the deferred-evidence pass, where F dropping to 2 produced adoptions that were still fine *because they had to clear a ~0.9 posterior bar*. A sweep of F against welfare and against how much ends up parked as backlog would settle it. Now partly superseded by §9.0a — where the roster sets its own quorum, the default matters less, but a default is still needed.

251. **The token economy has no perpetual form** (raised 2026-08-16). Grant 4, drip 1 per 10% of window, reset each session — none of it survives a document without a window (§9.0). The drip becomes a rate against real time and the reset simply goes, but the rate and cap want choosing, and the choice is now visible to members rather than internal: Ed's `edit-wallet` (2026-08-16) draws tokens as ✏️ glyphs, one vanishing per use, with the next one inking in as the drip accrues. Worth knowing before tuning it: §7's calibration note says participants **sit near the cap** at v1 defaults, so in a windowed document the wallet would show eight full pencils all session and say nothing. Two risks in that. A device that never moves is not earning its place; and a visibly depleting wallet reintroduces psychological friction that the economy does not actually require, which cuts against the whole point of the always-on composer. Either the economy tightens enough for the display to be honest, or the wallet stays quiet while it is full. *Half-answered 2026-08-16 by the composer: proposing spends a pencil and withdrawing returns it, so the wallet now answers your own actions and is not purely decorative. The calibration worry stands — at v1 defaults a real member still sits near the cap.*

252. **A perpetual document loses the reset** (raised 2026-08-16, from §9.0). §9.4 and §4.5 lean on it: next session the threshold resets, the backlog re-enters stake-waived, and everything becomes contestable again — "entrenchment is session-scoped". With no sessions, an adopted clause stands until something clears the same fixed bar, and there is no periodic moment inviting the room to reconsider. This is the one place where perpetual is worse rather than merely different. Candidates for a replacement: a scheduled thaw; a rule that a clause untouched for long enough re-enters the backlog; or accepting that a perpetual document *should* entrench, since that is what a standing charter is for.

259. **The founding ceremony is a third ceremony, and wants designing** (raised 2026-08-16; Ed: "look forward to designing the founding ceremony!"). §9.0a specifies the rule — blind collection of each member's lowest acceptable quorum and threshold, maximum of each, distribution published without names — but not the surface. It is the only moment where the mechanism chooses its own parameters, it happens before any drafting, and the record of what people asked for is itself part of what the convention produced. Related: the `document-creation` screen (Ed, 2026-08-16), which is where every §9.0 setting is chosen and which Ed wants to see in full before any options are cut. **Naming**: Ed called it a "draft creation screen"; `draft` already means a candidate patch throughout this project, so the surface is `document-creation` and the thing it makes is a document.

## Spent numbers

**1133–1144 are the plan-queue batch of 2026-09-02 (queue items 145–148)**, claimed by the batch pre-flight against `ca67b1f` **before any of the four was built**, and claimed for the whole batch at once for the reason the 1113–1120 block gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. All four are Ed's QA of batch AD, 2026-09-02. **1133–1135 are *📝 is the door in both directions*** (queue item 145): **1133** Ed's ruling of 2026-09-02, put to him as page-versus-spec — *the page is wrong, one press leaves* — pressing 📝 with a draft open closes the card, drops the lift and the caret and **keeps the draft**, K31's *leaving is not discarding* unamended; **1134** what the diagnosis actually finds, if it is not the strip/`card-morph` path the plan guesses at (the pre-flight's reading is that `toggleEditMode`'s entering branch calls `SESSION.closeCard()` and its leaving branch does not); **1135** any K31 clarification the fix proves necessary, which the plan would rather not need. **1136–1139 are *clause text wears the clause font*** (queue item 146): **1136** Ed's general ruling — *all clause text in radio options should be in clause font* — folded as a **T-rule** and a §7 item, and the question of whether a typographic rule belongs in STYLE.md at all or in SURFACE §9.3 beside CP1; **1137** ⏱️'s inputs inline in the sentence, which at `ca67b1f` is not a CSS matter: the founder's card is `opt(S,'rateBy','founder','I set it', …)` over three stacked `num()` fields, so the question is whether ⏱️ takes CP1's named-rung shape (Q1104 (b)) or keeps one block with the fields inlined into a `RATE_RULE`-shaped sentence; **1138** the clause-text-versus-control-label test at every option site, and the flag on the option the plan offers to propose rather than restyling everything; **1139** the geometry, if the ~14% widening produces a `card-audit` finding at either window size. **1140–1142 are *the ladder says what it built*** (queue item 147): **1140** Ed's ruling that `--to` needs a manifest — counts read from the document rather than the rung's intentions, and the seat named wherever a thing is seat-gated; **1141** where the manifest's counts come from, the walk driving the bar and never seeing `LadderResult`, so the choice is a new field on `GET /api/dev/ladder` in `server.ts` (product code carrying a `DEV:` block) or `engine.jsonl` off disk; **1142** `--to=birth`, which the walk's own five-rung `RUNGS` cannot stop at. **1143–1144 are *🌍's closed rung is one sentence for a clerk*** (queue item 148): **1143** the capitalisation — Ed wrote *founder* and the surface's convention is **the Founder** as an office, so it goes in capitalised and is flagged for him to overrule; **1144** whether the clerk form wants a walk of its own, no walk seating a clerk today and the copy golden therefore green through this change. Blocks are generous; **whatever is not spent is released when the batch folds**, and a plan that spends none of its own is named as having spent none. No `R-nnn` key is claimed here: none of the four folds a SPEC ruling, and `R-081` stands as the tail. **Queue item 145 built 2026-09-02: 1133 and 1134 spent, 1135 released.** **1133** is Ed's ruling as this block states it, built without amendment. **1134** is the diagnosis, and it is neither of the two guesses: the press reaches `toggleEditMode` and its leaving branch is sound, but `S.editMode` is already false by then, because `extra.closeOthers` clears it whenever *any* charter card opens and the editing card is one — so 📝 took the **entering** branch, closed the card and turned the mode back on, which is Ed's report to the letter. The fix keeps edit mode for the draft's own card (K31 lists what leaves, and the editing card is not among them), closes the open card as the door leaves, and stops a trailing-gap draft rendering twice now that the gap block survives the card opening. **1135** is released: K31 needed no clarifying clause. Correction to this block's own record: *nothing asserted the closing half* was stale at `ca67b1f` — `journey-walk` asserted the plain leave twice; what was unasserted is leaving with an editing card open, and that is what the new step walks. **Queue item 147 built 2026-09-02: 1140, 1141 and 1142 all spent.** **1140** is Ed's ruling as this block states it, built without amendment: `manifestOf` reads the counts back from the engine's own candidate states and log — never from `LadderResult`'s `built`/`skipped`, which are the rung's intentions — and `ManifestLine.seat` names the founder's seat on the 👑 line, which is the line that would have unblocked QA 4. The distinction is not academic: on the plan's own seed 567693 the engine log holds **four** `candidate-awaiting-assent` events, of which three were assented and adopted (c1, c4, c7), so the document has **one** parked adoption standing — the manifest says one, and the plan's Context, reading the raw event count, said four. **1141** is answered *the route, not the disk*: the counts ride a new `manifest` field on `GET /api/dev/ladder` (`server.ts`), because the walk drives the bar and so never sees `runLadder`'s return, and because the walk holds a slug and not a docId, has no `DRAFT_DATA_DIR`, and reading `engine.jsonl` would couple it to `FilePersistence`'s layout that `PgPersistence` does not share. The handler body is inside the `DEV:` label esbuild drops bodily, so *nothing here ships* still holds and `scripts/build-server.mjs`'s grep is the guard. One thing found in the building: a manifest that throws takes the **bar** down, because the bar draws nothing if this GET errors and the walk then reports *the ladder bar has no ⏭ — is the server in dev mail mode?*; the field is therefore served through a `try`/`catch` that degrades to one line. **1142** is answered by B49 (Ed, 2026-09-02): **`--to=birth` is dropped from Verification and refused at the door**. The walk's own `RUNGS` stays the five *presses* — a bare `npm run ladder` is unchanged — and an unknown `--to` now exits 1 with *no such stop*, so `--to=birth` can no longer walk the whole ladder to `closed` and read as a pass. The reason it can never be a stop is `runLadder`'s own shape: the birth is *no document*, and the first press both creates it and fills the room.

**1136–1139 built 2026-09-02 — all four spent, none released.** **1136** folded as **STYLE T46** and **§7 item 79**, and its *where does a typographic rule live* half answered **STYLE.md, with a pointer from SURFACE §9.3's CP1**: CP1 states the option block's parts and no type treatment, so it cites T46 rather than restating it (CLAUDE.md's own *cite rather than restate*). **1137** answered as the block's second option — ⏱️ keeps one option block and `RATE_RULE` **is** its text, with three inline `numIn` inputs where the numbers go; CP1's named-rung shape (Q1104 (b)) was not taken, because a rung offers preset values where Ed asked to read *the sentence with editable numbers in it*, and rungs would have invented rung labels this plan was told to change no strings for. Two consequences worth Ed's eye at the QA sitting: **the three field labels and *I set it* leave ⏱️'s card** (the sentence says what each number is), and **a number typed into the sentence claims the rung it stands in**, the boxes being legible from the moment the card opens rather than hidden behind `.pick > .inner`. **1138** answered by adding the flag the plan offered to propose: `ctl`, marked on the **exception** — eighteen control-word sites listed in item 79 — so an option carrying a clause is right by default, which is the direction Ed's rule is general in. **1139** spent on the measurement and **found nothing**: `card-audit` at 1600×1000 and 1280×900 reports the same ten findings before and after, same codes and counts, so no new P1, P5 or S1 at either size; every radio still lines up down one left edge and no rung gained a wrapped line. **Open for the QA sitting**: whether 👥 should follow ⏱️ — it still reads *I set it* over a `num()` field, being the other numeric setting card, and this plan's scope was ⏱️ alone.

**1122–1128 are the SURFACE §8.1 tabulation** (plan-queue queue item 140, backlog entry 191 as **1050** redefined it, answering **1006**'s *its own question number when it is written*), claimed by the batch pre-flight against `5b126a8` **before the plan was built**, for the reason the 1113–1120 block gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. **1122** the pass itself — the column set F1–F23 were actually tabulated under, or the evidence that they cannot be tabulated without loss, which 1006 admits as an outcome; **1123–1127** the row-by-row verdicts Ed gives in `design/spec-pass/pass-5.html`, one number per family of veto rather than one per row, and any rule the builder marks as a question instead of a row; **1128** the F-id guard's own finding — F5, F18, F19, F7, F3, F9, F8, F23 and five more are **cited more than once** inside `SURFACE.md`, so an assertion that each id *appears* exactly once is unbuildable and what is asserted has to be that each is **defined** exactly once. **The tabulation must not renumber F1–F23** (Ed, 2026-08-29, twice). Blocks are generous; **whatever is not spent is released when the batch folds**, and a plan that spends none of its own is named as having spent none. **Built 2026-09-01: all seven spent, none released, and every one in the shape claimed for it.** **1122** the pass's verdict — the evidence that the rules cannot be tabulated without loss, which 1006 admits as an outcome, measured rather than asserted: +1,681 B for a faithful table. **1123–1125** the row-by-row verdicts, put as **one question per family** — the eight that tabulate cleanly (7.6% of the section), the nine that are strained, the six that resist (62.8%) — with a per-rule override on all 23 items, which is *one number per family of veto rather than one per row* as this block asked. **1126** F19's six `readiness()` reason codes, **the one genuinely tabular thing the pass found**, asked rather than built because it is a table *inside* F19 and changes its shape. **1127** an *id → what it governs* index above the prose — 1006's readability half without its size claim. **1128** the guard, built exactly as this block predicted: **definitions, never occurrences**, plus the stronger claim that every `F<n>` written anywhere in `SURFACE.md` resolves to a definition.

**1129–1132 are *the gate and grant cards say what the power is*** (plan-queue queue item 141, the open item of batch AD's *accepted-with-open-items* verdict), claimed by the same pre-flight and against the same commit. **1129** Ed's ruling of 2026-09-01, in his words — *You don't even have to say "nothing is being asked here"; just say what proposals/voting/✏️/✒️ are and then they can press OK* — and the principle under it, that **a card body says what the thing is, never what the control does**, which is a candidate T-rule and the rule that would have prevented the dangling *OK puts ✒️ in your wallet* T44 left behind; **1130** the five sentences the build writes for 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ ✒️ 🛡️, which are **Ed's to approve at the QA sitting** and are listed in the run report for that purpose; **1131** how this sits with **T43**, whose own text still permits a card to explain *tasks — what a press does, what is being waited on*, since the new principle narrows exactly that carve-out; **1132** — spare, for what the copy freeze turns up. It supersedes the two sentences STYLE item 76 recorded as kept (*The closing sentence keeps saying what lands* and 💡's count line's OK tail); item 76 stays as the record of what was said when. Same release rule as the block above.

**1121 is *the powers wear their real names*** (plan-queue queue item 137, backlog 263, Ed 2026-09-01: *we scrap "the pen" "the pencil" "the shield" etc and we use the real names… there isn't actually a pen, the icon is just shorthand for the action*). The batch claim below reserved 1113–1120 for queue items 131–136 only and named nothing for 137, so this number is taken from the tail of the sequence rather than from that block. It records **two rulings of the same conversation, both answered before the build**: the **💡-over-✏️ ruling** — the ✏️-grant card, which is also the 💡 gate (Q461a), keeps `g: '💡'`, its settled tab and its place in the preamble stack, and only its **title** becomes *Proposals*; and the **commit principle**, *you should only click a button with 🛡️ on it when you're actually doing a veto*, folded as **STYLE T44** and SURFACE §9.1's OK row — a commit wearing a power's glyph is pressed only when exercising that power, so an acknowledgment never wears one. The second **reverses the commit half of entry 180** and nothing else: the four grants (💡 ✒️ 🛡️ 🏛️) commit with a bare OK, `take` is gone from the card table, and the **mark** still wears the power's glyph while the object still flies from the press. Names as Ed wrote them: ✏️ **Proposal(s)** · 🏛️ **Constitutional Proposal(s)** · ✒️ **Founder Action(s)** · 🛡️ **Founder Veto** — many actions, one veto; *amendment is the change and action is the verb to make the change*, so a text amendment stays an amendment, made by a Founder Action. The **glyphs do not move**, and code and mechanism names (`grant-pen`, `penwallet`, `voicewallet`, `holdsPenAnywhere`, `mayPenOn`, SURFACE §7's wallet row labels) stay as `roster` and `convenor` did. This supersedes **Q1111**'s *Your Consent* as the 🏛️ grant's title while leaving its reasoning standing. The clause-sentence sweep is **backlog 262** and is not this.

**1113–1120 are the plan-queue batch of 2026-09-01 (queue items 131–136)**, claimed by the batch pre-flight against `43a043f` **before either of the two unbuilt plans was built**, and claimed for the whole batch at once for the reason the 1038–1049 block gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. **1113–1115 are reclaimed from pass 4's released tail** on the same precedent 1109–1112 took (released numbers are reusable), so the run is continuous. **Queue items 131–134 — the card-pattern plans A, B, C and D — take no new number: they were built on 2026-08-31 at `dbb7551`, `1e91aa1`, `6c489b4` and `fd7144f`, and their questions are 1096–1108 above, already spent.** **1113–1116 are *the founder's seat reaches the judging surface*** (queue item 135, Ed 2026-09-01, *please fix both*): **1113** the ruling itself — a gate never withholds from the seat that set it, C8's *were you here when it was set?* read from the founder's side, and where that exemption is expressed given that 💡 and ⚖️ are **derived** gates with no holder of their own (`canPropose()` is `textConfirmed && constituted()`, `canJudge()` is `constituted && !frozen`), so the test has to be written over the settings the gate follows from rather than over the gate; **1114** what a **delegated** gate means for the exemption, the plan's third acceptance case, which has no referent in the catalogue today; **1115** the empty-rail defect found while diagnosing (a hand-seeded `draft:grants:<slug>:founder` empties the rail entirely) — reachable by a real member path, or shown unreachable; **1116** — spare, for whatever the diagnosis turns up. **1117–1120 are *📝 is the door*** (queue item 136, Ed 2026-09-01, *Design moved: click/📝 first*): **1117** the K13 amendment — the charter mounts in read mode with no caret and 📝 is the door, and which of K13's and K31's read-mode-keystroke sentences go with it; **1118** the walk's typing step entering through 📝, and the crash-to-FAIL guard item 261 deferred; **1119** the setup-probe's four dead 💤 steps and `probe-coverage`'s `✗ lapse` — **the cause is not the retired selectors the plan names**: the founding scenario chooses `docShape=meeting` at its `choose-shape` step and `shapes.ts` gives a meeting `hides: ['lapse']`, so 💤 has never been in that scenario's DOM, and the choice is between changing the scenario's shape (which re-frozen references would absorb wholesale), adding a second scenario, and an EXEMPT the plan forbids; **1120** — spare, for the freeze's own findings. Blocks are generous; **whatever is not spent is released when the batch folds**, and a plan that spends none of its own is named as having spent none.

**1109–1112 are reclaimed from pass 4's released block** (2026-08-31 evening, Ed's QA of the card-pattern batch, claimed against `dfd46e7` — released numbers are reusable, as pass 2's reuse of pass 1's established): **1109** the founding setting cards' conformance scope — **answered by Ed the same hour, (a), the charter card's grammar whole**, and built the same night: the options are the clause map's own sentences (`ADM_RULE` and family), the why paragraphs and explainers went, 🎩 and 👥's form pair got clause-voice sentences of their own (STYLE §7 item 74), and the head half was already built (`headFor` gives an open settled card its clause head). **1110** the blind answer rungs' register — **answered by Ed 2026-09-01, (a)**: they keep *Prefer this*, an answer being yours alone but feeding the room's rule; SURFACE CP2 states it and `ansRow`'s comment carries the reason. **1111** the 🏛️ grant's name — **answered by Ed 2026-09-01: *Your Consent*** (his *Your Veto* float declined on two grounds put in chat and taken: veto is already 🛡️'s word — *Does the Founder Have a Veto?* — and the token says yes as often as no), built the same hour: the title, the take-verb, the why, the standalone clause (now *Nothing constitutional passes without every member’s consent.*), the four provenance sentences and the value line; `grant-voice`, `voicewallet` and SURFACE §7's `🏛️ voice` row label stay as code/mechanism names. **1112** — **answered by Ed 2026-09-01, (b)**: *we should try and use clause sentences whenever we can; in some cases this may mean we need to add to the clause sentences themselves* — the ladder, composer, strip and readbacks follow the founder card; batch-sized (STYLE item 72's twelve sites, its strip-width measurements redone), **filed as plan-queue backlog 262** with the readback-quotes-the-document change (T43 item 8) inside it.

**1096–1115 are the card pattern (pass 4)**, claimed 2026-08-31 against `f3ee881`. **1096–1102 are Ed's seven rulings on the target card grammar**, asked one at a time in chat and all answered 2026-08-31; folded as SURFACE §9.3 (CP1–CP7) and the DECISIONS entry of the same date. **1096 (a)** one selection grammar everywhere, settings included — each alternative an option block with a hairline between, a radio beneath, and a separate commit; `lanepick`'s click-the-sentence retires as a distinct control. **1097** — against the recommendation (a preference/consent label split) — the radio reads *Prefer this / Preferred* everywhere, consent cards included; that accepting a constitutional change binds you moves into body copy, no longer the label. **1098 (a)** rationale rides acts that propose or change (drafts, motions, the composer, a founder's change); judgments stay silent ranks, blind answers stay sealed, a founder's first set carries none (K12/C11 stand). **1099** — Ed's own shape, not among the three offered — Indifferent is a full option block: hairline like the others, no text, its radio labelled *Indifferent* instead of *Prefer this*; supersedes §9.1's row-left placement. **1100** the stated commit list was illustrative, not exhaustive — 🪶 🍾 📨 and the Take-verbs stand, K29's pairs (✏️+✒️, ✒️+🏛️) stand — and the **👑 question takes the pattern whole** (Ed's same-day refinement): 🗑️ at the left closes it pending, **✒️ accepts and 🛡️ refuses** at the right; Refuse/Accept as words retire and Y20's 👑 clause is struck at the build (amends §9.1 and Y20). **1101 (a)** a member-pile card heads with its people — ✉️ and ❌ take the pile's avatar-and-name list as the head, the status quo being the card's standing text, price and sentence beneath; `adm:` keeps its one-applicant head. **1102 (a)** 🗑️ on every card but Y20's five, judgment cards included — C4 wins; on a judgment 🗑️ clears the selection and closes; whether §9's table rows describe the built page or were a drafting error is the census's first finding. **1103–1108 are the census's questions, all answered by Ed 2026-08-31** in the pass-4 artifact's paste-back, folded as §9.3's amendments the same day; every finding F-A–F-G stands, F-F as a recorded exemption (*📧 can be used twice*). **1103 (b)** — against the recommendation — 🍾's power table joins the block grammar as the surface's one **two-column** card, a ✒️ column and a 🛡️ column of option blocks; the `switch` control retires, and this supersedes the plan-queue backlog entry asking for proper switches. **1104 (b)** — against the recommendation — a numeric setting offers its named rungs as option blocks whose text is **the rule as it would stand** (`meaningOf`'s sentence, the number stated), *A number of my own* last with the field, radios *Prefer this*. **1105 (a)** the direct door's row wears the drawn closing ✓; "Done" retires. **1106 (a)** a card that can never commit shows no commit (CP9). **1107 (a)** the row states the act, the title the state (CP8). **1108 (a)** a patch's site sub-blocks are parts of the patch's one card. **1109–1115 are released.**

**945–947 are the alpha-readiness pass** (plan-queue **77**, backlog entry 77), claimed 2026-08-27 against `c4c030c`. The plan itself said *next unused is 523*, which was true when it was written and four hundred numbers stale by the time it was built; these are taken from the tail of the sequence instead, and none of the plan's own numbering survives. **945** the non-converged Davidson fit, **946** the cooldown's home, **947** the mail-failure surface — **all three answered by Ed 2026-08-27** in the orientation session while batch Q built: **945 (a)** adopt and record the flag on the receipt, with the cap-height test beside it (SPEC §4.2, R-051; build filed as plan-queue backlog 172); **946 (a)** an operator env knob now, a birth-time engine parameter when a second kind of room exists (SPEC §4.2 and the settings table, R-052; no build); **947 (c)** — against the recommendation — the founder's ✉️ row *and* every member as a news card, never the invitee (SURFACE E34; build filed as backlog 173). All three deleted from the Backlog section above. The plan's fourth open question was about Q512 (a ramp whose start equals its close) and needs no number of its own, since 512 already has one. Two things the pass found and did **not** number, because a finding is a backlog entry: `probBeats` returns exactly 1.0 on a well-separated pair at 400 judgments through `normalCdf` saturating, with the posterior variance perfectly healthy, so it clears any bar §4.2 can state; and a judgment arriving at a race that resolved a moment earlier is refused with *candidate c1 is not in a live race*, which names an engine id at somebody who pressed a button (found by `npm run soak`, two or three presses per run at six to ten concurrent seats).

**540–582 were spec pass 1** (2026-08-22), asked in the pass artifact and answered by Ed the same day; all folded — SPEC.md v0.65 §9 rebuilt to the governance normal form, `SURFACE.md` born with the event matrix, `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` born with the history, `npm run spec-check` in CI. Closed by it: **54** (§8.5 now says the narrow thing), **324** (the observer sentence struck; the metrics feed parked with 42). Its two follow-ups were done the same day: **583** — the engine did count the applicant's derived preference toward their own admit race's floor; fixed in engine-core as the rule E implies (a suspended author's derived preference is not a mover), stated in SPEC §8.2 and R-025; **584** — a judgment now closes its card and files as ⏳, the tab reopening it (session.js). **585–619 were released** when pass 1 closed.

**585–624 were spec pass 2** (2026-08-22), asked in the pass artifact and answered by Ed the same evening; all folded — SURFACE.md §6–§9 (the marks alphabet and the rail, the wallets and holds, the founding order and the band, the card kinds, the commit row and the composer), STYLE.md §8 (the card copy rules), the two notes files marked superseded (587), `spec-check` extended to the new tables and the founding walk (586 c), and the code findings and answered questions built as their own commits (602–609, 611; 605, 612–615, 618–623). **610 was released.**


**626–630 were the delegated-question blocking rule and what the work beside it turned up** (2026-08-22). **626** and **627** are answered above and folded; **628–630** are open. Correction to pass 2's own record: finding **D15** was written up as no-drift — *SPEC's "a delegated question on any setting blocks the start" is enforced by the module* — and it was not; it was drift, found and fixed under 626. The pass-2 extract that carried D15 is deleted (Q582 a), so the correction lives here, where the pass's record now is. **632–633 are the glyph rename of 2026-08-22** (the threshold 🌡️, the ramp 🪜, removal 🥾) and what it turned up; both open above. **634** was raised the same day, from Ed’s question about the difference between 👤 and ✍️; open above. **635–637 were the 👤/✍️ re-framing**, answered by Ed in the same conversation before they were put to him (2026-08-22): **a rename, no functionality change** — ✍️ *Can Members Make Anonymous Proposals?*, 👤 *Are Anonymous Proposers Revealed?*, both rung sets and every route untouched — and, agreeing with the recommendation, **the two swap places** so the gate is asked before its follow-up (`ORDER`, the `privacy` section, SURFACE §8, the golden walk). **636** never arose: with 👤 keeping three rungs it is never moot, so nothing is hidden or inert. The two strings the rename did not reach are **638** above.

**639–641 were the ✒️/🛡️ grant clauses** (2026-08-22), raised by Ed — *I don't like the clauses "The Founder holds the pen on _ settings." … these clauses only exist to give "Your Pen" and "Your Shield" news a home.* He is right twice over: the count is already stated per setting by `holderLine` (*The Founder may amend this at will*) **and** in the second person on the ✒️/🛡️ wallet tooltips (*You can change 16 settings yourself, without asking anybody*), so the clause was the third statement of it, in the voice that needed it least, and the one that went stale whenever a power was laid down. **639** answered **(a) the Founded line**: the two grants move their tabs onto *Founded by [avatar] [name] 👑*, which stands in the opening run, carries no power tabs of its own — every settings clause wears ✒️ and 🛡️ for **its own** powers, so hanging a grant on one would put two ✒️ tabs meaning different things in one stack — and already wears 👑, the mark that means *holds either power*. The dividend is that the crown stops being a tooltip and becomes the way in to what it means. Rejected: the you-row (no sentence owed, since ✋🖼️🪪🎩 already write none, but the founding stops running in single file at steps 4–5 and it makes a five-tab pile on a row with the Q308 crowding residual); the Proposals preamble (the best fit for what the powers *mean*, since its last sentence is the baseline they deviate from, but it puts the founder's first task after the save at the bottom of the constitution); and keeping the paragraphs blank in place (no order cost, but two permanently sentence-less clauses). **640** answered **(a)**: the Founded line **stands from the save** and *gains* its moment at the 🍾 press, which narrows F16 rather than breaking it — what is unknown before the start is the moment, not the founding, and the same reasoning made an untitled document read *Untitled* rather than leave a gap. **641** answered **(a)**: 🏛️ Your Voice **keeps its clause**, since *Each member holds one voice in every constitutional question* is not restated anywhere and so earns its paragraph by Ed's own test. **642–644 are the three calls the build made**, open above: what a member is told about the founder's powers now the clauses are gone, *Founded by Anonymous* before ✋ is answered, and 🌍 arriving above the Founded line.

**645–648 are the founder's own tasks** (2026-08-22), raised by Ed's live walk — *I'm never offered the "Your Name" and "Your Picture" tasks … I am never offered the "delegate to members" tasks either.* Both open above with his answers: **645** the two gaps (✋/🖼️ declared settled by `hydrateS` without ever being asked; the founder's `ans-*` cards starved by 🍾 counting itself as an outstanding task), answered — the answers arrive at the end, Q408's rule kept — and **646** the 🎩 re-tick wipe, answered *fix it in this pass*. **647–648** are the two module divergences the trace turned up and are **filed, not built**: the displayed electorate is not the one that resolves the question, and the readiness readout counts answers from people who have left.

**649 is the free-typing fix's residual** (2026-08-22), raised by Ed's live walk — *when the document has begun, as a founder, I can still write freely in the text area, and my edits don't create amendments.* Two holes found and shut in one commit: the charter's `beforeinput` let a host-caret keystroke through to the browser (the bug Ed hit), and the module's `begin` never waited on the confirmed text (§9.0b), so a wire-level start could wedge a document with no text and no way to propose one; `#prose` also ships read-only now, and `npm run journey` asserts the begun state and types on the host. What the refusal left open was the confirmed-**empty** text, filed as 649 and **answered by Ed the same night as (a)**: an empty document renders one empty clause keyed to engine line 0, wearing a grey invitation, so the first keystroke opens the editing card like any other. Built 2026-08-23 — and it found a second truth on the way: the engine holds an empty document as **zero** lines, so the proposal is an insert at [0, 0), and every hunk is now clamped to the line count the engine actually holds. `npm run journey -- --empty-text` walks it.

**650–654 are the dead-click nudge** (2026-08-23), raised by Ed — *users in the birth phase don't always realise they should be clicking on tasks in the rail, as they have just started using the product and we are teaching them new behaviour* — and reframed by him the same evening into something better than the intermittent jiggle he first asked for: *how about a settleLift nudge on the "Title" queue-card when someone clicks over where the "Untitled" helper text is? That's the most common behaviour, because they expect to be able to edit it directly.* The mechanism is confirmed in the code, and it is worse than *not noticed*: `#doctitle` is a bare `<h1>` with no `data-card` and nothing listening above it, so **clicking "Untitled" does nothing at all**. The tester Ed watched reached for the obvious door, got no response, and went looking for the nearest thing that would accept typing — the prose column underneath. **650** answered **(a)**: the 🪶 tab in the gutter and the 🪶 rail entry beat **together**, so the beat teaches that the tab, the rail entry and the card are one thing rather than merely pointing at a location. **651** answered: the idle jiggle is **dropped** — every motion on this surface is a consequence (a clause fades because it was born, an entry lifts because it was pressed, the pulse beats because somebody acted), an ambient jiggle would be the first thing on the surface to move for no reason, and the birth has no clock to ride anyway, the 4s poll being live-mode only. **652** answered: the nudge **never opens the card**, however many times they click — it stays a teacher, which is C1/F10's whole point. **653** answered: the trigger is **any dead click in the document during the birth**, not the heading as a special case. **654** is open above.

**655–673 are the mobile plan's decisions** (2026-08-23), raised in `design/MOBILE.md` §5 with a recommendation each and **all answered by Ed the same day** as multiple choice. As recommended: 655 (breakpoint 900), 657 (the rail becomes a pinned dock — Q420 reversed), 658 (the fold triangle visible under `hover: none` only), 659 (`--border-strong`, surface-wide), 661 (two-tap recorded as a SURFACE §7.2 column and a §3 exception), 662 (pseudo-element hit areas), 664 (`walk-lib.mjs`), 665 (a `walks` CI job, advisory a week then gating), 666 (Chromium first, WebKit once green), 667 (`?v=<sha>` rewrite), 668 (silent reload when quiet), 669 (a manifest per document), 671 (the 🔔 card), 673 (`push_subscriptions` now). Otherwise: **656** — *no proposing in mobile view so no real need to see sockets*, so the sockets are hidden on narrow in v1 and the topbar stays one row (a narrow exception to W11 that ends when proposing arrives); **660** — 15px prose on narrow; **663** — the birth is **supported** on a phone (*it is just doing three cards, and the mobile experience must be able to let you interact with cards*), so v1 is read + judge + the birth; **670** — the `web-push` package rather than hand-rolled crypto; **672** — push carries **asks only**, never owed OKs. The 2026-08-20 questions **419–429** were answered through them, and directly for 422 (no queued judging), 423 (**no pulse in the mobile app**) and 429 (**no budget**); each stays above until its rule is built into SURFACE.md.

**674–682 are the phase ladder** (2026-08-23), raised by Ed — *it would be good to have a test harness that with repeated button clicks could go from {fresh document} to {start of the constitution} to {begin with a variety of constitutional settings} to {halfway through a session with a long document with a load of active amendments} to {a session that closes in 5 minutes} to {closed session with comments}*. **674–678 are his answers**, given the same day: **674** the ladder walks a **live** document, not the fixture, since the fixture is what every live-only bug this project has hit was invisible to; the fixture stays as it is for design work. **675** one document **evolves**, a rung per press, rather than a menu that builds a fresh document at each phase — so the phase boundaries are watched rather than skipped. **676** the constitution is **seeded random, generated from the catalogue itself** rather than from named presets, so a setting added next month is randomised the day it is added and the surface is fuzzed against combinations nobody hand-picked; the seed shows in the bar and can be typed back. **677** it is a **test** as well as an instrument: a walk asserts the surface at each rung, not in CI for now. **678** — the correction that mattered — **rung 3 stands immediately before 🍾 is pressed**, not after it, because the start is the most interesting transition on the surface and a stagehand that spends that press hides it; and the session carries **~20 members and at least 30 live proposals of different kinds**, which is the first time the rail's fit cap, `fitStacks` and the tab stacks meet a roster that size on a live document. **679–680 are the two production defects the design review turned up**, each fixed on its own before the ladder: the minute tick has no per-document guard, so one document that throws stops the clock for every document after it in insertion order, once a minute, for ever; and mail needs refusing at the mailer for RFC 2606 reserved TLDs, which is how the ladder's cast is silenced in a way that survives a restart, a later tick and the close, where a build-time flag survives none of them. **681–682 were the build's own findings**, both real and both fixed. **681** — *reading a document is a write.* Presence stamps `now` (§9.5a), so merely looking at a ladder document pinned its log to the present and the next rung had no past left to write into; since the bar reloads after every press, that happened between every pair of them, and the elapsed session the whole instrument exists to show was impossible. A ladder document's clock is the ladder's now: the view path skips presence for it, dev-only, inside the label. **682** — *🎩 records the act, not the value*, found by the ladder on its first outing and the sharpest kind of find, because the comment directly above the line describes this exact bug class and had fixed only the begun case. Pre-🍾 the surface kept 🎩's settledness in `S.seen` alone, which no reload rebuilds, so **a founder who answered *am I a member?* and reloaded was asked again — and since 🎩 blocks the founding order, the whole constitution below it was withheld again with it.** Measured by the walk: acknowledging the pen used to release seven band tabs and no cork, and now releases twenty and offers 🍾. The module folds `membershipSet` from the events the log already carried; `setConvenorMembership`'s no-op short circuit was the other half, since a founder who was already a member and answered *member* emitted nothing at all.

**Open from the build, for Ed by number.** **683** — the ✒️ grant's OK lives in `localStorage`, and it is `blocksOrder`'s one blocking grant, so rung 3 hands the founder a document whose constitution is withheld until they press OK on their pen. That is the founding order working and it is pleasant to watch, but it means rung 3 is not quite *immediately before 🍾* as Q678 asked: **(a)** leave it, the grants being real news worth meeting; **(b)** have the bar seed the acknowledgement in `localStorage` so rung 3 is exactly the cork and nothing else. Recommend (a). **684** asked what *n still to confirm or answer* counted, and is **closed by the note's removal** (plan 15, 2026-08-25 — see 783–784 below): the count is gone from all three of its states, so there is nothing left to count.

**685–722 are claimed for the card audit** (2026-08-23) — the pass that reads every decision card on all four surfaces against `design/STYLE.md` at once, through Ed's three lenses (helper text · spacing and positioning · buttons), with `design/tools/card-audit.mjs` as the kept instrument and `design/spec-pass/card-audit.html` as the questions artifact. Claimed before any of it is written, per the two-sessions rule: the block the plan named (683–720) had been spent by the phase-ladder session between the plan and the build, which is the failure the rule exists to prevent. Whatever the pass does not spend is released when it folds. Next unused number was **723**, claimed below.

**723–731 are the documentation refactor** (2026-08-23) — the third extraction from `CLAUDE.md`, and the first one with an admission rule and a checker behind it. **The plan that ordered this work named 685–693, and every one of those numbers was already spent** by the card audit above between the plan's writing and its build: the same collision, on the same day, for the third time. Renumbered here rather than in chat, per the rule. The map, so the plan can still be read against the work: **723** claim the block · **724** split the glossary into names and gotchas · **725** reduce every name entry to name + job + pointers · **726** salvage and delete the two superseded notes files · **727** the README accuracy pass · **728** the PRODUCTION.md reconciliation · **729** the operational-docs fixes, with **729a** the six duplicated passages, **729b** the `NOTES.md` files and the two undocumented scripts, **729c** MOBILE.md's discipline as the house style · **730** the `spec-check` extension (pointer resolution, entry kinds, section shape) · **731** the admission rule written into `CLAUDE.md`. Scope was set by Ed's answers of 2026-08-23, quoted in the plan: the session-loaded set, the outward-facing docs and the plan documents are in; the reasoning archives are not restructured, though salvage writes into `DECISIONS.md`; gotchas stay, in a section of their own; and there is no byte budget, because a budget would press on exactly the gotchas that earn their place. Next unused number was **732**, claimed below. **One dangling pointer this leaves, noted here rather than by editing spent items:** 467, 502 and 504 above send a reader to `design/session-view.notes.md` for the reasoning behind Q221, Q224–243 and the session-view UX decisions. That file and `design/setup.notes.md` were deleted under 726; both are whole in git at `aba22fd`, and `design/DECISIONS.md` says how to read them.
**732–735 are the 🖼️ Your Picture card** (2026-08-23) — the review, the resize, the repicker and the upload (Ed), built the same day. **Claimed first as 685–688 and renumbered here: the same collision for the fourth time in two days, and the first one no second session caused.** The claim was written at 03:12 and the build then stopped on a question it could not answer alone, so it sat on a branch for four hours where nothing else could see it, and the card audit and the documentation refactor took 685–731 in the meantime. The rule above says to claim a number by writing it into this file; what this adds is that **a claim only counts once it is on the branch the next writer will read** — work that parks takes its claim out of sight with it. The commits carrying this work, `8f79ddd` and `e0ca3d1`, still say 685–688 in their messages; the numbers below are the ones that count. **732 — a full in-page emoji picker**: search box plus the standard Unicode categories, lazy-rendered, reserved and taken glyphs greyed **in place** with their reason. Built on a second pass, once the run was given the fetch it had blocked for: `design/emoji-test.txt` is committed beside `design/emoji-data.js`, which `npm run emoji-data` generates from it — 1906 fully-qualified glyphs in nine categories with their CLDR names, skin tones and the Component group dropped, and a tonable flag read off the file rather than guessed, since People & Body also holds 🦴. The curated six rows are gone. Three things the build learned. **Only the open category may be drawn**: this body is rebuilt by every `render()` and the live page polls every four seconds, so 1906 buttons is not a thing to build on a keystroke — the grid is one group at a time, 169 of them on the category it opens on. **The search field cannot go through the page's own render**, which would rebuild the input under the caret, so the grid refreshes in place and the category and the query live beside `FACE_TONE`, never stored. And **`faceBtn` needed no change at all**, which is the pleasant part: it already greyed both refusals with their words, so offering the whole of Unicode simply means the surface's own marks are in the grid now, refusing themselves where a member meets them. **733 — the initials are not a section**: the card is *pick an emoji* → *upload an image* → *Currently* with Remove, and what you get with neither is a sentence rather than a row, because initials are not a third answer. Built, and `pictureBody` is now shared with the applicant's 🖼️, which had hand-rolled its own picker and carried no uploader at all. **734 — the reserved-furniture rule stays**, and **Q632 closes as its (a)**: 🍾 🥂 📨 join `SURFACE_EMOJI` in both `design/setup.js` and `packages/server/src/faces.ts`, byte-identical as the parity test requires. The build-time scan Q632 offered as (b) is still not taken. With it, the grounds `c0`–`c5` and drawn marks `m0`–`m2` are **deleted rather than tolerated**, on Ed's standing note the same day — *we're still in alpha, you can mostly ignore backwards compatibility, there are no real documents yet* — so `validPicture` refuses them and SPEC §9.0c names the two shapes for the first time (v0.68, R-046). **735 — downscale and re-encode client-side**, the data URI staying in the event log: 256×256, JPEG at 0.8, `imageOrientation: 'from-image'` so a portrait photograph is not delivered on its side by the very re-encode that strips its EXIF. Three defects closed at once — a phone photo that previewed and then failed on Save, GPS coordinates landing in a hash-chained log that holds answers in plaintext, and unbounded log growth; `LIMITS.picture` comes down from 150,000 to 40,000, set for what we now encode. **The review's own findings**, all fixed with it: the 7px emoji was a specificity accident no rule of that shape could win, so the emoji stopped being an `.av` at all; the *right aligned* half was `.cpv.founded .av` overriding `display` to `inline-flex`, where `place-items`' justify half means nothing — **which was miscentring the initials too**; and the emoji path was **entirely unprobed**, every fixture picture being the empty string, which one 🦉 on the founding cast and a 🖼️ step in the setup-probe now fix. Next unused number: **736**.

**736–739 are the CLAUDE.md eviction rule** (2026-08-23) — raised in chat when Ed asked why the file is so large. Claimed here before being put to him, per the rule that a number is claimed in this file and nowhere else. The measurement behind them: 104 KB standing after the 723–731 refactor of the same day, of which the glossary is 52 KB over 68 entries (771 bytes an entry, against an admission rule that says *a name, a one-line job, and pointers*) and the gotchas 33 KB over 38. Three extractions are visible in the git history — 186→126 KB at spec pass 2, 149→104 KB at the refactor — with regrowth of 3–5 KB a session, so the file returns to its pre-extraction size in about a fortnight. **736** — an eviction rule to sit beside the admission rule: a gotcha whose mistake is now caught by a named guard (`spec-check`, `journey`, `slug-walk`, a golden-log test, a parity test) reduces to one line naming the failure and the guard, its post-mortem moving to `design/DECISIONS.md`; 8 of the 38 gotchas name such a guard today and account for ~13 KB. **737** — a per-entry byte cap asserted by `spec-check`, the checker having gained shape enforcement under 730 but no size enforcement; the ten largest glossary entries are 25 KB between them, a quarter of the file. **738** — whether the design-system and server material moves to directory-scoped `CLAUDE.md` files loaded only when that subtree is touched. **739** — a finding, fixed rather than asked: the *Sizing: `span.av.emoji` at 18px/38px* gotcha was stale on the day it was measured, the class having been retired by 732's move to `.emojiface`, and the `identity-cards` entry beside it already said that fix could not work. Deleted. Next unused number: **740**, claimed below.
**Built 2026-08-24, and here is what each number came to.** **736 and 737 are folded** — both rules are written into `CLAUDE.md`'s *What goes in this file* beside the four admission questions, and both are asserted by `npm run spec-check`: every glossary bullet at any depth names something and declares its kind, no glossary entry runs past 400 characters, and no gotcha that names a guard runs past 400 either. The checker was extended and committed **before** any prose moved, so the worklist was derived rather than asserted — it printed 84 disagreements (47 bullets naming nothing, 30 entries over the cap, 7 guarded gotchas over it) and the extraction is those 84 worked to green. `CLAUDE.md` went **107,836 → 85,952 bytes**; nothing was deleted, and the two record sections in `design/DECISIONS.md` — *The guarded gotchas, lifted 2026-08-24 (Q736)* and *The glossary prose, lifted 2026-08-24 (Q737)* — hold every removed bullet verbatim. **738 was declined by Ed**: directory-scoped `CLAUDE.md` files are not the answer, because these gotchas are organised by failure mode rather than by path and a scoped file is not loaded when you are reasoning about a subsystem rather than editing in it. It is closed, not open. The build's own follow-up is **742**, above.

**740–741 are ⏱️'s *I set it* filling its own fields** (2026-08-24) — Ed, 2026-08-23: *when you choose "I set it" on the proposal rate, it should have defaults — 4 to start with, up to a maximum of 6, an additional every 10 minutes.* Claimed here before the numbers are used anywhere, per the rule. **740 — CLOSED, the ruling and its four answers.** The founding has carried no defaults anywhere since Q511, so picking *I set it* met three empty boxes and a dark ✓; the standard rate is now written into them by the pick. Ed's four answers: **(1) when** — on clicking the rung, not on card open, so the card still *arrives* with nothing answered and SPEC §9.0a stands unamended; **(2) commit** — filled is answered, the ✓ wakes and the founder may commit unchanged; **(3) copy** — silent, no *suggested* note; **(4) scope** — only the delegated fallbacks follow, ⏩'s `SEED` and the post-start composer keep their numbers. This is a **clarification of SURFACE F6 rather than an exception to it**: F6's *typing into a rung's own field is choosing that rung* can never fire first here, because `.pick .inner` is `display: none` until the rung is on (`design/setup.css:148`), so the radio click is the card's only entrance and hanging the fill there is complete. ⏱️ does **not** join 🪜 in F6's exceptions line. Two guards the design rests on: only **empty** fields are filled, so a founder who typed 12 keeps it; and only on the **transition** into the rung, so a field deliberately cleared is not refilled behind them. Reasoning in `design/DECISIONS.md`, including why the consent-slider's anchoring ban does not reach a founder-set value. **741 — open**, below.

**741** is open, above: the four different numbers that stand in for the drip when nothing is set. Next unused number: **742**, claimed below.

**742–772 are the five queued plans of 2026-08-24**, claimed here in one act before any of them is built. Every one of the five was written against a tree in which **740** was the next free number, and 740–741 were spent by ⏱️'s *I set it* between the writing and the queueing — the fifth collision in three days, and the first one a batch-wide pre-flight has been able to prevent rather than record. The numbers below are the ones that count; where a plan's own text names 740-something, read it through this map. Blocks are generous by instruction; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.**

**742–743 are `CLAUDE.md`'s eviction rule and entry cap** (plan 08). The rule itself is **736–739**, claimed above on 2026-08-23 and still standing — nothing there is re-claimed. What this block is for is the work's own two loose ends: **742** — the follow-up the plan raises and declines to build, a checker that asserts a `[symbol]` entry's *claims* against the source rather than merely that the symbol resolves, since the branch reset showed `CLAUDE.md` can go stale against its own code in one git operation and not only by drift; **743** — spare, for what the extraction turns up while it works the checker's list to green. **Folded 2026-08-24: 742 is open above** (a checker that asserts a `[symbol]` entry's claims rather than merely that the symbol resolves), and **743 is released unspent** — the extraction turned up nothing that needed a number of its own, the eight overshoots left after the first pass being wording rather than decisions.

**744–747 are 📄 Text and 🍾 arriving before it can be pressed** (plan 09). **744** — the 📄 card gains a value (the block-count line), a caret in the prose column on open, a commit that names what it commits, and loses its 🗑️ and its false *members can start proposing* sentence. **745** — 🍾 is not served until it can be pressed: hidden while `readiness()` is false or 🏛️ is unacknowledged, which is SURFACE **F9** applied to the one card that broke it. **746** — the finding filed rather than fixed: `canPropose(member)` in `@draft/constitution` has no `constitutedT` guard, so the module and the surface disagree about the rule the same plan deletes as false copy. **747** — spare. **Built 2026-08-25.** **744 is folded**: the card is `.why` → the count line as its value → the *Or leave it blank* note → the commit row; opening it puts the caret at the end of `#prose` (C1, and the only thing on the surface that says the column may be typed in); the commit says **Set the text** / **Start it empty**; the bin is gone, `cardKeys()` having been empty for it all along, and Y20 lists it; the false *members can start proposing* sentence and the literal `AA` in the grey writing invitation (STYLE finding 690) are both gone. **745 is folded**, with **one correction to the plan worth recording**: the plan's voice half, `!mustAct(card('grant-voice'))`, deadlocks the commonest founding of all. With nothing delegated, `tasksFor` hangs 🏛️ off ⚖️ Judging, which is hidden until 🍾 — so the voice is never *offered* before the start, while `mustAct` is true from the founder's arrival, the card being open and unacknowledged. `founding-walk` stopped dead at *commit text* with an empty rail. The predicate asks instead whether the voice is being **served** (`visible(card(voiceHost()))`), which is the rail's own enumeration and covers the clerk case the plan reasoned about by the same clause. This is **Q645's deadlock in a new place**, and the rule already written above `otherTasksLeft`: *what a question hands you cannot be the reason to withhold the question*. `spec-check` now asserts 🍾's hide, its readiness half and its voice half as three separate claims. **746 is open above.** **747 is released unspent.** Next unused number is still **773**, below.

**748–752 are the Proposals run merged into one clause** (plan 10). **748** — one paragraph, four tabs (🍾 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️) in one `chipsFor` stack. **749** — the clause is composed from per-decision fragments rather than four stub paragraphs, so the duplication between 🍾's preamble and 💡's and 🏛️'s sentences goes. **750** — 🏛️'s clause is pinned to the merged paragraph; only the news still travels with the question that needed it. **751** — the two rulings this reverses: **Q641** (🏛️ keeps its clause — it does not, the fact *is* restated) and **Q605**, narrowed to the rail. **752** — the `anchorOf`/`hostParaOf` fix and the build's own findings. **Built 2026-08-25** (the plan's own text names 740–744; read it through this block). **748–751 are folded**: the Proposals section opens with one paragraph, `data-para="begin"`, carrying 🍾 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ in one `chipsFor` stack; `proposalsClause()` composes three sentences from per-decision fragments, so 💡 keeps both its `canPropose()` branches and ⚖️ its two, and the duplication with the preamble goes; all four card heads are that clause (F15); 🏛️'s sentence is its trailing fragment; SURFACE gains **Y23** and `spec-check` asserts `PROPOSAL_CHIPS` against the band prose's glyph run. The two copy calls stand, both one-string reverts: *when the **document** begins* (the preamble said *session*, which is engine vocabulary) and a singular sentence 2 (*each member holding one voice* will not scan against a plural subject). **752 is spent on two things.** The hole: `anchorOf` resolved a rail entry through `tabFor`, and an **inert** chip carries no `data-tab` — so a non-front stacked tab's entry fell through to `#titlepara`. Nothing had ever reached that branch (🪜 and the power tabs have no rail entries); this group is the first where three of four do. Every chip now carries `data-chip` and `hostParaOf` consults it, a fix for every pile. And the finding: **🏛️ is deliberately *not* in `SEC.rate.keys`**, which the plan's edit list asked for. `secLive()` makes a section live when one of its keys is visible, and 🏛️ is visible from ORDER row 22 (or earlier via `early()`), so admitting it would pull the whole Proposals section forward and offer its news before 🍾 — the F9 situation Q745 closed the day before. The clause is pinned by `chipsFor` instead, `spec-check` takes an explicit `hosts` override, and one predicate — `voiceInStack()` — decides everywhere whether 🏛️ is a fragment or a paragraph of its own; it keeps its own paragraph in the one state with no preamble to join. `tasksFor` is untouched, which is what keeps Q605's news travelling, and the group's note counts the pinned tab back. Reasoning in `design/DECISIONS.md`. Nothing released — 748–752 are all spent.

**753–760 are the members list** (plan 11). **753** — the *(nobody else here yet)* placeholder moves **above** your row, where it is a statement about the list rather than a caption on you. **754** — the list is written for the founder alone: a member reader wears the founder chip, is listed twice, and never sees the founder. **755** — typing into 📧 writes the viewer's address onto the **founder's** roster row. **756** — `meRow()` is renamed `founderRow()`, the misnomer that caused both. **757** — the stale `setup.css` comments and the duplicated `.memrow.meRow` rule, with the 42px clearance moving off the row and onto `.memlist`. **758** — a clerk founder is **not** in the Members list. **759** — a clerk's ✋🖼️📧 identity tabs ride the Founded line, under *your identity tabs ride wherever you appear in the document*. **760** — the applicant's fake you-row, fixed by derivation from 759 rather than by a ruling; the one item Ed has not ruled on directly. **Built 2026-08-25** (the plan's own text names 740–747; read it through this block — the batch pre-flight had already claimed 753–760 here, so there was nothing left to claim and the build folded instead). **753–760 are all folded.** The list is now split by `showMe` — `S.roster.indexOf(viewerRow())`, `-1` for the applicant and stranger stubs — rather than by `!p.founder`, and the chips are per row on both halves, so 754, 758 and 760 fall out of one predicate; `inList` is `members()`'s own `p.role !== 'clerk'`. **756 turned out to answer 755 by itself**: the `meRow()` line under 📧 is **deleted**, not re-pointed, because `S.myemail`'s accessor has always written `viewerRow().e` — the line was redundant for the founder and a clobber of the founder's row for everybody else. **757 needed a measurement the plan did not anticipate**: `margin-top` on `.memlist` cannot work. The list is the first in-flow child of a `.cpara` with no top border or padding, so the margin collapses out to the paragraph and carries the absolutely-positioned 🪪 pile down with it — clearance measured at **−3px**, an overlap, from a rule that reads as if it adds 42. It is `padding-top: 42px` on `.constsec .cpara .memlist`. Measured after, 🪪-pile-bottom to ✋-pile-top: founder alone **35px**, founder with others **8px**, a member reading a full room **3px**; the clerk's five-tab Founded-line pile is 46px with 25px above and 45.5px below. Two items of the plan's documentation list were stale: `CLAUDE.md:134`'s exception sentence had left for `design/DECISIONS.md`'s two **verbatim archives** (Q725 and Q737), which are not edited — the live homes are STYLE.md §3 and SURFACE §9's card table — and `founding-golden` records no roster paragraph, so it matched unchanged. `npm run ladder` walks it: the membership check counts `.memrow:not(.nobody)` and reads 20 rows at both `ready` and `session`, and the Q754 reproduction was made on the **live** document the ladder leaves standing, through the bar's own seat control — seated as a cast member the list opens *Moss Whitlow you* with no founder chip, the founder stands in the tail wearing it, and no name appears twice; seated as the founder there is one row and exactly one founder chip on the list. **One finding that is not this plan's**, recorded here because the walk is how it was found: `ready: 🍾 is offered and not yet pressed` fails, and it fails identically at the commit *before* this one, so it is a regression of **Q745** rather than of the members list — plan 09 taught 🍾 to hide until `readiness()` and the 🏛️ acknowledgement are both in, and the ladder's rung 3 lands with the voice unacknowledged, so the rung no longer stops one press short of the cork as Q678 requires. Unspent numbers: none — 753–760 are all spent.

**761–772 are the three card defects** (plan 12). **761** — the 0px seam between a card's value radios and the delegate rung, on all twelve delegable cards and loudest on ⏱️, which has one `.pick` and so no internal rhythm to read it against. **762** — `card-audit` rule **P3**, stacked radio rows are spaced on the scale. **763** — one fact, one home: how blind answers aggregate has two homes and both print into one body, verbatim on ⏱️ 👥 🥾; the catalogue record gains `meanwhile` and `theyDecide` stops taking a per-setting note. **764** — the `why` cut, to subject plus one consequence. **765** — STYLE **T36**, so the next one is caught. **766** — `card-audit` rules **C1**/**C2** and a seventh `delegated` walk, the state the existing six pass straight through. **767** — the surface merge: one card, glyph 👤, **Anonymous Proposals**, two axes underneath. **768** — ✍️ narrows to `['nobody','each']`, which removes the 👤 `anonymous` + ✍️ `everybody` contradiction structurally; closes **Q634** and **Q638(i)**. **769** — a signature is always named; `closingSignatures()` stops reading a setting. **770** — the per-proposal sign control, without which `each` stays offerable and inert. **771** — the two `role="radiogroup"` elements writing one state key, which `chosenOf` and arrow-key roving cannot see across. **772** — spare, for the instrument's own findings.

**Built 2026-08-25** (the plan's own text names 740–751; read it through the map above). **761, 762 and 771 are folded**: `.choice.delegrung` and `.choice + .eyebrow.fieldlab` take `margin-top: var(--s4)` in `setup.css` — one number for one relationship, with 👥's inline style retired onto it — the delegate rung stops claiming a `role="radiogroup"` of its own, since it writes the same state key as the values above it, and `chosenOf` bridges the pair so a delegated card opens with the keyboard on the answer that stands. `card-audit` rule **P5**: the plan asks for `P3`, which is taken by the tab-growth rule, so the positioning lens's next free number was used. **763–766 are folded**: the note argument leaves `theyDecide` and the card record gains `meanwhile`, so the aggregation rule is stated once (🤝 gains the `rule` it never had; 👁️'s stops carrying a sentence its own body states); thirteen `.why` lines cut to subject plus one consequence, the longest 393 → 145 and ⏱️'s 139 → 68; STYLE **T36** and **T37**; `card-audit` rules **T36** and **H4** (the plan asks for `C1`/`C2`, and the audit's letters already mean *a STYLE rule* and *the helper-text lens*, where C1–C16 are SURFACE's card lifecycle) and a seventh walk, **`delegated`**. All three new rules were shown red on the pre-fix page first — P5 on 13 cards, T36 on 🥾 and 👥, H4 on 9 — and are green on all seven walks after. **768 and 769 are folded**: ✍️ narrows to `['nobody', 'each']`, which deletes the 👤 `anonymous` + ✍️ `everybody` contradiction structurally rather than guarding it, and `closingSignatures()` stops reading a setting and stops anonymising — **Q634 and Q638 (i) close with them**. SPEC v0.69 (§3.5a's *Who may sign*, the §9.7.1 row) and `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` **R-047**.

**767 is folded, and it is a ruling rather than the plan's design.** The build stopped and asked: the plan wanted one card standing for **two settings**, and this surface derives a card's composer from the open card's own key (`S.draft.k` is `S.open`, and a motion payload names one setting), its ✒️/🛡️ tabs from `PW_KEYS` filtered through the card list, its owed-OK sync from `midOf(c.k)` and its blind-answer card from `CARDS.filter(c => ANSWER[c.k])` — so a two-setting card needed a draft carrying a setting distinct from its card, power tabs acting on two settings, and an answer card committing two. The plan also asked for two things that cannot both hold, `ANSWER` committing both axes in one act and a `deps: ['authorship']` that makes the second of them throw. **Ed, 2026-08-25: *I think five rungs is fine; there is definitely a ladder going from anonymous forever to open forever*** — so the answer is **one setting**, not one card over two. ✍️ leaves the catalogue entirely and 👤 takes a ladder of five, most-private-first: `anonymous` · `anonymousElective` · `sealed` · `sealedElective` · `public`. The ruling withdraws the independent-resolution concern that had kept the axes apart: the order is **total**, so one consent resolution down one ladder hands nobody an exposure they did not accept. One setting means one card (👤 **Anonymous Proposals**), one clause, one rail entry, one motion, one pair of powers, one answer body — and every derivation above keeps working untouched, which is what makes this the cheap answer as well as the right one. The catalogue is **18 settings**, the judge-gates **six**. SPEC v0.70 (§3.5a rewritten, the §9.7.1 rows, §9.0b's gate list), SURFACE §2 E1 · §4 · §8's rows and band, `R-047`, the golden log and the founding walk re-frozen. **`founding-v0.jsonl` was re-frozen too, and that is a decision worth recording**: it had been the literal bytes of 2026-08-20, and a log naming a setting this build no longer has cannot be replayed while a hash-chained file cannot be patched — so `freeze.ts` now derives it from the same walk with `schemaVersion` stripped, and the test asserts it chains to *today's* hash, which is a stronger statement of *absent means 1* than the drifted inequality it replaces.

**770 is folded** (plan-queue 59, 2026-08-27, entries 30 and 31): the per-proposal sign control in the editing card under the two elective rungs, `authorVisible` as the one reveal rule in engine-core, `propose-text` as the narrow gate, and Ed's **honour** ruling — a proposal keeps the disclosure rung it was made under, stamped on the candidate and shown in the record beside the rung that stands. SPEC v0.78 (§3.5a), R-050, SURFACE K28. **Q634's surviving (i) closes with it.** **772** — spare, still.

**The batch was re-flown on 2026-08-25 with plans 08 and 09 built, and no new numbers were taken.** Three of the five remain queued — **10** the Proposals merge, **11** the members list, **12** the three card defects — and the blocks claimed for them above (**748–752**, **753–760**, **761–772**) stand exactly as written; the re-run re-checked them against the tail rather than claiming a second time, because a block claimed for a plan that has not yet built is still that plan's. Two of the five have now spent theirs: 742–743 (plan 08) and 744–747 (plan 09), both folded above. What the re-run adds is in `BATCH.md` rather than here — chiefly that plan 09's build put `beginOffered()` and its three `spec-check` assertions in the way of plan 10's *delete `voiceHost`* edit, and that plan 08's extraction moved the sentence plan 11 cites as `CLAUDE.md:134`. **Whatever each block does not spend is released when that plan folds.**

**773–810 are the eight queued plans of 2026-08-25**, claimed here in one act by the batch pre-flight before any of them is built — the batch is Ed's docs.vote founding walk of the same day, backlog entries 10, 13, 14, 16, 33, 34, 35 and 36. Every plan was written against the tree at `8099b0d`, where **773** was already the next free number, so nothing below renumbers anything a plan says. Blocks are generous by instruction; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.**

**773–778 were the founding never running out of queue cards before 🍾** (plan 13, built 2026-08-25 — **773–777 spent, 778 open below**). Ed: *before begin, there shouldn't be a situation where I don't see any queue-cards.* **773** — the invariant, now SURFACE **F18** and asserted in `journey-walk`'s founding loop: an empty rail before Begin with 🍾 unoffered is a failure, not a `break`, and it prints the page's own `window.__founding` readout rather than the symptom. Its second half is 🍾's **last-resort door** — `beginOffered` returns `nothingElseServed()` when `readiness()` is not ready — which restores the ruling the 🪪 card already carried (Ed, 2026-08-21: *whether a document can begin with nobody in it is 🍾's question, and 🍾 has a readout that names what it is waiting for*) and that Q745 had hidden in exactly the state it was written for. **774** — the walk variants, both built: `--delegate-all` hands over every delegable setting as it is served, `--proposals-first` takes 🍾 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ the moment any is offered. `--delegate-all` reproduced Ed's dead end on the first run. **775** — **the cause, and it was Ed's own guess** (*maybe the 🌡️ and 🪜 didn't generate a task*): 🪜 rode only the *set* branch of 🌡️'s commit, below the delegate return, and 🪜 is in neither `ORDER` nor any section's keys — so a delegated 🌡️ left `mustAct('pace')` true for ever with nothing on the surface able to ask for it, `otherTasksLeft()` counted it, `ansDue()` was false, and **not one of the eleven delegated questions ever came back**. Fixed twice over: the instance (the rider stands above the delegate return, so the commit answers 🪜 whichever branch it takes) and the class (`otherTasksLeft` counts only what `railable` says the rail could offer). **776** — the founder-member's delegated questions, served in `ORDER`, asserted as a *sequence* by the `--delegate-all` walk. **777** — the pre-Begin non-empty rail check, on `constitution` and on both sides of the pen's OK at `ready`.

**778 is open — the surface offers a blind question §9.0a says is not answerable.** Found by the `--delegate-all` walk on its first full run. 🌡️ and 🪜 are `deps: ['ending']`, and `session.ts:1129` refuses an answer to a dependent while its dependency is still collecting: hand over both ⏰ and 🌡️ in a room of one and the rail serves 🌡️'s question with a live ✓ that the module answers `'bar' waits on 'ending' (§9.0a)`. The cascade behind it then stalls, since `ansWaveReady` waits on an answer that cannot be given. Two halves to decide, and the second is the real question: (a) `visible(ans-*)` should also require the host setting's **dependencies** to be settled — but the page holds no copy of the catalogue's `deps`, so this needs a mirrored list and a `spec-check` assertion tying it to `CATALOGUE`; and (b) whether a question that cannot yet be asked **pauses** the cascade or is **looked through** by it — Q416's *if I have to do all of them anyway, they should cascade* argues for looking through, but looking through a question you will be asked later changes the order the founder meets them in, which is the one thing `ORDER` exists to fix. The walk holds ⏰ back meanwhile, with the reason at `journey-walk.mjs`'s `wantDelegate`.

**779–782 were the quorum blind task's dead slider** (plan 14), **built and spent 2026-08-25**. Ed: *clicking on the slider control doesn't appear to do anything, so I can't set my quorum answer.* The plan's own reading — an `input` that never reaches the handler, or a value written to a key the readout does not read — was wrong on both counts and measured to be wrong: the handler fired and the readout followed it the whole time the control was broken. **779** — **two causes, neither of them the event.** `slider()` tested `v === null` for *unset*, and a question nobody has answered is `undefined` (a member's answers object starts empty; the live hydration `continue`s past a null `myAnswer`), so on every real document the unset branch never fired: `value="undefined"` is invalid, so the browser parked the thumb at the midpoint under a readout reading `undefined% — NaN of 5` — **a suggested value, painted**, which is the one thing a blind collection exists not to show, and a click on a thumb where it already sits fires no `input` at all. And the `input` handler called `render()` on every step, which rebuilds the card — so the range input holding the pointer capture was destroyed on the first move of a drag and the thumb froze. Fixed as *unset means no value* and *nothing rebuilds under a press*: the gesture repaints the readout in place (`syncSlider`) with the rail and the commit around it, and the full render waits for `change`. **The no-defaults rule is not touched** — the fix restores it, since before it the control shipped an anchor. **780** — the readout and the ✓ follow: 👥 reads in the founder's chosen form (`55% — 2 of 2` for a share) and the ✓ files the answer, which `slider-walk` asserts. **781** — **every blind slider, because the cause is in `slider()` itself**: 🌡️'s `ans-bar` was broken identically and is fixed by the same two lines; both are walked. **782** — spent on the guard: `npm run slider-walk`, which drags with a real pointer, because nothing else here does. *(Two findings for plan 13's block, out of scope here: with the founder alone on the roster `ansDue()` is false until nothing else is outstanding, so 🌡️'s answer card is unreachable unless somebody has been invited — the walk invites; and delegating both 🌡️ and 👥 empties the rail before 🍾 altogether, which is Q773/Q776's subject.)*

**783–785 were the constitution group's note** (plan 15), **built 2026-08-25 — 783–784 spent, 785 released unused.** Ed: *there's some helper text in the document that is like 'n still to confirm or answer', please remove this* — then the *all confirmed* state and the pre-save feathers as well. **783** — the removal, all three states: the `constitution` group's `note` is gone from `design/session-view.html`, and with it the birth's fixed count of the three feathers and the 🏛️ re-add that existed only to feed it. It was the only `note` any group defined, so both of `setup.js`'s renderers stop emitting `.pilen` rather than emitting an empty one, the two counts that fed them (`wants` over the sections, `left` over a plain pile) go with it, and `.setrow .pilen` leaves `setup.css`. **784** — **closes Q684**, which asked what the note counted; the answer is that it is gone. The only measured consequence is the note's own line box: `card-audit` at 1280 and 1600 moves everything below the band's last section up **19.5px** and changes nothing else, no card's strings or geometry among them. **785** was spare for what `.pilen` and `wants` left behind, and they left nothing behind — released.

**786–789 were the founder's answer-as-a-member clause** (plan 16), **built 2026-08-25 — 786–788 spent, 789 released unused.** Ed: *whether the founder specifically has answered their member question doesn't matter enough to have a whole clause, it's just part of the {x/y members have answered this delegated question}.* **786** — `decisionLine` writes nothing for an `ans-*` card: the branch returns `''` rather than being deleted, since without it the card falls through to the room-setting branch below and states a rule it does not own. **787** — where the tab stands instead: the ✒️/🛡️ precedent (Q639), riding the host setting's own pile, so no paragraph is emitted — `chipsFor` gains the answer card and the `ctx.tasksFor` paragraph seam drops it, the same seam 🏛️ uses under Q750.
