The pile is the value's tab, your question and the two power tabs; `stackOrder` puts the question in front while it is asking and the watcher in front once it is answered, and the clause's own count goes *0 of 2* → *1 of 2* as the only thing that changes. **788** — **`hostParaOf` needed nothing**, which is the pleasant part: every chip has carried `data-chip` whether or not it is clickable since Q752, so the rail entry finds its host's paragraph by the lookup the Proposals stack already forced. Nor did the raw `tasksFor` change — the rail, `railable`, `servedCards`, `foldedSec` and F18's `otherTasksLeft` all read it, so what is *owed* is untouched and only where the tab is drawn moves. Verified headless from a blank arrival to a delegated 👥: no `ans-quorum` paragraph and no *answered as a member* sentence in either state, the tab in the quorum pile, the card opening on the quorum clause. `founding-golden` unchanged (its walk delegates nothing), `card-audit` identical at both sizes but for wall-clock stamps, `slider-walk` green. **789** was spare for the 📧-precedent branch, which was not taken — released. **`SURFACE.md`'s outside-`ORDER` list** carries the new rule; the reasoning is in `design/DECISIONS.md`. *(The setup-probe was already failing against its frozen reference at `e9686a6`, on the same 136 diffs before and after this change — pre-existing, and not this plan's to re-freeze.)*

**790–796 are pen and shield laid down before 🍾** (plan 17). Ed: *it should be possible as soon as the Pen and Shield are accepted, after each setting is set for the first time* — and *I think they should take effect at begin.* **790** — the module rule: the text-confirmed guard becomes *the setting has a value*. **791** — whether **assent** takes the same test, which today may go from creation on an unset setting and is the first half of delegating through the two power tabs. **792** — how a pre-start release is recorded so that `replay` stays bit-identical and the golden log re-derives. **793** — the surface: `blockUGive`, its note, and the *released, from the start* wording. **794** — SPEC §9.7's amended sentence, the version bump and the `R-` entry. **795** — the `founding-walk` step that releases 👥's pen. **796** — spare.

**797–801 are 📄 Text as an acknowledgement** (plan 18), **built 2026-08-25 — 797–800 spent, 801 released unused.** Ed: *the action on the main 📄 tab is an acknowledgement, and it's the ✒️ tab under 📄 that allows the founder to relinquish it.* The plan asked for no new numbers, the batch pre-flight having claimed this block on 2026-08-24; nothing was renumbered. **797** — `BODY.text` states the fact rather than an instruction to write: *The document begins from whatever stands in the column below, and from then it changes by proposal — whoever holds the pen.*, and the note becomes *Write it below, or leave it blank. The column is yours until you press OK…*, which keeps *Or leave it blank* in substance. **798** — **the commit is now OK**, the word the gates and the grants use, replacing the ✒️ that Q744 had left titled *Set the text* / *Start it empty*. The two tooltip branches survive, because the empty one is Q744's warning to a founder who has not realised they may type — restated as facts, *The document begins from what is in the column* / *The document begins empty*. The pen falls away by itself: `holdWallet` keys the hold on the ✒️ in the button's own text, so the flight, the spend-preview and `mayPen()`'s gate all retire with the glyph. `data-confirm` is deliberately kept — the act is still `confirmStartingText`, and a `data-ok` here would be clicked by `journey-walk`'s own OK branch before it reached the step that types into the column. **799** — **the clause, checked and found not to exist.** `doc` is a rail-only section and `textAnchor` draws 📄's pile beside the charter heading, so `DECIDED.text` renders nowhere on any surface; `headFor` returns null for 📄 because its clause is the document itself (F15). It is updated anyway, into a pre-start tense and a post-start one, so the sentence and the card cannot drift apart if 📄 is ever given a paragraph. `proposalsClause` does not say it — the preamble is about who may propose *rules*. **800** — SURFACE gains a 📄 row in §9's card table and names 📄 in §9.1's OK row; `CLAUDE.md`'s `Text` entry says the task is an acknowledgement and points at §9; the reasoning is in `design/DECISIONS.md`. **801** was spare for the rail entry's wording, and there was none to change: the entry is the card title alone (a task you have to do carries no subtitle), and *Text* names the subject rather than an act — released. **The ladder's `ready` rung names no 📄 step**, so nothing moved there either. Verified: the four gates, `spec-check`, `founding-golden` **unchanged** (📄 has no clause, so the golden's clause list never held it) with the walk committing 📄 at *OK*, `card-audit` at 1280 showing the same 20 findings with three more sightings in one bucket — B5, *a button label is --t-ui*, which the grants' OK was already in — and the setup-probe on the same 136 pre-existing diffs before and after. `journey` types at 📄 and commits it *(OK)*; the run then fails downstream at *pre-start editor still live* / *no editing card*, **identically at the parent commit**, so that is pre-existing and not this plan's.

**802–805 were the one measure** (plan 19), **built 2026-08-25 — all four spent.** Ed: *the text area for 'Text' is wider than the constitution text; they should be the same.* The plan asked for no new numbers, the batch pre-flight having claimed this block on 2026-08-24; nothing was renumbered. **802** — **the measurement, and it is not 3px.** At 1600 the two columns differed by the plan's predicted 1.5px a side (the rows' unexplained `+ 3px`); at 1280 the prose ran **24px further out on each side**, because a `.setrow` carries its tab gutter *inside* its `max-width` and `.prose` carried none — so wherever the document column is narrower than the measure the row loses a gutter and the prose does not, and that is the width Ed was looking at. Before: prose text `383…905`, clause text `407…881` at 1280; `502.13…1105.88` against `500.63…1107.38` at 1600. After: `407…881` and `502.13…1105.88` — **0px on both edges at both widths**, title, `.cptext`, `.setrow`, `.prose` and an open `.sugg`'s head all on the same two numbers. **803** — the property pair, `--measure` and `--measure-gutter`, declared on `.doc` and used by `.setrow`, `.prose`, `.doctitle` and `.sugg`: max-width is the measure plus two gutters, padding is one gutter, so the prose now carries the tab column the rows always did and the two agree by construction. The `+ 3px` is gone; nothing in `design/DECISIONS.md` ever explained it, and it was the whole of the wide-window disagreement. **804** — **yes, `ch` resolved differently, and worse than the plan guessed.** It is not that the two columns use different fonts; it is that an *unregistered* custom property substitutes its tokens and resolves `ch` against whoever **uses** it, so `.doctitle`'s own `max-width: 70ch`, read at `--t-lead`, was a column ~150px wider than the prose's and never bound at all — the row it sits in was the only thing holding the title. `--measure` is registered with `@property … syntax: "<length>"`, which resolves `70ch` once, at `.doc`, at document type, and inherits it as a length. Pinning it in `rem` was the alternative and was rejected: `ch` is the right unit for a measure and only the resolution site was wrong. **805** — spent on the last edge, which the plan did not name: `.doc .anch` gave back its left padding with a negative margin and not its right, so a charter paragraph wrapped **8px** earlier than a constitution clause even once the columns agreed. It takes `margin-right: calc(-1 * var(--s2))` now, on the same selector as `.clausehead .headclause` so the two boxes stay identical. **Measured consequences**: `card-audit` at 1600 is finding-for-finding identical to the parent commit, and at 1280 two findings **improve** — P2 *the glyph moves 26px sideways when the card opens* → 2px, and P3 *its left edge moves 18px* → −6px, which are the 1600 values, so the tab-you-click-does-not-move promise now behaves the same at both widths instead of losing a gutter at the narrow one. `founding-golden` unchanged. The session-probe goes from IDENTICAL to 838 diffs and the setup-probe from 136 to 188, **every added diff a `geo.*` one and no counts or html hashes among them** — the frozen references hold the old measure, and re-freezing them is not this plan's to do.

**806–810 are the hairline and the title from the first open** (plan 20). Ed: *immediately after the birth, when my named document opens for the first time, the Text area should already have the title and hairline above it.* **806** — splitting `textAnchor`'s gate: the hairline and the repeated title are facts about the document, the 📄 pile is the task's. **807** — **the two-titles question**, which is the plan's real call: `#doctitle` at the head of the page and `#dochead` beneath the constitution are two titles *by decision* (Ed, 2026-08-19, `setup.css:428–431` — *the title standing again as the charter's own heading*), so the plan's *one title, one place* would reverse a standing ruling and would take `anchorOf`'s fallback, the cork flight's landing target and `ladder-walk`'s name check with it. **808** — `.cpara.textanchor` holding its layout with an empty pile. **809** — the grey invitation now sitting under a title. **810** — spare.

It lands in **plan 23**'s territory — see `BATCH.md`.

No spec text: it is the same electorate 647 has just settled, stated once. **Owed, not built**, and it is 647's own commit; also **plan 23**'s territory.

The carve-outs are the ruling's substance: a checker that cries wolf on prose is worse than none. **Owed, not built**, and it is a `scripts/spec-check.mjs` change with no plan in this batch behind it.

**811–849 are the five queued plans of 2026-08-25**, claimed here in one act by the batch pre-flight before any of them is built — backlog entries 51, 56, 57 + 41, 60 and 50, all from Ed's docs.vote founding walk. Every plan was written against a tree at or near `92c5f2f`, where **811** was already the next free number, so nothing below renumbers anything a plan says. Blocks are generous by instruction; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.**

**811–818 are the invite door** (plan 21), **built 2026-08-25 — all eight spent.** Backlog 51: *I managed to invite one additional member … and further ones don't work; their names don't appear in the list, and they don't receive emails.* The plan asked for no new numbers, the batch pre-flight having claimed this block above; nothing was renumbered. **811** — a door that will not open says why: `invite`, `uninvite` and **both** `openMotion` presses (the ✏️ click and the 🏛️ assembly, which the plan named as one) keep the answer `api.cmd` already resolves with and write it under the control that tried, as `propose-text` writes `back.refusal`; a refused motion comes back as an unsent draft rather than an empty composer. One `.why.refusal` element per card (`S.doorErr`), retired by the next keystroke in place, with the module's trailing `(§…)` stripped — that pointer is this repository's vocabulary, not a member's (STYLE.md §8), and a page-side translation table for every refusal would be a second copy of the module's rules. **812** — one gate, and the module was the side that stayed: `membershipReserved()` narrows to `unilateral && !crownLapsed`, and `invite()` **calls it**, so the two cannot drift apart again. **813** — the shut-door sentence, which already named ✉️ and now says inviting is a proposal and why. **814** — Enter sends, and `[data-add]` is read from the card that was pressed (`data-setupcard` is the key the refusal is filed under). **815** — the empty-field and duplicate refusals, lowercased to match the store. **816** — `commitFor`'s `myHeldOut() !== c.k` exemption dropped; **it is unreachable today** — a card holding your live motion renders the motion, not a composer — so this is defence, not a fix, and the disabled state now states the module's rule instead of approximating it. **817** — the test: the plan said `session.ts:1120` has none anywhere, and **that is half right** — `founding.test.ts:344` covers the delegated case; the state that bit (pen laid down at 🍾, 🛡️ kept) had none, and the new case asserts all three states with both gates agreeing in each. **818** — spent on the walk: `journey-walk.mjs` invites twice from the single-name control (the second by Enter), asserts both rows and both dev-outbox mails, takes one deliberate case-variant duplicate and reads its sentence off the card, then post-🍾 lays the register pen down through 🤝's ✒️ tab and asserts the box is gone and the card names ✉️. Two findings inside it: **🪪 is a band tab and never a rail task** (always settled, having no value to settle), so the door is walked at the last moment before 🍾 rather than waited for; and **a closed pile's other chips are inert peeks with no `data-tab`**, so 🤝 is opened before its ✒️ tab can be. Verified: `packages/constitution` (216) and `packages/server` (57) suites, `spec-check`, `founding-golden`, `founding-walk`, `ladder`, and `journey` green end to end — and **red on the pre-fix page in exactly Ed's words**: one row, one mail, the second invitation gone without a trace, a 400 at the wire that the screen never mentioned, and the box still drawn after the pen had gone. The two probes sit on the same 838 / 205 diffs before and after (plan 19's measure, against frozen references). SURFACE gains **Y24** (the door's condition) and **Y25** (the refusal sentence) and its 🪪 / ✉️ rows point at them; `design/DECISIONS.md` and one guarded `CLAUDE.md` gotcha carry the rest.

**819–825 were the prose column live until 🍾** (plan 22, backlog 56), raised by Ed 2026-08-25 — *clicking 'OK' on 📄 locks the text from being edited; it should remain editable until Begin. It is an acknowledgement that the text will become the starting text, not a ✒️ for setting the starting text now* — and **all built the same day, none released**. **819** — `charterOn` keys on `constituted()`, so `#prose` stays displayed and `#charter` stays empty until the cork. **820** — the contentEditable test and the copy that goes with it (`BODY.text`'s retrospective branch, *until the document begins*). **821** — the write channel after the OK: a debounced `confirm-starting-text` rather than a stash, the stash route being shut by the confirm. **822** — the Begin handler's flush, which covers the debounce race and the fixture path, modelled on ⏩'s own. **823** — the two comment blocks that were the recorded reasoning for the old behaviour and now record its amendment. **824** — the `founding-walk` assertions (the column shown and editable after the OK; the line typed then, in the charter after 🍾) and the golden re-freeze for the one inserted step. **825** — the three further `charterOn()` readers the plan did not name: `tocLead`'s heading source and `afterToc`'s prose folds, both of which want *whichever column is on screen*, and the ✏️ wallet tooltip, where the gate was already redundant. Module, server and SPEC unchanged — §9.0b wants a confirmed decision, not content. Reasoning: `design/DECISIONS.md`.

**826–834 were Begin's reason, 🪪's remedy and 🏛️ served to a founder-member** (plan 28, backlog 57 + 41 in one), **built 2026-08-25 — 826–833 spent, 834 released.** Ed, founding alone: *I did all my open tasks and then got served Begin while being unable to action it … my guess is this is because I delegated things to the members and I'm the only member*, and *when I (as a founder-member) was granted 🏛️ I did not get a task. The 🏛️ tab was grey.* **The one-voice rule is working and was reported as a fault**, which is the whole shape of this: `maybeResolve` refuses to resolve a blind question on an electorate of one, and the surface could not say so. **826** — `readiness()` returns `holds` beside `waiting`, the same ids with a reason each, computed in the order `maybeResolve`'s own gates run so an unopened invitation reads `invitation-open` rather than `one-voice`. **Five reasons, not the plan's four**: `text-unconfirmed` is `waitingOn`'s own §9.0b prerequisite, which the plan did not name and which had to be given something. `waiting` is untouched — `begin`'s refusal and every existing reader want bare ids, and changing its shape would break all of them to save one caller. **827** — 🍾's card names both remedies and the closing line stops promising a wait that ends by itself; the counts stay and stop standing alone beside *1 of 1 have answered*. **828** — 🪪 stands as a task while the document waits on `one-voice`, its card leading with why. Derived and nothing else: no `S.okd` key, no `ACK_KEYS` entry, no news, nothing owed — so it is clear of F8, and it leaves the instant an invitation goes out or the setting is taken back. **829** — 🏛️'s pre-start host is 🪪 rather than ⚖️, which is hidden until 🍾 and so put the grant behind the very press it stands in front of. Only the enumeration moves; the tab stays pinned to the Proposals preamble (Q750) and the rail entry follows its own chip. It changes **every** founding by a founder-member, not only Ed's — 🏛️ now arrives after 👁️, its place in `ORDER` — and that is right for all of them. **830 was the real question and the answer is narrower than a door.** Serving 🪪 makes `nothingElseServed()` false in exactly the state F18's last-resort door was written for. Opening a second door on `readiness()` was tried and `journey-walk --delegate-all` killed it in one run: a question is `one-voice` from the moment it is handed over, so 🍾 jumped ahead of the ten questions the founder still owed as a member (*handed over 10, asked back 0*, Q776 undone). **The remedy is the dead end wearing a card, not the next task**, so it is excluded from `nothingElseServed` — and, for the same reason, from `otherTasksLeft`, where counting it would have withheld every delegated question from the first delegation onwards. That is Q775's shape for the third time, after 🍾 (Q645) and 🪜 (Q773). **831** — `owedUnservable` is read at every step of `journey-walk` and fails the run, rather than being printed for whoever comes to read the dump. **832 was real and narrower than the plan said**: `founding-walk.mjs --delegate=<key>` clicked the delegate rung and then filled every empty field, and typing into a rung's field chooses that rung — the fill already skipped `range`, but 🌡️ carries a `number` input, so `--delegate=bar`, the one reproduction of Ed's case, walked to a begun document with 🌡️ founder-held and blamed the page. **833** — `spec-check` pins the three one-line predicates a refactor could drop with everything still rendering (the door's exclusion, the remedy, `otherTasksLeft`'s exclusion), and the `founding-walk` golden was re-frozen for 🏛️'s move, which is the whole of that 16-difference diff. SURFACE gains **F19** (the reason, the remedy, and that the two are served together) and **F20** (a grant a founder-member holds is served before the start), with rows 11, 21 and 25 and F5 and F7 amended; `design/DECISIONS.md` and two guarded `CLAUDE.md` gotchas carry the rest. Verified green: `packages/constitution` suite, `spec-check`, `founding-golden`, `founding-walk` plain and `--delegate=bar`, and `journey` in all three moods. **834 is released.**

**835–841 were the sole member's own judgment and the 50% bar** (plan 24, backlog 60), **built 2026-08-25 — 835–839 spent, 840 raised in the backlog above, 841 released.** The block was claimed by the batch pre-flight on 2026-08-24 and was still free at the build, so nothing was renumbered. **The headline finding is that the engine was never the reason**: at E = 1, Q = 1, θ = ½ the floor is 1, the author's derived preference meets it, `judge` has no author exclusion, and `feed` serves the sole member the edge card — driven directly it returns `comparison, adopted` in one call. Both defects were readouts that had assumed a room bigger than one. **835** — the `mine` filter carries its premise as a condition (`&& E() > 1`): *a race holding only my own proposal is mine to withdraw, not to judge* is a rule about **other people**, and at E = 1 it silently became *no card, ever*. The withdrawal stays; at E = 1 the sole member gets both. **836** — the closeness span is floored at `MIN_CLOSENESS_SPAN`, the span of a 51% bar. **The choice is between the plan's two candidates and it went to the floor rather than to a θ = ½ special case**, because the special case would have been discontinuous in the wrong direction — 0.500 reading 60% where 0.501 reads 100% — whereas the floor is continuous and its blast radius is exactly nil: `max` picks the real span for every bar the surface can express above its minimum. Stated cost, in `design/DECISIONS.md`: at ½ the meter saturates on the derived preference alone, which is what 0.51 has always done. **837** — three vitest cases in `packages/engine-core/test/session.test.ts` (`a document of one`): the floor, an hour of `tick()`s emitting nothing before a judgment, the edge card served, `comparison, adopted` in the one call; then 0.50 against 0.51, and the untouched formula at 0.90. **838** — `spec-check`'s `checkSoloJudgment` pins the `E() > 1` condition as source, verified red on the pre-fix line. There is no walk at E = 1 anywhere and the plan said not to build one, so this predicate is the whole guard, and its failure is silent — an empty rail looks like a document at rest. **839** — SURFACE E19's Ask column, plus the `design/DECISIONS.md` section and two `CLAUDE.md` gotchas. **841 is released.** **835's exception overturned 2026-08-29, backlog 253** (plan 126, SPEC v0.94): an author is never served their own text against the incumbent at any E, so the sole member's self-judgment is gone and at E = 1 the proposal adopts on submission; `checkSoloJudgment` is now `checkAuthorNeverAsked`, asserting the exclusion in `bestPairFor` and `explorationCard` and the page's unconditional skip.

**842–849 are a setting that predates you** (plan 25). **842** — the ruling as a spec change: SPEC §9.0a and §9.6a, *had no say* time-indexed to arrival, grants excepted; **the version to bump to is v0.73**, 647 having taken v0.72 above. **843** — R-016's inheritance clause reversed in `design/SPEC-REASONING.md`, extending its own *a gate opening is the exception* sentence rather than contradicting it. **844** — `oweOnJoining` emits nothing, and the three R-016 tests invert. **845** — `constituted()` into the `news` disjunct, so no stale view can serve a pre-Begin ack. **846** — one press: the key held in flight until `api.cmd`'s promise resolves, **and** `syncOwedOks` made symmetric with `hydrateS`. **847** — SURFACE E5's audience column and `CLAUDE.md`'s two lines. **848** — the journey-walk extension with a second seat. **849** — spare.

**826–834 and 842–849 are re-queued, not re-numbered** (batch pre-flight, 2026-08-25). Plans 23 and 25 of the 2026-08-25 batch were started and abandoned before either wrote a line — `pq/23-begin-says-what-it-is-waiting-for-offers-the-rem` and `pq/25-a-setting-that-predates-you-is-what-the-document` are both empty against `main` — and the same two plans return as **plan 26** (Begin's reason, 🪪's remedy, 🏛️ served to a founder-member) and **plan 27** (a setting that predates you). Each **keeps the block already claimed for it above**: **826–834** are plan 26's, **842–849** are plan 27's. No new numbers are taken, because a block claimed for a plan and spent by nobody is still that plan's; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds**. One correction carried forward from that claim and worth restating, since plan 27's own text still says v0.71: the SPEC version to bump to at **842** is **v0.73** — SPEC.md now stands at v0.72.

**826–834 and 842–849 are re-queued a second time, and still not re-numbered** (batch pre-flight, 2026-08-25, against `5a82fce`). Plans 26 and 27 both came back *could not run* — an infrastructure failure, not a build that learned anything: `pq/26-begin-says-what-it-is-waiting-for-offers-the-rem` and `pq/27-a-setting-that-predates-you-is-what-the-document` are empty against `main`, as 23 and 25 were before them. The same two plans return as **plan 28** (Begin's reason, 🪪's remedy, 🏛️ served to a founder-member) and **plan 29** (a setting that predates you). Each **keeps the block claimed for it above**: **826–834** are plan 28's, **842–849** are plan 29's. No new numbers are taken — a block claimed for a plan and spent by nobody is still that plan's, and re-claiming would burn seventeen numbers to say the same thing three times; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.** Two corrections carried forward, both restated because each plan's own text still contradicts them: at **842** the SPEC version to bump to is **v0.73** (SPEC.md stands at v0.72, and plan 29 still says v0.71), and plan 29's list of documents to amend misses **`SPEC.md:341`** (§9.7½: *an admitted applicant inherits the constitution and is owed its acknowledgements like any late joiner*), which is the same reversed clause in a second place and would otherwise be left standing against §9.0a — spend **849**, the block's spare, on it. What else this pre-flight found is in `BATCH.md` rather than here, chiefly that **SURFACE row 25 and F5 now state 🍾's voice half as a rule** (*`readiness().ready` and 🏛️ not still being served*), with `spec-check` pinning it as source — so plan 28's **829** is a SURFACE amendment as well as a page change, and its **830** is the collision that decides whether **827**'s sentence is reachable at all.

**826–834 and 842–849 are re-queued a third time, and still not re-numbered** (batch pre-flight, 2026-08-25, against `2bae3fb`). `pq/26-begin-says-what-it-is-waiting-for-offers-the-rem` and `pq/27-a-setting-that-predates-you-is-what-the-document` are both empty against `main` — `git log main..<branch>` returns nothing for either — so neither plan has written a line, and `main` has not moved since the last claim except by that claim itself. The same two plans return as **plan 28** (Begin's reason, 🪪's remedy, 🏛️ served to a founder-member) and **plan 29** (a setting that predates you), and each **keeps the block claimed for it above**: **826–834** are plan 28's, **842–849** are plan 29's. No new numbers are taken; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.** The two corrections in the paragraph above stand unchanged and are not restated: at **842** the version to bump to is **v0.73**, and **849** is spent on `SPEC.md:341`. What this run adds, all of it bearing on numbers already claimed: **828 and 829 change the founder-alone rail**, which is exactly what `design/tools/founding-walk.golden.json` records, so the golden must be re-frozen deliberately (`node scripts/founding-golden.mjs --update`) and the re-freeze is **833**'s, beside the `spec-check` work — none of the three queuings of this plan had noticed the golden. **832 is narrower than the plan says**: `founding-walk.mjs`'s fill-every-empty already excludes `range` inputs, so the un-delegation it describes can only bite a rung with a `number` or `datetime-local` field, and which settings those are wants checking before the number is spent. **848 is cheaper than the plan says**: `journey-walk.mjs` has invited two guests pre-Begin since Q818, with `inviteFrom` and the dev-outbox read already written, so 848 buys the second browser context and that seat's assertions and nothing else. And both plans regenerate `design/constitution.js` by `npm run bundle`, a committed artifact neither mentions. The rest is in `BATCH.md`.

**842–849 were a setting that predates you** (plan 29, backlog 50), **built 2026-08-25 — all eight spent, none released.** Ed's ruling: *a setting that predates you is simply what the document says; a power handed to you is news addressed to you*, and the symptom beside it — *I click a queue-card, click OK, then click the queue-card again, then click OK, then the queue card goes.* **The two are separate bugs reported as one experience**, and the second is the more general: a press that reads its own answer out of the view it was made against. **842** — SPEC bumped to **v0.73** (the plan's own text still said v0.71); §9.0a's inheritance bullet says *owed nothing*, and §9.6a time-indexes *had no say* to arrival, excluding the later joiner beside the removed and the never-arrived. **843** — R-016 reversed in `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` by extending its own *a gate opening is the exception* sentence: the general rule underneath both is that **what a member is owed is what happened while they were here**, which leaves the lapsed member owed (they were here) and the grant addressed to whoever it is handed to (a thing that happened). **844** — `oweOnJoining` and all three of its callers deleted rather than emptied, with `settledConstitutionalIds`, its only reader, behind them; every other owing path was already post-arrival and already skipped `arrivedAtT === null`, so the emission was the whole mechanism. The three R-016 tests invert, and `founding.test.ts`'s now also asserts the *next* amendment does reach the arrival — the half of the rule that stays. **845** — `constituted()` into the `news` disjunct. It was a real second bug, not only belt and braces: the `!constituted()` guard three lines above governs the ternary chain and the `||` escapes it, so a member was served the whole settled constitution **before 🍾**, in a document whose founder may still re-set every clause of it, against C8 and F8. **846** — one press, and it took both halves. A pressed key is held **in flight** until `api.cmd`'s promise resolves (which is after the refresh it triggers), and `syncOwedOks` gained the direction it never had — it adds when the module does not owe, as `hydrateS` always has. Symmetry alone flashes the task back for one round trip; the in-flight set alone loses the acknowledgement wherever `hydrateS` does not run. **Only the `set-` half is symmetric**: a delegated question resolving owes no OK in the module at all, so module silence is no evidence about `res-` and reading it as evidence would retire the resolution news. **847** — SURFACE E5's audience column, `CLAUDE.md`'s band rule and its lapsed-member gotcha (the test is now one question — *were you here when it was set?*), plus one new guarded gotcha for the double press. **848** — `journey-walk.mjs` grows a second browser context, which nothing in the project had ever driven: the dev ladder presses every owed OK for every member at once, and `founding-walk.mjs` and both probes are the founder alone in the fixture where no command is sent. An invited guest follows their own invitation pre-🍾 and is asked what is served to them; the founder amends 🌍 at the wire; the guest presses OK once and the task must leave, survive a poll, and survive a reload. **The reload is the tell** — before the fix it fails at the press and passes after the reload, verified by re-running against the pre-fix page. A second pre-Begin assertion was added that the plan did not ask for and the walk immediately earned: **🍾 is not a second chance to serve what predates you**, which is how the stale-module run was caught. `window.__founding` carries `okd` beside `owed`, because *the task is still there* names a symptom and cannot say which of the two mechanisms broke. **849** — `SPEC.md:341`, §9.7½'s admitted applicant, the same reversed clause in a second place. Reasoning: `design/DECISIONS.md`. Expected and not a regression: `npm run ladder` now reports fewer *owed acknowledgements given*, `oweOnJoining` having been most of what it counted.

**850–890 are the four queued plans of 2026-08-26**, claimed here in one act by the batch pre-flight before any of them is built — backlog entries 42, 15, 39, 40, 37 and 52, batch B2b. All four plans were written against `1754de4`, where **850** was already the next free number, so nothing below renumbers anything a plan says. Blocks are generous by instruction; **whatever is unspent is released when each plan folds.**

**850–857 were names and faces reaching every seat** (plan 30, backlog 42), **built 2026-08-26 — all eight spent, none released.** **850** — `syncFromCs` reconciles `row.n`/`row.pic` from `rec.name`/`rec.picture` on **every** sync, in a `syncIdentity` helper, rather than only on the push that created the row. Its one exception is **your own row**, which ✋ and 🖼️ write onto as you type and as you pick: the module's answer must not land back over the one you are making, which is the rule the value hydration already keeps for the open card. **851** — the `mid2 === FOUNDER` skip is gone entirely, not only for identity: the row is found by `mid` and the founder's is seeded `mid: 'founder'`, the same literal the server mints, so nothing was ever pushed twice and the only thing the skip protected was the removal branch — which now guards on `row.founder` and says so. **852** — the founder's `pic`, which no live path had ever written; it needed no boot line, `render()` calling `syncFromCs` first and the founder's row therefore being reconciled before it is drawn. **853** — the clerk **is** reached, by the second source: `cs.convenorRecord()`, consulted exactly when the founder's id is absent from `memberRecords()`, which is what 🎩 does to a clerk. §9.7 X15 makes it not optional. **854** — Q278's surface half about *faces* is answered above, at Q278 itself. **855** — `CLAUDE.md`'s `me` entry rewritten: your own face, not the only one, citing §9.0c and §3.5a. **856** — the second seat, and **it already existed**: `journey-walk.mjs:317–400` has carried a full guest rig since Q848, so the plan's *build a second browser context in `ladder-walk.mjs`* was stale and the check is three assertions bolted onto the seat that was there. Both directions, both through the **4s poll** and not a reload, a reload rebuilding the rows from the module and passing either way. **857** — the spare, spent on what the fix turned up: the mockup's own `invite()` pushes a Title-Cased courtesy name for somebody who has not arrived to choose one, and a name only the page holds is now a name the next sync overwrites with the address — so it is told to the module in the same act. Live documents never take that branch. Reasoning: `design/DECISIONS.md`.

**858–864 are the topbar face row** (plan 31, backlog 15) — **all seven spent, built 2026-08-26.** Ed: *where we currently say "n in the room" in the topbar we should show a row / stack of user avatars.* **858** — `#faces` beside `#quorum` in the `room` span, written by `renderClock` from `members().filter(m => m.in)`, capped at five with a `+n`. It is **keyed, not diffed**: `renderClock` compares `textContent` and beats on its own 30-second interval out of step with `render()`, and a row of faces is markup, so the key is the ids, pictures and names it drew plus the overflow — otherwise the stack is rebuilt twice a minute under whatever the pointer is doing, the 4s poll deferring under a press where the clock tick does not. **859** — the viewer is not in the row, their face being `me` at the end of the same bar, so **a room of one draws no stack**; and a stranger and an applicant are drawn none of it, the door being a deliberately narrow statement (Q508) rather than a place to answer *who is here*. **860** — the count parts company with the row: *quorum k of n* survives verbatim from 🍾 and is the **engine's** electorate, the row and its `+n` are the **register's** arrived rows, and the pre-Begin head count — the sentence the row replaces — survives as the row's `title`. **861** — the navbar's height is unmoved and measured both ways: **48.5px bar, 26px `.stats`**, with the row and without it, empty and full, the stack stating 22px against `me`'s 26. **862** — the overlap CSS, with the `.emojiface` twin (17px/22px, the assembly's shape) beside the `.av` rule since an emoji is not a disc (Q735), and `pic-measure` gaining the site so the next change here is measured; the ground is `background-color` and never the `background` shorthand, which would out-specify `.av.photo`'s `cover` and paint a 256px upload at natural size in a 22px hole. **863** — SURFACE **C16** rewritten: the row, the cap, what a face may say, and which count is whose. **864** — the `+n` is a bare count in `--t-micro` muted, no tooltip of names (that nicety is still not taken), and Q278 is closed by 42's own argument — presence is membership, which is public (§9.0c), where authorship is sealed (§3.5a) and direction is never shown (§3.5). Reasoning in `design/DECISIONS.md`; the guard is `npm run journey`'s second seat, each of the two seats seeing exactly the other and never itself. Nothing released.

**865–876 are the leaving rules before the roster, the roster's subheadings, and Proposals above Decisions** (plan 32, backlog 39 and 40) — **eleven spent, built 2026-08-26; 876 released.** Ed: *💤 and 🥾 sections should be above the Members heading … separate subheadings under Members for applicants and invitees*, and *Proposals above Decisions*. **865** — 💤 and 🥾 stand above `roster` in `ORDER`, and their clauses above `<h2>Members</h2>` in `SEC[0].body`. **866** — the cascade shift, walked rather than reasoned about: `orderReady`, `blocksOrder` and `ansWaveReady` all read `ORDER`, and `voiceHost()`'s pre-start fallback is `'roster'`, so the register — and F19's 🪪 remedy with it — is now served two constitutional questions later; `founding-walk.mjs --delegate-all` shows an empty rail at exactly one step, the last, so **F18 holds**. **867** — Q617 (a) is **half kept, half reversed**, and its comment block rewritten in place: 🤝 still leads the section, but *a register is the fact, and those are rules about it* is true of a finished document and false of the founding, where the register is the founder's own name and every rule under it will be in force before the first invitation. **868** — an **Invitees** subheading under the arrived rows, and the *invited* chip retired, the heading saying what it said. **869** — Applicants under *Members* is a **second view**, not a move: `memApplicantsHtml()` reads `view.applicants` for names and faces and the still-asking only, while `rosterClause()`/`liveApplicantsHtml()` keeps the **admit judgment** and its controls untouched — moving that block would put controls in the prose, copying them would put one race in front of a member twice, and an open 🪪 replaces the paragraph the list stands in, so they are never both on screen. (It is also plan 33's edit site, and 33 builds after this one.) **870** — each subheading stands only where it has rows, and *(nobody else here yet)* now counts the **arrived** rows alone, so it stands above your row while an invitation is out. **871** — the `rate` section object above `deciding` in `SEC`, and 💡 ⚖️ 🏛️ ⏱️ 🤖 above the Decisions and Anonymity keys in `ORDER`; 🍾 stays last in the file though its clause opens the section, 📄 last but one. **872** — *Proposals reads fourth* (Ed, 2026-08-21) reversed and its comment block rewritten: that ruling's objection was a whole section arriving at once, which Q748's four-tab preamble and `ORDER`'s single file had already dissolved. **873** — `SURFACE.md` §8's table renumbered, with the chained *hidden until* column rewritten for every row the reorder passed — 💤 now until `mypic`, 🪪 until `removal`, ⏱️ until `roster`, ⏰ until `machines`, 📄 until `judgments`; `spec-check`'s `checkOrder` sees only the key and section columns, so that column is on the author. **874** — §8's band paragraph reordered, and the two rules stated once each as **F21** (the three groups) and **F22** (the two reorders), rather than twice in prose. **875** — `founding-walk.golden.json` re-frozen and the diff read before it was accepted: a **pure permutation**, 55 steps before and after, same step names, same clause strings, same rail entries, new order. **876** — released: `card-audit` had nothing to say about the subheadings (264 cards, 23 findings, all of them the standing set).

**877–890 are 🪪 Membership, where inviting is the proposal** (plan 33, backlog 37 and 52). Ed: *the Invite / Send invitations buttons should be ✒️ (or ✏️ as appropriate), as each invite is the proposal. The main action bottom-right is just ✔️. It should be possible to resend emails to invited members with a 📨 button.* **877** — **the question the build must put before it writes the branch**: an invite motion's route is **constitutional** (`session.ts:1487`), so its price is 🏛️ and not ✏️, and the 🪪 card's own copy already says so in words — *everybody answers it, and one refusal keeps them out*. Ed's note says ✏️; the mechanism says 🏛️. **878** — the second question beside it: ✉️ *Invite a Member* already **is** the post-start proposal door (`:1520`, `hide: () => !motionsLive()`), and B2's Q813 deliberately replaced the 🪪 composer with a sentence pointing at it — so backlog 52 may be asking for a branch that exists under another tab. **879** — whatever survives 877 and 878 as the proposal path, `open-motion` with a `kind:'invite'` payload being already whitelisted and email-checked at `commands.ts:261–274`. **880** — the mark on the send button: ✒️ where `directInvite()` holds, the route's own glyph otherwise, drawn with `motionCommitHtml`'s alphabet rather than a second one. **881** — the *Several at once* textarea sharing that path. **882** — the foot: `commitGlyph`'s roster branch becomes TICK, routed to `data-close`, the confirm handler having no-opped roster since the register began committing row by row. **883** — `card-audit` gaining roster's foot as a ✔️/close *main action* case. **884** — the geometry of a glyph on a body button, which is not a `.commitrow` button and is not measured by B1/B5 as one. **885** — the 📨 resend affordance, per invited-not-arrived row, never on an arrived member. **886** — the module command behind it. **887** — the server half, re-driving `member-invited` through `loginLink()` (`server.ts:163–177`). **888** — **voiding the prior token**, which needs either a per-member current-invite `tokenHash` or a `revokeForMember` sweep, and which Q519 already ruled on for the founder's own link (*they all stay live*) — so the answer here must either follow that ruling or say why an invitation differs. **889** — the `journey-walk` additions, on top of the invite door it already drives. **890** — spare, for `SURFACE.md`'s 🪪 rows and `CLAUDE.md`'s 🪪 line.

**891–893 are the stated address carrying `/d/`** (plan 34, backlog 70), **built 2026-08-26 — all three spent.** Ed: *the Link clause says "The document lives at docs.vote/[slug]" whereas in fact it lives at docs.vote/d/[slug].* **The plan named no number**: the pre-flight's 850–890 above covers plans 30–33 only, so this block is taken from the tail rather than folded. **891** — every *stated* address gains its route segment: the Link clause and its composer sibling, the birth mail's prose in `mailer.ts` and its mockup twin in `setup.js`'s `MAILS.verify`, 📍's three verdict sentences, its field prefix, the mockup-inbox line, and the three short-form value maps (`VALUE`, the motion *to* half, `WORDS`). Nothing clickable changed — every link the server builds was already `/d/`, and the bug was only ever in copy a member reads and cannot follow. **892** — one home for it, `docAddr(slug)` in the page's own script, the eleven inlined copies being how they came to be wrong together; the composer's strip takes the `d/` as optional so a draft composed before this still round-trips into the field. **893** — the site the plan did not name and the fix would have broken: `linkify` turns a stated address in the constitution into a real anchor, and its pattern would have stopped at `docs.vote/d`. It takes the segment, optionally, so a member's own sentence naming a bare address still links. **Left undone, deliberately:** `design/tools/founding-walk.golden.json` holds the old clause in 52 steps and needs `node scripts/founding-golden.mjs --update`; that file is plan 32's to re-freeze (Q875) and it was building alongside this one, so racing it would have made a conflict for somebody. If 32 froze first, the re-freeze has to run again. Nothing released.

**894–896 are the open-join link admitting the visitor** (plan 35, backlog 73), **built 2026-08-26 — all three spent.** Ed: on a document set so *anyone with the link may join*, a visitor enters their email, gets a mail, clicks the link — and is not made a member. **The plan named no number**, the pre-flight's 850–890 above covering plans 30–33 only, so this block is taken from the tail like 891–893. **894** — `/auth/apply` (`packages/server/src/server.ts`) reads the live join policy and, under `open`, calls `submitApplication`, which auto-admits with no motion in the way. The rung means **auto-admit, no consent** — the card says *Anyone with the link becomes a member the moment they open it* and the module's own comment says *anyone with the link joins on arrival* — but the handler only ever ran `verifyApplication`, so the visitor stopped at status `verified` and stayed there. Nothing else could move them: the page renders an `open` applicant **zero** rail entries, on the belief that landing already admitted them, so `open` membership was unreachable through any UI. The module's `open` test passes because it calls `submitApplication` directly; the gap was always the HTTP seam. **895** — the cookie the landing hands back is now a **member's** (`memberIdByEmail(doc.cs, rec.email)`), with the `app:` cookie kept as the fallback for every road that lands short of membership — which is what makes the front end need no change at all, the empty `open` applicant branch simply never firing. **896** — the guard: a `packages/server/test/server.test.ts` case that begins an `open` document, knocks at the door, consumes the mailed link and asserts an arrived member, an `admitted` applicant record and a member view — with no `submit-application` posted, no admit motion and no admit race. It fails on the pre-fix handler at *expected undefined to be defined*. **Two plan adjustments, recorded rather than asked:** the plan named `ConstitutionSession.joinPolicy()`, which is **private**, so the setting is read through the server's own existing idiom (`settingState('applications').value`, as at `server.ts:864` and `:1000`) — the same value by another road; and its `design/session-view.html` line numbers had shifted under plans 30, 31 and 34, the copy it quotes now standing at `:4180` and `:4618` and needing no change either way. **Left alone as the plan says:** the `apply` and `proposed` rungs, which correctly land verify-only and keep their later submit and their motion; the empty `open` applicant rail; and any UI submit task for `open`, arrival being the joining. Nothing released.

**897–899 are a proposal on a heading opening its card** (plan 36, backlog 74), **built 2026-08-26 — all three spent.** Ed: *when there is a proposal to edit a heading in the document, the decision-card for that proposal won't open.* **The plan named no number**, the batch pre-flight's 850–890 covering plans 30–33 only, so this block is taken from the tail like 891–893 and 894–896 before it. **897** — `renderDoc`'s heading arm (`design/session.js`) `continue`d before the arm that emits cards and gutter marks, so a heading-targeted proposal had no mark to press and opened nothing when the rail pressed it: the open-card swallow and the `.achip` stack are `swallowOpen()` and `chipStackHtml()` now, called by the heading arm and the paragraph arm alike, behaviour unchanged. It is the second half of a fix dated 2026-08-17, whose own comment says the composer's branch was hoisted above the heading branch *because a draft on a section title opened nothing at all*; the submitted-proposal half was never lifted with it. **898** — the geometry the marks brought with them, which `card-audit` reported in numbers before anybody looked: a heading's chipcol stood 12px inside the gutter column and jumped that 12px on open, `--s3` exactly, being the negative margin a `.anch` paragraph has and an `<h2>` has not; the heading's chipcol takes it explicitly and the fold triangle steps outside the marks, so the marks stay one unbroken column. **899** — the guard, and **two gaps in `walkCharter` rather than one**: the fixture had no heading-targeted proposal (`quick-shedhead` is one now, on a heading given an explicit `key`), and the walk swallowed its own null at `if (m) { … }` with no else — so *nothing happens* was the one shape of defect this instrument could not see. The else is conditioned on a **way in** existing for that id before the toggle, because the walk drives `toggle()` rather than a click and an unserved salience diagonal is legitimately absent from rail and gutter (SPEC §8.3a); *no way in* and *opened nothing* are the two different reports `openAndMeasure` has always made. Reasoning: `design/DECISIONS.md`. No engine or SPEC change — a heading is an ordinary line to `engine-core/src/text/*`. Nothing released.

**900–904 are the entry-94/97 documentation sweep** (2026-08-26), a consistency pass over the session-loaded set after the membership rebuild, answered by Ed the same day. **900–902 are open above**, each with his answer; **903 is spent** — the rename, built 2026-08-27 — and **904 is spent** — 🤝's `proposed` and `open` rungs went with entry 94's four-rung `joinPolicy` and the module folds every legacy value to `{apply}` on load, so the two label maps that still speak them cannot fire; Ed's answer was to mark them dead where they sit and remove them in one deliberate sweep rather than piecemeal, which is what the code now says. **905–909 are released unspent.**

Twelve findings were fixed with them rather than asked, `spec-check` having been green throughout — which is the lesson of the pass and is worth keeping: **the checker asserts the tables and the prose around them is unguarded**, so a rebuild that appends beside what it contradicts passes it. The worst of them, SURFACE **F19**, had recorded entry 94's move of the invitation box as an *instruction to substitute* — its headline saying every 🪪 below now reads ✉️, its body still saying 🪪 — and four other sites had copied the unsubstituted form. The rest: SURFACE Y24's subject and gate; E10 claiming invitations and removals for the constitutional route alone, where their route is now 🪪's and 🥾's price; CLAUDE.md's *two kinds of decision* opening by listing the membership as constitutional and closing by calling it a fact; its 🤝 entry describing the pre-entry-94 split in every clause; `applicants` reading its policy off 🤝; `founderDirect` claiming the register's pair still lives in the applications value; `door-cards` filed under `constitution-section` and pointing at the page-key table; SPEC §9.6's *join policy*; and README at v0.73 and 501 tests against a v0.76 spec and 531. Commits `032f00b`, `c2e4dca`, `17bfbed`, `37daf20`.

**910–912 are what the entry-95/96 build left standing** (2026-08-26), all three open above and none of them raised by the work itself — they are things the work walked into. **910** the setup-probe's four uncovered cards, found while re-freezing the reference and older than this session. **911** a walk running against a server another session left listening, met in the wild against a build old enough to know the `signing` setting v0.70 retired. **912** the two undecided details in the admit card's news form. Nothing released.

**913–914 are what answering 910 walked into** (2026-08-26), claimed here as they were raised, both open above. **913** the two guards sharing one CI job, where a red first step means the second never runs — found because it had already happened, and `founding-golden` was 52 differences stale by the time anybody ran it. **914** 🛡️ Your Shield, uncovered by the setup-probe and structurally invisible to `--strict`, since a grant blocks nothing and so can never produce a dead step. Nothing released. **913 (5) and 914 (7 + 9) answered and built the same day**; **915** is what the audit 914 (9) asked for then reported — the thirteen cards below ⏩ that the probe has never opened — claimed as it was raised and open above.

**913–916 were all answered and built on 2026-08-26**, the day they were raised, and **910, 911 and 912 close with them** — the three the session opened on. **917** is the one thing left standing: nothing runs the server walks, raised in chat while answering 916 and filed here rather than lost. It is the same shape as 910, 913 and 915 one level out — a guard nobody runs, or whose failure nobody sees, is not a guard. Nothing released. **911 answered (a) the same day**, and **916** is what building it walked into: `npm run journey` broken against entry 94's door rework, pre-existing and confirmed at HEAD, with five CLAUDE.md gotchas resting on it as their named guard. Nothing released.

**625, 628, 629, 630, 633, 642 and 840 were the seven answered on 2026-08-26** and folded by the batch-N pre-flight the same day. **Three close and leave the register**: **628** — the golden walk's locale-dependent date, whose surviving half asked whether the *page* should date identically for every member; Ed: *leave the date locale-dependent*, so the reader's own locale stands and (a)'s pinned walk context is the whole of the answer. **633** — whether 🌡️ reads at tab size; Ed: *close it — the thermometer stays*, so the thin-diagonal and the cold-clause-in-a-hot-palette observations are both recorded-and-declined rather than open. **642** — whether a member is owed an aggregate of what the Founder holds; Ed: *leave it per-clause — no aggregate sentence anywhere*, which is (a) as built, and it makes the governance preamble's job **state the baseline, never audit it** a rule rather than a preference. **Four are answered and stay open as work owed**, each rewritten above with its ruling: **625** the golden founding walk moves to the end of each sprint (a `ci.yml` removal, and Q917's neighbour); **629** the sim-harness founding walk is updated to the no-defaults world and put in CI; **630** one shared list-joiner at every site naming several settings; **840** the threshold card names the ceiling its own roster can reach. Nothing released.

**924 and 925 are the git question** (2026-08-27): 924 open, sub-paragraph Gate 1 with a sim measurement first; 925 parked, the dependent-candidate, version-chain and patch-id ideas kept together.

**918–923 are the seat matrix** (plan-queue **38**, backlog entry 127), claimed by the batch-N pre-flight on 2026-08-26 **before the build, not by it**, so a parked branch cannot take them twice. The plan asked for a block of five for the *no rule* rows its first green-path run reports — events its step table triggers whose SURFACE §2 Audience cell it cannot honestly reduce to a predicate (E9's *the actor; every member* and E17/E18's conditional cells being the likely shape) — and one spare stands beside them for whatever the first run turns up that is neither a finding nor a *no rule*. Each question quotes the cell verbatim, names the step and the seats, and offers the reading the builder would have taken. Unspent numbers are released. **Folded 2026-08-27 by the first full run** (plan-queue **41**, entry 139, against build `7eceef8`): **918** spent on E9 — the one genuine *no rule* row, once per hat, now carried as `filed: 'Q918'` so the harness is green over it; **919** spent on the run's one member-hat finding, 💤 unrehydratable and every card below it withheld from members; **920** on its one clerk-hat finding, `view.convenor.isMember` stale for a clerk so 🍾 waits on a voice they do not hold; **921–923 released unspent**. E17/E18's conditional cells were never reported — the step table raises no text race. The two last lines, verbatim: `seat-matrix: findings=15 noRule=0 shape=0 errors=0 refused=0 unstood=0 exit=1` (member) and `seat-matrix: findings=25 noRule=0 shape=0 errors=0 refused=0 unstood=4 exit=1` (clerk) — red on 919 and 920 and on nothing else; the run's JSON is gitignored by design, so a later session re-runs `npm run seat-matrix -- $DRAFT_BASE_URL --hat=<hat> --out=<file>` for its baseline rather than looking for one. Note that **924–925 are claimed twice in this section** — below, for the copy freeze (plan 39), and above, for the git question, which spent them (items 924 and 925 exist) — so plan 39's claim is void; which block gives way is not this fold's call.

**924–925 are the copy freeze** (plan-queue **39**, backlog entry 128), claimed at the same moment. The plan names **no** identifiers of its own and may well spend neither: the pair is provisioned for what freezing 278 cards of copy exposes that normalisation cannot honestly hide — a string that moves between two clean runs for a reason nobody has named, and the two known errors it freezes as facts (*seat:applicant offered no cards*, *closed: no backlog (U:) records on the page*) becoming questions rather than records. Unspent numbers are released.

**926–941 are batch L, promise-coverage** (backlog entries 78–85; plan-queue **42–49**), claimed by the batch pre-flight on 2026-08-27 **before the builds, not by them**, so no parked branch can take them twice. Each session audits one setting against the room's promise and files what does not hold; a finding is a backlog entry and needs no number, but a gap that turns out to be a **SURFACE §2 Audience cell** the seat matrix cannot read is a *no rule* in the harness's sense, and that is a question — quoting the cell verbatim, naming the step and the seats, offering the reading the builder would have taken, and never editing the cell, which is Ed's. **926–927** 🪪 admissions and 🤝 applications (entry 78); **928–929** the register and the two doors ✉️ ❌ (79); **930–931** 🥾 removal (80) — which also has the *consent by abstention* reading and the electorate half of X5's ground shift to put to Ed; **932–933** 💤 lapse (81), the pre-start clock and the package mail; **934–935** 🌍 chamber (82); **936–937** 👤 authorship (83); **938–939** 👁️ judgments (84), one of which is provisioned for the room-of-two arithmetic that identifies a keep on the count alone, on which SPEC is silent; **940–941** 👥 quorum (85). Unspent numbers are released.

**930–931 were folded by the 🥾 build** (plan-queue **44**, backlog entry 80, 2026-08-27, against build `ee8bb8c`): **930** spent on E11's audience cell *whoever the router serves* — the one *no rule* row the harness's new `remove-motion` step reports, once per hat, and the only §2 cell the 🥾 audit met that the harness cannot read; **931 released unspent**. The audit's two design questions — whether `consent` means *not over a keep* or *only by an accept*, and whether X5's ground shift moves a running motion's **electorate** as well as leaving its route alone — went to Ed in the run's own report rather than onto the register, and its three gaps are backlog entries, which need no number. The member hat's line, verbatim: `seat-matrix: findings=12 noRule=1 filed=1 shape=0 errors=0 refused=0 unstood=0 exit=1` (`--to=live`) — the new row contributing no finding, no page error and no refused command.

**942 is the band's record chip** (plan-queue **60**, backlog entry 72): the reversal of the band's 2026-08-18 *a settled setting stays because it is the rule, and the band keeps no history* rule, which now also holds one grey record chip per settled motion behind the rule at that clause. Claimed so the reversal is a decided item with a number, recorded in `design/DECISIONS.md` under it rather than as an unattributed change of mind. **Built 2026-08-27**: `recordCard` / `settledMotionsFor` / `recordBody` in `design/session-view.html`, the reversal written into `setup.js`'s *piles* comment, `SURFACE.md` §8 and §9, and the reasoning in `design/DECISIONS.md` under *A settled motion files behind its rule*. Two rulings the build had to make and the plan did not: the chip is keyed `rec:<pile>:<n>` rather than by the motion id, because a ✒️ amendment's folded id carries the wall clock and a card key is a copy-golden key; and a ✒️ set whose value did not move files nothing, since *changed it from rising from 55% to rising from 55%* is a sentence about nothing. **Spent.**

**943 is the pruning-test audit of CLAUDE.md** (plan-queue **75**, backlog entry 47, batch K) and **944 is the SURFACE.md extraction pass** (plan-queue **76**, backlog entry 143). Both are read-only passes that write a numbered proposal to `design/spec-pass/` and change nothing in the document they audit; the number gives each pass an identity so the later plan that applies Ed's line-by-line approvals is recorded under it, the way Q736 and Q737 carry CLAUDE.md's last extraction. Neither is released: a pass that was made has a number whether or not any item is approved. **943 is spent** (2026-08-27, against `6d64d28`): the proposal is `design/spec-pass/claude-md-audit.md`, **282 items** — one per line of `CLAUDE.md`, in one continuous sequence, plus item 283 proposing a fifth admission question — approved line by line and applied by a later plan. Verdicts: **235 keep · 21 move · 26 rewrite · 0 retire**, and the nought is the finding rather than an omission — nothing in the file guards a thing that has left the tree, so the inversion retires nothing. The arithmetic, in UTF-8 bytes on disk including each line's CRLF, the 282 units summing to the measured 97,343 B exactly: 72,356 B kept, 11,222 B proposed to leave (+1,369 B of residual pointers), 418 B proposed to move *within* the file, and 13,347 B of rewrites proposed at 8,565 B — everything approved would leave 82,708 B, which is a consequence and not a goal (Ed's *no byte budget*, Q723–731, is not touched). The largest coherent group is *The two kinds of decision*, which `SPEC.md:249`, `:255` and `:257` already state in the same words. **Nothing in `CLAUDE.md` moved**; the diff is this paragraph and the proposal.
**943 is the pruning-test audit of CLAUDE.md** (plan-queue **75**, backlog entry 47, batch K) and **944 is the SURFACE.md extraction pass** (plan-queue **76**, backlog entry 143). Both are read-only passes that write a numbered proposal to `design/spec-pass/` and change nothing in the document they audit; the number gives each pass an identity so the later plan that applies Ed's line-by-line approvals is recorded under it, the way Q736 and Q737 carry CLAUDE.md's last extraction. Neither is released: a pass that was made has a number whether or not any item is approved. **944's proposal is written** (2026-08-27, plan-queue **76**): `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md`, **45 numbered items** — 6 retire, 14 reduce, 1 move, 5 point, 19 keep — plus the budget item and four the pass could not decide, and **nothing in `SURFACE.md` moved**; no row, cell, id or heading is proposed for removal. Its second section is the **reader contract** — what `spec-check`, the seat matrix and `checkClaudeMd` actually read out of the file — which is what makes an approved move mechanical, and which corrects two facts the plan carried: the seat matrix's `AUDIENCE` has **seven** verbatim Audience cells now, not five, and `CLAUDE.md` cites **eight** `SURFACE §` sections, not nine, with §4 and §5 cited by nobody. The arithmetic is the item worth Ed's eye: the file measures **75,959 bytes on disk** at `5d2ac41`, having gained 4,671 in the five days this batch took to reach the plan, so a full approval saves 5,465 and leaves it at **70,494 — still over the 70,000 watch**, which is why item 46 recommends raising the budget to 76,000 rather than the plan's own *keep 70,000*.

**840 is built** (plan-queue **65**, backlog entry 61, 2026-08-27) and leaves the open list: the threshold card names the ceiling its own room can reach. Ed answered it (a) on 2026-08-26 — name it, change nothing — so the mechanism is untouched: no cap on the bar by E, no per-pair evidence accumulation, no re-pricing of a document of one. The ceiling as a **function of E** is `unanimousCeiling` / `ceilingPct` in `packages/engine-core/src/ranking/ceiling.ts`, built on the engine's own `fitDavidson` at the prior `fitRaceMembers` uses; the page cannot call it, because `design/constitution.js` deliberately carries no engine-core, so `packages/constitution/src/threshold.ts` carries the whole-percent table (`BAR_CEILING_PCT`, `barCeilingPct`) and `test/threshold.test.ts` pins the copy to the fit, entry for entry, out to E = 40. **Floored, never rounded**, which is the new STYLE.md §2 bullet: 0.7978 reads *79%* because 80 is the first bar a room of one can never clear. **When it re-states itself was the open half of the ruling, and the answer is live** — read from the room as it stands on every render, no snapshot at the founding and no notification, since §9.0a already makes an arrival while a question collects a ground shift; the sentence is present-tense and says the ceiling rises as members arrive. It shows only while the control can express a value above it — to a room of 3 under the slider's 95, to 9 under the founder's own 99 — and it is its own grey line rather than a third sentence in the body, which card-audit **H4** caps at 200 characters. Two cards moved in the copy golden (plan-queue **39**'s), both the founder's own 🌡️ set card: **no card-audit walk renders the member's answer body for 🌡️**, so the slider's note is unfrozen and `slider-walk` is the only thing that opens it. The neighbouring `rivalGateMinComparisons` unreachable constant is recorded in `design/DECISIONS.md` and deliberately not touched.

**945 is an abstention resolving a founding question at the act** (2026-08-27, riding R-049 and Q647), claimed by the batch pre-flight for a decision that was **never in the open register**: it was asked from backlog 64's plan while that plan was being written for the overnight draft run, the build assumed the answer, and Ed gave it the next day (*yes — the sign-out resolves it immediately*). It is recorded here so the ruling has an identity, and **not added above**, which is where things still open live. The rule: a sign-out is itself a resolution moment, so an abstention that leaves a founding question's electorate exactly the members who have already answered settles it at that act rather than at the next unrelated answer — `signOut` calling `maybeResolveAll(t)` beside `maybeSettleMotions(t)` (`packages/constitution/src/session.ts`), which is what the tree already does. The three smaller readings the plan stated in its Context were offered for refusal and none was refused, so all three stand: an abstainer **stays listed** in the 🍾 readout owing nothing rather than dropping out of it; the members who have already answered are **not told**, because a ground shift is a roster move and an abstention is not one; and an abstainer may still submit an answer, which is recorded, served back as their own `myAnswer` and counted towards nothing. **Folded**: SPEC §9.0a's electorate bullet states the moment (**v0.79**, `README.md` following), and `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` R-049 carries the confirmation and the three readings. **Spent.**

**945 carries two things, and neither is renumbered** (noticed by the batch-P pre-flight, 2026-08-27). Two sessions claimed it on the same day: the alpha-readiness pass took **945–947** for the non-converged Davidson fit (open above), and the batch pre-flight took **945** for the abstention ruling recorded below. Both are published — `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` R-049 cites *Q945* for the abstention, and the alpha-readiness block cites 945 for the Davidson question — so renumbering either would break a citation, and the house rule is never to renumber. **945 therefore means both, told apart by context**, and the two *Next unused number* lines this collision left behind are collapsed into the one below. The lesson is the one CLAUDE.md already teaches and this is the second instance of: a number is claimed by writing it into this file, and two sessions that both read the tail before either writes will take the same number.

**948 is the disclosure rule's other direction** (2026-08-27, riding R-050 and Q770), claimed by the batch-P pre-flight for a decision that was **never in the open register**: it was asked from backlog 31's plan while that plan was being written for the overnight run, the build assumed the answer, and Ed gave it the next day. The question: §3.5a says a proposal keeps the disclosure rung it was made under, and the ruling behind it was argued only for a rung that **loosens** — a proposal made under *Nobody's name, ever* is never named later. The other direction was unnamed: a proposal made under *Names at the close* when the room afterwards tightens to *Nobody's name, ever*. Ed: *Symmetric — the rung at submission governs, always.* So the author who accepted that rung when they proposed is named at the close, and the rule needs no second clause and no per-direction test. **Folded**: SPEC §3.5a's *made under* paragraph now says the rule is symmetric and names the tightening case (**v0.80**, `README.md` following), and R-050 carries the confirmation. Nothing in the code moves — `authorVisible` already reads the stamp on the candidate and never the constitution's current value, which is direction-agnostic by construction, so the ruling makes explicit what the mechanism already does. **Spent.**

**949 is the wallet above its maximum** (2026-08-27), claimed by the batch-P pre-flight for a second decision that was **never in the open register**: it was asked from the promise-coverage plan for ⏱️ (batch O) in its *Questions for Ed* and answered the same day. The ⏱️ clause a room agrees reads *Members start with 4 ✏️ each, up to a maximum of 8*, and the engine treats the maximum as a cap on **accrual** and not on possession, so a motion that cuts the cap leaves members holding more than the clause says they may — locked as intended by `promise-rate.test.ts`'s fold case (*bo held 4 under a cap of 8; the cut to 3 does not claw them back*). The choice was to admit it in the clause or to clamp. Ed: **Clamp on a cap cut.** The narrow reading is the one taken, and it is the only one consistent with what stands: `packages/engine-core/src/tokens.ts` already says *refunds are never forfeited to the cap*, so a refund may still carry a wallet above the maximum between cap changes; what changes is that **a motion or a pen that lowers the cap clamps every wallet to the new maximum at the moment it lands**, and the sentence a member reads becomes true again there. **Folded into SPEC §7**, which now states the clamp. **The code does not do this yet** and the promise test locks the opposite: the clamp is a build of its own — engine-core's rate handling, `promise-rate.test.ts`'s fold case turned, and the ⏱️ amendment path in `engine-bridge`. Recorded here per the spec-pass rule that where spec and code disagree the disagreement is written down and the code change is a separate decision; a backlog entry carries the build. **Spent, less the build.**

**950–963 are batch P** (plan-queue **79–83**), claimed by the batch pre-flight on 2026-08-27 against `6221e55` **before the builds, not by them**, so no parked branch can take them twice. **950–951** — plan-queue **79**, the surface says *vote*, never *judgment*; the plan claims none of its own and these are provisioned for what extending `checkBannedWords` and card-audit's T15 turns up, where a literal cannot be narrowed without losing the word or the Hollow Oak charter's own clauses need the exemption looking at rather than the fixture. **952–954** — plan-queue **80**, 🌡️'s three rungs and the number behind a fourth; the plan asks for *probably one*, for the pre-existing fixture-strip defect (`watchBody` draws Hollow Oak's histogram and a literal *result* on every live delegated card), and the rest are for what building the strip off the room's own distribution exposes. **955–956** — plan-queue **81**, `docs.vote/pairwise`; the plan claims none and prepares its findings as *Questions for Ed*, so these are for a genuine design question the explainer raises. **957–959** — plan-queue **82**, `meaningOf` for 👥 ⏱️ 💤 🪜 🌡️; its one identifier is a **T-rule in `design/STYLE.md` §8, not a question number** (take **T39**, the table running to T38 at this sha), and these are for what folding five hand-written meanings into one family disagrees about. **960–963** — plan-queue **83**, the founder chooses a shape before the birth; the plan asks for one, for the value table's status (*which cells move when `alpha-preset` sweeps each shape, and whether 🥾 and 👁️ belong in the shaped set at all*), and a block of four because it is the batch's only mechanism change and the one whose table is explicitly marked for QA to reshape. **`R-051` in `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` is claimed here too**, for plan-queue **83**'s SPEC §9.0a sentence — a shape chosen at the birth is folded as the convenor's own pre-start sets. **Unspent numbers are released**, and a plan that spends none says so in its closing report.

**960 is the shape table's status** (2026-08-27, plan-queue **83**, entry 166, riding R-053; v0.82), spent by the build out of the batch-P block above. The three shapes' values in `packages/constitution/src/shapes.ts` are **placeholders until `alpha-preset` sweeps each shape on *alive***: the sweep now carries one derived row per shape (`shape: meeting` · `shape: conference` · `shape: ongoing`), reported and not checked. Open for Ed: which cells move once the sweep has been read; and whether 🥾 and 👁️ belong in the shaped set at all — 🥾 is on neither Ed's unavoidable list nor his shaped list and the plan shaped it (*consent* for a meeting and a conference, *assembly* for ongoing), and 👁️ is shaped to its most protective rung where QA may prefer *after* for a conference. The one cell with evidence behind it is the meeting's ⏱️, which is the sweep's own *ALPHA PRESET*. **Spent.** **961–963 are released**: the build raised no second question.

**964–992 are batch R** (plan-queue **85–98**), claimed by the batch pre-flight on 2026-08-28 against `a72702f` **before the builds, not by them**, so no parked branch can take them twice. The batch is one chain — 87 → 88 → 89 → 90 → 91 → 92 → 93 → 94 → 95 → 96 → 97 → 98, with 85 and 86 free-standing — and every plan but one declares *Identifiers: none*, so most of these blocks are provisioned for what a build turns up rather than for a question the plan already holds. **964** — plan-queue **85**, the *authorship on the wire* tests under the file's 60,000 ms budget (one line; it can raise nothing, and the number is a formality). **965–966** — **86**, the `qa:freeze` script and `probe.mjs --update`; for the freeze cadence its first real use settles, and one spare. **967** — **87**, the delegate rung relabelled. **968–969** — **88**, the 🏛️ route said one way everywhere; for a rung-label reading the sweep turns up. **970–971** — **89**, the governance sentence on its own line; for the escaping question the joiner raises. **972–973** — **90**, the empty-subsection placeholders and the 🎩 alignment. **974** — **91**, 👑/📯 on the founder's row. **975–977** — **92**, the ✋ card opening on your own row; three because the plan extends `card-audit` with a new rule (P6) as well as fixing the page. **978–979** — **93**, the newborn-subsection fade. **980–982** — **94**, ✋ and 🖼️ at the save; the plan asks for **one**, to record Ed's ruling as a decided entry so `blocks: false`, the SURFACE rows and the journey assertion cite a Q rather than a backlog entry in another repository — the other two are for the reorder's findings, one of which the plan already names (SPEC §9.0c's *never asked at the founding*, contradicted since Q645). **983–984** — **95**, the founder's ✉️ task once the Membership rules stand. **985–986** — **96**, a grant wearing the glyph of the power it grants. **987–988** — **97**, the picture card as only the grid; one is likely to be the live page's render cost, since entry 186 reverses Q732's *only the open category may be drawn* and the plan does not measure what that costs on the 4-second poll. **989–992** — **98**, the click gesture behind one switch; four, the largest block in the batch, because it is its only mechanism-shaped change and a gesture trial raises questions by construction. **Unspent numbers are released**, and a plan that spends none says so in its closing report.

**980 serves ✋ and 🖼️ at the save, and lets the founding walk past them** (Ed, 2026-08-28, plan-queue backlog entry 179: *✋ and 🖼️ should both appear together after the birth, and not block the path*). They had been rows 10 and 11 of `ORDER`, born one after the other between 🎩 and 💤, and `blocksOrder` treated every non-gate card as a blocker until it settled — so a founder could not reach 💤 until they had OK'd a name and then OK'd a picture, one at a time, in the middle of the constitution. Ed: **both born at the save alongside the pen, and neither holds the queue.** The reasoning, in the order it decides things: **a blank is a real answer** on both — a blank name is Anonymous (SPEC §9.0c: *a name, not a gap*) and a blank picture is initials — so there is nothing the constitution is waiting to learn from either, which is what makes walking past them lossless rather than a deferral. **They are personal and bind nobody**, so they do not belong in the single file the way a setting does. And **they stand above ✒️**, which needed its own argument, the pen's rule being that *every card below it in the order is committed with the very pen it hands over*: the rule does not reach a pair committed with no power at all. The mechanism is a flag, **`blocks: false` on the two card literals**, read by `blocksOrder` and deliberately not `isGate`, which also means news-not-question, hidden-from-strangers, no composer and a dozen other things none of which is true of a personal card. `spec-check`'s `checkOrder` reads the flag off the literals and asserts SURFACE §8's *blocks?* column against it, so the exemption cannot be widened silently; SURFACE F2 and F3 carry the rule, and `journey` asserts the pair adjacent, after 📧 and before 🌍 — position rather than adjacency to the pen, since the walk takes rail entries in document order and may meet 🛡️ first. **They arrive *together*** for a reason worth stating: 🖼️ follows ✋ in `ORDER`, so the instant ✋ stops blocking, `orderReady(mypic)` is true exactly when `orderReady(myname)` is. A **copy correction** rides with it, because serving ✋ at the save serves it *before* 🎩 is answered — so almost every founder meets the member branch of its note first and a clerk sees theirs only by reopening the card, and both branches now have to be true on their own: neither says *optional* as though the other were not, the clerk's naming the *Founded by* line and the member's saying it is how you appear in the room. **One thing the build reasoned about and deliberately did not change**, recorded because plan-queue **95** widens the same predicate one plan later: `nothingElseServed` (`session-view.html`) is `!servedCards().some((c) => !c.isBegin && !remedyOnly(c))`, and `beginOffered` consults it as the **last-resort 🍾 door** whenever `readiness()` is not ready — so two cards that stand served and unsettled make it false. They are **not** exempted, and should not be: an unanswered ✋ is genuinely something being served, and a 🍾 offered over it would be the door opening while a real task stands.
