Nothing is lost, because both are always committable — ✋ is `ready: () => true` and 🖼️ has no `CHOSEN` entry — so F18 holds (the founder in the `one-voice` dead end is served ✋, 🖼️ and the ✉️ remedy, all three actionable) and the door opens on the next press. Before this change the pair could not be unanswered in that state at all, the single file having made the founder pass them to reach a delegation; after it, the dead end costs two presses more and no reachability. **Spent.** 981 and 982 are open above — SPEC §9.0c's *never asked at the founding*, which this makes sharper, and the `--delegate-all` mood that Q967's rename blinded, found while trying to walk the paragraph above rather than argue it.

**993–1012 are twenty decisions Ed gave on 2026-08-27 and 2026-08-28**, claimed and folded by the same pre-flight. Nineteen of them were **never in the open register**: each was asked from a plan while it was being written or from a batch's run report, and answered before anything here carried a number, so they are recorded as decided and deliberately not added above. The twentieth is **Q70**, which had a number already and stays above until its build lands.

**993 is the disclosure rule's other direction, answered a second time and the other way** (Ed, 2026-08-28; SPEC §3.5a **v0.83**, R-054). Q948 recorded *symmetric — the rung at submission governs, always* on 2026-08-27; the next day, reading the edge stated plainly — *a proposal made under Names at the close in a room that later tightens to Nobody's name, ever is still named at the close; honour reveals something the later, stricter rule would have hidden* — Ed answered **asymmetric: a later stricter rule wins for reveals**. The later answer stands and Q948 is superseded, not renumbered. The rule is now the protective conjunction: a name appears only where the rung at submission **and** the rung standing at the reveal both allow it; loosening still reveals nothing, which is R-050's original half untouched. **Folded** into SPEC §3.5a and R-054. **The code does not do this yet and the disagreement is deliberate**: `authorVisible` reads the stamp on the candidate and never the constitution's current value, which was exactly right under the symmetric rule and is now half a rule, and `packages/server/test/server.test.ts`'s *👤 authorship on the wire* cases lock the symmetric behaviour. The build — `authorVisible` taking the standing rung as a second input, those tests turned, and the 🥂 card's *names* line — is a separate decision, per the spec-pass rule that where spec and code disagree the disagreement is written down. **Spent, less the build.**

**994 is §3.5a's judgments rung saying what it means** (Ed, 2026-08-28; **v0.83**, R-055). The 👁️ ladder's surface label was changed in batch O to say plainly that *revealed after the decision* means the end of the document; the sentence that produced the ambiguity was still in the spec, reading *revealed after the decision they contributed to*. Ed: **amend §3.5a to say "at the end of the document"**. **Folded.** **Spent.**

**995 is one word for the close, across both ladders** (Ed, 2026-08-28). Two ladders named one moment differently — 👤's rung read *Names at the close*, 👁️'s *at the end*. Ed: **"at the end" everywhere**. Within each ladder one label per rung is already the rule (T5); this makes it one label across the two, so a member reading both cards meets one word. **The spec half is folded** (994's sentence, and R-055 records the word). **The surface half is a copy sweep** — 👤's rung label and every site that prints or keys on it, `card-copy.golden.json` re-frozen, one STYLE audit item — and is owed a build; it must not ride a plan already in flight, because batch R has eight plans re-freezing that golden and a ninth string arriving mid-chain is how a freeze swallows somebody else's change. **Spent, less the build.**

**996 is the per-decision reveal rung**, filed above in the Backlog. Ed's 👁️ note had two halves; the first is built and the second — names shown as each decision closes, decision by decision, rather than at the end of everything — names nothing today. Ed: **file a backlog entry for the per-decision rung**. Done, under this number. **Spent.**

**997 ratifies the 🛡️ card's redraft** (Ed, 2026-08-28). The batch-O plan *The pen card reads for someone one minute in* rewrote ✒️'s body for a newcomer — no *locks*, no *spent*, no *the membership passes* — and the builder redrafted 🛡️ on the same rule, 🛡️ standing immediately after ✒️ in the founding queue and breaking it in the same three places. That was an assumption named and taken, not a ruling. Ed: **Ratify**. Nothing to build; the redraft is in, green, and the copy golden re-frozen. STYLE.md item 55 is its record. **Spent.**

**998 is the applicant's readout on the 🪪 proposal route** (Ed, 2026-08-28). Under *assembly* the count now fills; under *proposal* it still reads *0 of E have judged it*, for a correct reason — a race's answers are blind while it runs (§9.0a), so the module serves `answeredCount: 0` deliberately, and the sentence is true-but-empty by design. Ed: **say nothing on the proposal route while the race is blind**. So the readout is suppressed rather than reworded, and the card says nothing about counts until the race resolves. **Owed a build** (the applicant's card, one predicate on the price); no spec change, since §9.0a already states the blindness. **Spent, less the build.**

**999 is the fifth admission question** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the CLAUDE.md audit's **C6**). The audit ran under a *pruning test* — does a session need this loaded every time, or could a pointer do? — separate from the file's four admission questions. Ed: **yes, add the fifth question as written at item 283**. **Folded** into `CLAUDE.md`'s *What goes in this file*, verbatim from the proposal, with the two *four questions* sentences around it corrected. The lens now applies at every extraction rather than only when a pass is commissioned, and it is explicitly not a byte cull — Q723–731's *no byte budget* stands. **Spent.**

**1000 retires `the-briefing`** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the audit's **C2**). The glossary carried a 314-byte entry for a surface that does not exist, with a SPEC §3.5 constraint attached; the audit's rule 3 says an aspirational state leaves the file unless a plan or a backlog entry names it, and neither draft's nor plan-queue's `BACKLOG.md` mentions it. Ed: **not planned — retire the item; its SPEC §3.5 constraint moves with it**. **Folded**: the entry is deleted from `CLAUDE.md`. Q52 above still describes the briefing as *still to be rebuilt*; it is left standing because it is a question about the composer's three unruled calls and not a plan to build the thing. **Spent.**

**1001 keeps the card lifecycle stated twice** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the audit's **C3**). `CLAUDE.md`'s 598-byte restatement of the lifecycle rules says of itself *kept here in one line each because they bite*, and SPEC/SURFACE state the same rules — with `spec-check` now asserting SURFACE's tables, which it did not when the decision was made, so a second copy that can drift is arguably the risk rather than the insurance. Ed: **the decision stands — keep the lines in CLAUDE.md**. The audit's item is withdrawn. **Spent.**

**1002 keeps *One card per decision* whole** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the audit's **C5**). The reading offered was that the rule is a test a session applies while proposing a card, and the two worked examples (⏰ swallowing *whether it ends*, 🎲 ceasing to be a card) are reasoning that belongs in `design/DECISIONS.md`. Ed: **keep all of it**. The examples are what makes it a test rather than a slogan — it is what the entry says of itself, *the test cuts both ways, which is what makes it a test*. **Spent.**

**1003 moves the governance rules out of `CLAUDE.md`** (Ed, 2026-08-28, closing the audit's **C7**, its largest and most consequential item), filed above in the Backlog. Seven items — 23, 26, 28, 29, 34, 202, 211, some 4,915 bytes, 5% of the file — restate rules `SPEC.md` or `SURFACE.md` already state in the same words, against the file's own admission rule (*what comes back here is a § pointer*). The argument for keeping them is that **SPEC.md is not loaded per session**, so a session that needs the two kinds of decision must open it, and one that does not know it needs to will not. Ed: **move them — pointers only, SPEC is the source**. **Owed a build**, and a careful one: it is a large edit to the one file every session reads whole, its residual pointers must resolve under `spec-check`, and it must not land in the middle of a batch whose plans are editing `CLAUDE.md` for other reasons. Note it does **not** touch 1001's lifecycle lines or 1002's card rule, both of which Ed kept in the same sitting. **Spent, less the build.**

**1004–1005 are the SURFACE.md extraction's two open items** (Ed, 2026-08-28, on the batch-O proposal `design/spec-pass/surface-md-extraction.md`, which is not yet performed). **1004** — after the extraction, §5 has one surviving bullet, *The head of the document*, stated nowhere else; the choice was whether §5 survives as a section holding it or becomes a one-line redirect with the bullet moving to §8 beside *The band*. Ed: **§5 survives with its one bullet**. **1005** — whether a table cell may carry a `→ why:` pointer, the file's convention for sending reasoning to `DECISIONS.md` having lived in prose paragraphs only; two cells (❌ Remove's field, `adm:`'s head) are prose wearing a cell's clothes and the pass kept both. Ed: **no — cells stay self-contained; take the flag off**. So a cell that is long is simply what the file looks like when a card is genuinely complicated, and items 42 and 43 of the proposal are withdrawn rather than deferred. Both fold **into the extraction when it is built**, not before. **Spent.**

**1006 is the SURFACE §8.1 tabulation pass**, filed above in the Backlog. §8.1 was 13,548 bytes of unbroken prose in twenty-two rules when this was written, three of them past 1,100 bytes each, and is **14,177 bytes in twenty-three, F1–F23**, at the build — the figures above are the 2026-08-28 ones and should not be re-derived from; the extraction pass said, out of its own grounds, that tabulating F1–F22 the way §9 tabulates card kinds would do more for the file than every item in its proposal put together. Ed: **yes — file a backlog entry for a §8.1 tabulation pass**. It needs its own plan, its own line-by-line approvals and its own question number when it is written. **Re-confirmed 2026-08-29** with one addition — *it must not renumber F1–F22* — and merged with entry 191 by the same sitting's ruling at **1050**: the tabulation is what 191 now is. **Built 2026-09-01** as plan-queue 140, spending **1122–1128** — and the pass's answer is that **F1–F23 do not tabulate**. A faithful table of all 23, nothing shortened and nothing dropped, is **1,681 bytes larger** than the prose: §9's card-kinds table works because eight repeated field-labels lift out of every row into a header, and §8.1's rules share no field to lift, so the table has one honest column and that column is the prose again with scaffolding added. The eight rules that tabulate cleanly are 7.6% of the section; the six that resist are 62.8%, and F21 alone would be one cell of 2,577 characters. **§8.1 is untouched and no id moved.** What landed: `spec-check`'s `checkFIds`, and `design/spec-pass/pass-5.html` — every rule in full both ways, so Ed can overturn the verdict rule by rule. **Spent, less Ed's row-by-row answers.**

**1007 wires the freeze offer to draft** (Ed, 2026-08-28). `npm run probe -- --strict` had been red since before batch Q — 258 diffs on the setup probe, 12 on the session probe, the twelve being strings the *vote* plan deliberately moved — because plan-queue's per-batch *freeze the reference to what QA just approved?* offer is made only to a project naming a command in `cfg.qa.freeze`, and draft names none. Three sessions in batch Q saw the red and each correctly declined to freeze on its own, freezing being a claim that what is there is what you approved. Ed: **re-freeze after QA of Q, and wire a `qa.freeze` script so the offer comes to the phone after every batch**. The script is plan-queue **86** in batch R (`scripts/qa-freeze.mjs`, `npm run qa:freeze`, re-freezing all three references a batch moves — `design/reference/`, the copy golden, the founding golden); the plan-queue config key and **the first re-freeze itself are Ed's own hand**, and no build in batch R may commit a re-frozen reference. Note for whoever does it: batch R moves the probe's step list **twice** (plan 93 inserts a step, plan 97 deletes one), so the reference is two steps out by the end of the batch, not one. **Spent, less the script and the freeze.**

**1008 gives the *authorship on the wire* tests the file's budget** (Ed, 2026-08-28). Five of the seven timed out at vitest's 5,000 ms default under load during batch Q and passed on a quiet lane against the same code; each founds a real document over HTTP, and every other real-HTTP case in `packages/server/test/server.test.ts` declares 60,000 (or 30,000 for *the whole road*). An earlier answer had said *do not raise the timeout*, given on a wrong diagnosis, and was refused on Ed's behalf. Ed: **give them the file's 60,000 ms budget**. Built as plan-queue **85** in batch R. **Spent.**

**1009–1010 are the two document refactors**, both filed above in the Backlog. plan-queue measures three of draft's documents at the end of every run against budgets Ed set: after batch Q `CLAUDE.md` stood at 99,290 of 100,000, `SPEC.md` at **81,091 against 80,000** and growing 1,000–2,000 a run, and `SURFACE.md` at **77,151 against 70,000** — the append-only surface inventory, where every batch adds rows and nothing takes any out unless somebody decides to. The tool never gates on this and never files the entry itself, because what to take out of a document is a judgement about what the document is for. Ed: **file both refactors as backlog entries, SURFACE first**. **1009** is SURFACE.md, **1010** is SPEC.md. Every session reads `CLAUDE.md` whole and `spec-check` reads `SPEC.md` end to end, so these are a cost per session as well as a readability question — and note that 1003 and 1006 both cut into the same two files from other directions, so whoever writes 1009 should read them first. **Spent.**

**1011 is the two stale lines batch Q left behind** (Ed, 2026-08-28) — **already built**. README had been bumped to spec v0.82 by *the shape* while its counts still read *the constitutional layer (555 tests)*, and Q625 still sat in the Open register as *answered, owed, not built* although its build landed in batch O. Ed: **fix README's counts and Q625 by hand now, one commit, not pushed**. Done at `a72702f`, before this pre-flight ran. **Spent.**

**1012 is the removed invitee who was never here**, filed above in the Backlog. The batch-O plan *Three acts nobody is told about* added a mail for a convenor's removal — *You are no longer a member of …* — and the mail arm in `packages/server/src/server.ts` has no arrival check, while the session's own departure fold deliberately excludes people who never arrived; so an invitee who never turned up and is then removed via `remove` is told they are no longer a member of something they were never in, and the register's departure lines say nothing about the same act. The batch review left it alone as a behaviour choice. Ed: **keep the mail and also record the departure** — so the guard is not added and the *other* half is fixed instead: the departure fold records a removal whether or not the person ever arrived. Today it is unreachable from the page (invitees are routed to `uninvite`), so nothing a member sees is wrong yet, which is why it is filed rather than hot. **Spent, less the build.**

**1013–1025 are claimed by the batch-U pre-flight** (2026-08-29), one block per plan, before any of the five is built. Every one of the five plans says *this run has no pre-flight, so claim your own numbers at claim time* — **that sentence is now false**, and a session that claims again takes numbers a sibling in the same batch has already been given. **1038–1049 are the plan-queue batch of 2026-08-29 (plans 117–120)**, claimed by the batch pre-flight against `d43e2b6` **before any of the four was built**, and claimed for all of them at once for the reason the 1026–1032 block gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. **1050–1052 are the three rulings of 2026-08-29 (afternoon) that the register never held**, answered while batch X's plans were being written and recorded here as decided rather than filed above, the way 1035–1037 were. **1050 — *143 stands; 191 becomes §8.1 as a table.*** The question was whether entry 220's measurement lifts entry 143's binding ruling on the SURFACE pass (*refactor, not cull — every row and every cell of every table stays; no retire verdict for a table row, a rule id or a heading*), given that the arithmetic of a cut respecting it reaches 70,494 bytes at best and a 63,000 target cannot be met without cutting text a recorded ruling stands behind. Ed: **143 stands**, and entry 191 is redefined — the SURFACE work owed is now the **§8.1 tabulation**, which is **1006** above, rather than a whole-file cut. Two consequences worth stating: the byte target for SURFACE is whatever the tabulation produces and is not itself the object; and *refactor, not cull* is a ruling about **SURFACE.md**, whose tables are its substance — it is not lifted onto SPEC.md or `CLAUDE.md`, both of which are governed by their own rules (the spec-pass procedure and the five admission questions). **Where this stands 2026-09-01**: the written extraction that **1009** commissioned alongside the tabulation turns out to have been performed already, by entry 191's own commit `18710a9` (see 1009 above), so what entry 191 *became* is exactly what this ruling redefined it as — **1006**'s §8.1 tabulation is the whole of the SURFACE work still owed, and the byte target still follows it rather than the other way about. `SURFACE.md` stands at **95,917 B** against the 70,000 watch, verified by plan 139, which built nothing into it. **1051 — the byte target is *a consequence, never a criterion.*** Put on `CLAUDE.md`: the file carries Ed's *no byte budget* ruling twice (**Q723–731**) — the pruning question is *asked at every extraction and never as a byte cull*, and an unguarded gotcha *is capped by nothing* — while plan-queue watches the file at 100,000 bytes, so a plan aiming at 90,000 is on its face the cull that ruling forbids. Ed: **a consequence, never a criterion.** So nothing is removed because the file is long, every removal is justified by the pruning test or by authorship, and a pass that applies both honestly and lands above the number has **passed**, not failed; the number is reported, never chased. This is the same words the entry-47 audit already used of its own arithmetic (`design/spec-pass/claude-md-audit.md`, *A consequence, not a goal*), now a ruling rather than a session's care. Folded into `CLAUDE.md`'s admission rule by **plan 120**, whose step 6 is the paragraph that carries it. **1052 — *you mark the keepers first.*** The question was how a document pass tells Ed's words from a session's when every commit in the repository is authored `edsaperia` and most carry a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer — 92 commits on `SPEC.md`, 264 on `CLAUDE.md` — so `git blame`'s author field says *100% session-written* about files that are full of Ed's decisions, and taken literally would authorise deleting them. Ed: **you mark the keepers first.** So the order of work inverts: a pass makes one marking sweep over the section and marks every paragraph that carries a human decision — an `Ed, 2026-MM-DD` attribution, a `Qnnn` or entry reference, a verbatim quotation, an answer folded from one — **before** it judges anything for removal, and what carries no mark is the candidate set. Blame is still run, for *which commit* a paragraph arrived in and what that commit was for, but it is corroboration and never the test. Both document plans in batch X are written the other way round — cut-first, provenance as a filter — and are told so in their batch notes; neither ruling nor plan makes Ed himself the marker, and a pass that finds it cannot mark a paragraph either way has found the question its block exists for.

**1053–1059 are the plan-queue batch of 2026-08-29 (plans 121–124)**, claimed by the batch pre-flight against `0377d42` **before any of the four was built**, and claimed for all of them at once for the reason the 1038–1049 block gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. Blocks are generous; **whatever is not spent is released when the batch folds**, and a plan that spends none of its own is named as having spent none. **1053 is *`journey-walk`'s faces check asserts 2 members here*** (plan 121) — a **spare, and the plan asks for none**: it is two string literals in one walk and its own Identifiers section says *None*, so 1053 exists only for the question its Context defers (assert the title, or count `#faces .seat` alone — Ed's entry 215 offers either and the plan takes the sentence). Released unspent if it does not arise. **1054–1055 are *🤖 AI Proposals leaves the surface*** (backlog entry 251, plan 122). Two spares, on a plan that crosses thirteen files and two frozen goldens; the numbers it actually needs are **not** question numbers and are assigned here because a collision in those sequences is invisible in this file — **SPEC.md goes to v0.91** (the §9.7.1 `machines` row's last cell gains its retirement note; §10 and every other rule stand), the ruling takes **`R-078`** (`design/SPEC-REASONING.md` stands at `R-077` with 75 entries at `0377d42`; `R-039` and `R-041` are absent and stay absent, never reused), and its `design/STYLE.md` §7 audit item is **71**, read off that log's tail (70 is 👁️'s two clauses, plan 117). **1056–1057 are *A rung is a sentence about the subject*** (backlog entry 250, plan 123). Two spares, for what the 🥾 fitting turns up — the plan states as an assumption that Ed's three-rung template lands on `consent` · `assembly` · `proposal` with no *any member* rung to carry his third sentence, and says a different ruling is a string change and nothing else. Its own numbers are **`design/STYLE.md` T41 and T42** for the two rules (the §8 table ends at **T40**), and **§7 audit item 72**, which is 71 + 1 *because plan 122 lands first by this plan's own `after: 251`*; if 122 does not land, 123 takes **71** instead, read off the log's tail as always. **1058–1059 are *A member convenor can resign after the start*** (backlog entry 248, plan 124). Two spares: the plan is written under the recommended reading of 248 with Ed's ruling still on his phone, and it names one thing it deliberately does not answer — whether a resignation ends the **office** or only the membership (§9.3's foundership read, `seatAlive`) — which is exactly the shape of question a block exists for. Built in plan 123, and `design/STYLE.md` §8's T41/T42 carry it.

**1080–1094 are the plan-queue batch of 2026-08-29 (plan 129)**, claimed by the batch AD pre-flight against `f9a5c66` **before it is built**, and for the reason every block above gives: a plan that takes its number when it builds collides with the plan that built before it, and with the one still parked on a branch nobody else can see. **1080–1094 are *The text is the open card: 📝, edit mode, the riding tab, and gap sites*** (backlog entry **204**, plan 129). One block, err-generous, and **whatever is not spent is released** when the batch folds. The plan's own Documents section asks for *the block at the tail* and expects **1080**, which is what it gets.

The plan's first item is **decided, not asked**, and takes the first number. **1080 — the founder's ✒️ pre-🍾 is the confirm, and 🍾 confirms whatever stands.** Ed's ruling of 2026-08-29 21:20, chosen over *🍾 stays held until one ✒️*: with 📄's OK retired, `confirm-starting-text` is sent by the founder's own commit on the proposal-row, every press, and a document that reaches 🍾 having never been confirmed is confirmed by the start itself. **Filed as decided**; the plan is right that the module must be told, and §9.0b's *a confirmed decision, not content* is unchanged in substance — the confirmation moves from a press to the start.

The rest are for what the pre-flight found the plan starts and does not follow, and the first two are the ones that will stop a build. **1081 — where the confirm at 🍾 actually lives.** The plan puts it on the page (`session-view.html` `~L7300`, the flush dropping its `cs.textConfirmed &&` guard) and removes the module's readiness hold (`packages/constitution/src/session.ts` `~L1810`) — but `cs.begin(t, …)` is reached from `packages/server/src/commands.ts:243` as well, so any `begin` that does not come through that flush starts an unconfirmed document, and the code comment at `~L1805–1809` says exactly what that costs (*wedged with no text and no way to propose one*, `canPropose` at `~L3004` being false for ever). The reading the pre-flight recommends is that the confirm belongs in **`begin()`**, where every caller reaches it, and the page's flush then does nothing special. **1082 — what makes `text` settled in `ORDER` once the OK is gone.** The plan deletes `settled()`'s `cs.textConfirmed` branch (`~L2760`) *and* the `S.seen.add('text')` that feeds `~L2758`'s `!S.seen.has(c.k)` gate, and `'text'` stays in `ORDER` immediately above `'begin'` — which is the 🎩 failure of Ed's live walk of 2026-08-21 exactly: a card whose settledness the live path cannot rebuild blocks everything below it, and here that is 🍾. Either the founder's ✒️ marks it seen, or `text` leaves `ORDER`, or `settled()` gains a rule of its own; the plan says none of the three. **1083 — `pwHasValue`, not a second `settled()`.** The plan lists `~L2158` alongside `~L2760` as *`settled()`'s `cs.textConfirmed` reading*; `~L2158` is `pwHasValue`, the R-048 gate on **laying a power down** before the setting has a value, and the Text's answer to *has it been set* is its confirmation because it carries no managed value (Q440). Deleting it would let ✒️ and 🛡️ be laid down on an unwritten Text. It should stay, and if the confirm moves to `begin()` it needs re-reading against **1081** rather than deleting.

**1084 — what the empty column says once `writeready` and `pasteready` go.** The plan removes both classes with 📄's task (`~L5545–5547`), and they are what draws the column's grey invitation (`~L118`, `~L123`) — the *column invites before it asks* rule of Q513(c). Nothing in the plan says what stands there afterwards, and **518** (answered the same evening: pre-fill as a grey placeholder) lands on precisely that hook. The two must be settled together and 129 is first. **1085 — how read mode hears a keystroke.** The plan's mechanism is *a `keydown` on the section (printable, unmodified)*, on a host it has just set `contenteditable="false"`; a non-editable, non-focusable element is not the target of a keydown, which fires at `body` and bubbles to `document`. Either the section takes a `tabindex` and the focus, or the listener is on the document and filters by reading position. **1086 — the SPEC version bump the plan does not claim.** Part 5 renames 📄 in *SPEC's two mentions* — §9.7 rule 8's *the 📄 switch at the start* (`~L263`) and §9.7.1's `startingText` row (`~L275`) — and `CLAUDE.md`'s standing rule is *amend only with Ed's sign-off; bump the version*. **SPEC goes to v0.95**, standing at v0.94, and README states it twice. **1087 — the riding tab wearing the active treatment with no card open.** M12's 8px grow is the *open card's* active tab everywhere else on the surface; a detached tab in read mode would wear it while nothing is open. Whether that is a second meaning for one treatment, or whether *detached* wants its own, is a design call the plan makes in passing. **1088 — the two hosts pre-🍾.** The plan says edit mode's host is `contenteditable="true"` *only when `MAY_PROPOSE()` or the founder's pre-🍾 hand*, and then that pre-🍾 the column is `#prose` because `syncCharter` mounts from `constituted()` — so the pre-🍾 half of that predicate is unreachable. Its own *(mine)* note offers to unify the two if it proves cheap; this number is for the answer either way. **1089 — *n places changed*, and whether the surface may say *sites*.** The plan's row middle is its own *(mine)*, and it is the first member-readable string for what Q266 never named; `design/STYLE.md` §8 has to take it. **1090 — one placeholder or two.** The gap block's *Start a new clause here.* reuses `.doc p.editable.blank`'s treatment (`design/system.css` `~L755`), which is also the empty document's `L0` — two different things saying different sentences through one rule. **1091–1094 spare.**

**The identifiers outside this sequence** the plan needs are claimed here too, the way 1067–1071 claimed a SPEC version: SURFACE's K-series takes **`K31`**, the series standing at **K30** (backlog 255, Q1075) and the plan asking for K31 by name; **SPEC goes to v0.95** (1086 above); and if the 🍾 confirm is written as a ruling rather than a sentence, it takes **`R-081`**, `design/SPEC-REASONING.md` standing at **R-080**.

**Built 2026-08-29 (plan 129), and how each number was spent.** **1080** stands as decided; the module's `begin` writes the confirm. **1081** — in `begin()`, every caller's road (R-081). **1082** — `settled()` gained a rule of its own: the text is settled from the save, never a task, no `S.seen`. **1083** — `pwHasValue` kept as it was. **1084** — the invitation keyed on the column being the founder's and empty until 🍾; `pasteready` gone, `writeready` stays as Q518's hook, with a note in the CSS. **1085** — the listener is the document's, filtering by reading position. **1086** — SPEC v0.95, README's two lines with it. **1087** — one treatment, one meaning: in edit mode the text is the open card, and a detached tab is that tab travelling. **1088** — Ed, B46: two columns; the page draws the tab, the lift and the row around `#prose` pre-🍾, session.js draws them under the charter after, `proposalRowHtml` the one helper. **1089** — *n places changed*, in K15's own word; *sites* never reaches a member (STYLE §7 item 73). **1090** — one rule, two sentences; the rule is the geometry. **1091–1094 released.** **261 folded** into K31 (gap sites); **266 unchanged**. K31, v0.95 and R-081 taken as claimed. **Spent.**

(413–416 were answered by Ed 2026-08-19 within the hour of being filed, and built the same day: **413** answered (b) and folded into SPEC §9.0b v0.61 — a blind question does not resolve while an invitation is outstanding, *and never on one voice at all*, which is the reading his own reason forced (*a founder obviously does not intend to delegate to themselves … more members are coming*); §9.6a's one-unopened-email worry is answered by §9.6a's own remedy, the founder's free hand on the roster until judging opens. **414** answered — nothing is owed an OK until judging starts, because §9.6a lets the founder re-set freely until then, so an OK collected before the start acknowledges a rule that may not be the rule; a gate opening stays the exception, being a thing that happened rather than a rule that was set. **415** agreed — 📈 pacing is the founder's at creation and not delegable into a founding question, folded into SPEC §9.7 v0.61; delegating it is a hand-over like the title's, so 📯 stays reachable. **416** answered — *if I have to do all of them anyway, they should cascade*: the answer tasks ride the settings' own wave graph, looking **through** any dependency nobody is asked about, so the founding arrives in the constitution's own order rather than as one batch of eight. Alongside them, four surface rulings with no numbers: the dev bar moved bottom-left; the empty title reads **Untitled**; ✒️ on the 📧 card **is** the send, and the card lost both explanatory blocks; the magic link lands you on the document rather than opening the 🔗 card; **a task you have to do carries no subtitle** (*that's why it's yellow*), leaving subtitles to motions and news; and **the watcher on a group task waits for your own answer**, which retires the same title appearing twice in one rail. 408–412 were asked and answered in chat 2026-08-19, and built the same day: **408** — a delegated question waits for a member who has *arrived*, except that a founder with nothing else outstanding is served the rest anyway (residual at 413); **409** answered (a) — the founder's settings arrive in waves by constitutional section, Membership → Proposals → Decisions → Anonymity, by the dependency machinery that already paced title → email → link; **410** answered (a)+(d) — a born rail entry grows and fades, a born paragraph fades, and a batch cascades in document order with each column keeping its own count; **411** yes — the kind line left every setting card, since the Proposals preamble states both routes once (Q404's own logic), surviving only where a card deviates (⏰); **412** yes — 🗑️ *put it back as it stands* is the left of every commit row you can commit, "Close" survives only where the card holds nothing to put back, and **OK** stays what a card wears when it asks nothing of you but to have seen it. With it, Ed's note on grounds: an emoji glyph is a coloured object, so ✒️ moved off the solid fill onto the accent-subtle ground, leaving the drawn ✓ as the one solid green thing on a card. 401(a) was closed by Ed 2026-08-19 — *perfect* — the 🏛️-without-the-subject class exists; built same day, SPEC §9.6a v0.60, with (b) still open above. 399 was closed by Ed 2026-08-19 — batch adoption: at each tick every cleared race adopts at once, the cooldown stays engine tuning, no ⏳ — folded into SPEC §4.2 v0.58. 402–405 were closed by Ed 2026-08-19 and built the same day — the governance tab group: 403 ok, delegation unified with holding-no-powers; 404 yes, the preamble plus deviation-only clauses; 405 🔧 for unilateral change (Ed’s pick and reading: the founder gets things going, then steps back) and 👑 for assent; 402 superseded — the 🗝️ cards dissolved into the pattern — folded into SPEC §9.7 v0.59. 400 was answered by Ed 2026-08-19 — the Members composer splits into ✉️ Invite a Member and ❌ Remove a Member, his glyphs — built the same day. 390 was consumed in chat 2026-08-19 — ordinary motion races, folded into SPEC §9.6 v0.53; 392 was closed by Ed 2026-08-19 — reading confirmed, constitutional motions keep unanimity — and deleted. 397 was closed by Ed 2026-08-19 as its (a) shape — an admit motion is a one-candidate race against the membership as it stands, every application its own race, invitation proposals carrying rationales — folded into SPEC §9.7½ v0.56. 394–396 were closed by Ed 2026-08-19 and folded into SPEC §9.7 v0.55: 394 simpler-wins — the reserve motion's payload stays power-nameable defaulting to both, the surface offers the whole crown, revisit at QA; 395 confirmed with Ed's rationale — assent is the more restrictive power because it stops members doing what they've decided; 396 closed as (c) — setup behaves like (b), the constitution paragraph states the holder in (a)'s precise sentences.)

*Tidied 2026-08-16 (housekeeping pass): items sorted numerically within each section; 158 and 163 deleted as fully resolved; 70 and 92 rewritten to their surviving halves, since the questions they originally asked no longer exist.*

Everything below 260 that does not appear as an open, deferred or backlog item above is spent. Where the content went:
- **309** — the rail crowding at a clause with twelve decisions: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 and built the same day, and settled *against* the option I raised it with. I had proposed a pile in the rail, one entry per clause expanding on click, mirroring the `tab-stack`. Ed: *rather than have queue-card piles, we want to prioritise them in the sidebar in the same way that the tabs prioritise.* Which is right, and cheaper: the rail already had an admission rule (most urgent first, never a ranking over position) and simply had no tiebreak for entries at the **same** clause, so it was falling through to fixture order. Sorting those by the tab stack's own keys makes the two columns agree — whichever decision the gutter would open is the one the rail shows — and needs no new object. Building it exposed that the gutter had no tiebreak either, so the two were promptly made to share one `leverage` comparator rather than two copies of a formula. The general lesson is worth keeping: **when two surfaces annotate the same thing, an ordering they do not share is a disagreement waiting to be noticed.**
- **294** — clauses with many tabs, all three parts closed by Ed on 2026-08-17 and built the same day. **Order** and **volume** went with the `tab-stack`; **filed decisions** with the `filedpile` at the bottom of a card's tab strip, opening on click with the card growing to hold them. Reasoning in the design notes; the named parts are in CLAUDE.md. It also turned up a branch that had never run: the document only swallowed a clause into its record where the clause had nothing live on it, which had never been wrong because until the pile there was no way to reach a filed decision from a busy clause.
- **307** — where 50% is on a retired card, once the incumbent moved to the head: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **(a)**, leave it. *I don't think anyone will miss it, and you can understand what happened from the other things on the card.* My recommendation had been (b), a 50% rule in the field; (a) is the better answer for the reason the whole card was rebuilt on — the eyebrow already states the score against the bar, and adding a second device to say a third true thing is how the card got to three bands in the first place.
- **221** — the salience diagonal, built into session-view 2026-08-16; reasoning in `design/session-view.notes.md`.
- **246–247, 249–250, 253–258** — the document-modes conversation of 2026-08-16, all settled by Ed and folded into SPEC v0.17 §9.0, §9.0a and §9.5: two modes chosen at creation, the founding ceremony and its max-of-minimums consent rule, sign-out with holding and abstaining, and the freeze. 254 was settled against my recommendation and rightly — an abstainer had the freeze available and declined it — with the one addition that they are notified if the base drops materially after they leave.
- **245** — the author's own preference: settled by Ed 2026-08-16 (*the author's vote should count towards the floor*) and built the following attempt as a **derived** fact rather than a recorded comparison. Folded into SPEC v0.18 §3.3, §7 and §8.2, with the rule it turns on — *preference is not measurement* — stated there. The first attempt recorded it as a comparison and had to be reverted: a comparison is stamped with the ground it was cast on, so the first author into a contested area lost their voice as soon as anyone joined them.
- **260** — the mark for a live proposal of your own: settled by Ed 2026-08-16 in favour of the recommendation, so ✏️ runs all the way to the seal and **means *you wrote this*** rather than handing over to 💡 once proposed. The reason is the `contents-rail`, which draws marks with no colour, so green could not carry the difference there. Reasoning in the design notes.
- **263** — the zero-diff draft: settled by Ed 2026-08-16, and better than the option I proposed. Rather than have a draft that changes nothing quietly cancel itself, the **right lane marks its own new wording** the way every other pair does (91), so a draft that changes nothing shows no green at all and the decision about it stays with the author. Reasoning in the design notes, including what the technique costs.
- **The QA of 2026-08-17**, all built the same day. The clause **keeps its highlight** when it becomes a card's head, and its **gutter mark keeps its place** — both fall out of one move: the washed block inside the head is given the same box as a `.anch` paragraph, so the wash lands on the same rectangle and the `.chipcol` inside it lands in the same gutter column. Measured after: the mark moves 0px in both axes on every card kind, and the clause text sits on the prose's own axis to the pixel. Colour changes now **fade** rather than jump, everywhere — including queue cards, which needed the wash re-painted as a bar rather than a gradient, because no browser interpolates between two gradients. Since both rails are rebuilt wholesale on every render, each washed element is rendered wearing its *previous* colour and handed the new one after one forced reflow, keyed by what the wash is about rather than by element. **Cables** are flat by construction: the wire now leaves the entry at its own height and arrives at the same height on the card, clamped into the card's box, instead of taking a fixed inset at each end that differed by 2px. The **wallet** counts above four (`✏️✏️✏️✏️+1`) and says *when* the next one lands rather than how far off it is. And there is a **user avatar** at the right of the topbar — the only face on the surface, which is what 278 is about.
- **292** — salience arriving late and thin: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **(b)**, and folded into SPEC v0.22 §8.3a. Above **2E live questions** the stream reopens at the old ~1 in 10 for everybody, on the reasoning that a room that saturated is one where ordering the work is itself the valuable act; between E and 2E the audience gate alone applies; below E there are none. It answers the objection squarely — the audience gate starves salience of exactly the data it needs while a session is busy — and keeps the original complaint fixed, since fifteen questions on a roster of fourteen is between E and 2E and still gets no stream. My recommendation had been (a), narrowing what salience claims; (b) is the better answer because it keeps the consumer and fixes the supply rather than the other way about.
- **⚖️ becomes 🌶️** (Ed, 2026-08-17): *it's about saying which one is more urgent — judge makes no sense because all cards are judgements.* Scales say *weigh this*, which is what every card on this surface asks for, so they distinguished nothing; a diagonal asks which question is hotter, which is a temperature and not a balance. It also survives 13px where ⚖️ turns to mush, and it is already the hue its card wears. Watch the 🔥/🌶️ adjacency if the alphabet is ever reviewed whole.
- **Cables follow their card's colour** (Ed, 2026-08-17), over the same 700ms and by the same carried-previous-value technique the washes use. Third place that trick has been needed, which makes it a rule: anything rebuilt wholesale that is meant to animate must carry its previous value across the rebuild.
- **The diagonal was offered; now it is served** (Ed, 2026-08-17, reversing 291b's offer the day it was built, and folded into SPEC v0.21 §8.3a). The offer needed somewhere to live and the only place was the top of the rail — which is a margin index, and a panel pinned above it is a banner. The question it asked was one nobody benefits from being asked, since the whole reason a diagonal is cheap at an empty queue is that there is nothing else to do. The panel is gone; the card arrives.
- **A diagonal had a progress bar** (Ed, 2026-08-17): *if salience has no ratification threshold, why does the diagonal card have a progress bar?* It should not have. The fill is closeness-to-resolution and salience never resolves — no threshold, no quorum, which is exactly what lets it be advisory. Its rail entry washes flat now, for the same reason an unproposed draft does: there is nothing to be close to. The rule is written into §8.3a so no surface draws one again.
- **The cable's shadow clipped 3px low** (Ed, 2026-08-17: *how is it you managed to join two things that share a shadow with the tabs, but not with the cables?* — and he was right that it was the same technique, applied wrongly). A  resolves in the user space of the referencing element **after** its own transform, so putting the clip and the shadow offset on one group moved the holes down with the shadow and left a 3px sliver along the top edge of every card the cable met. Invisible on a decision card, where the cable arrives well below the top; obvious on a rail entry, where it arrives at one. Two groups now: clip on the outer, offset on the inner.
- **The diagonal was offered; now it is served** (Ed, 2026-08-17, reversing 291b's offer the day it was built, and folded into SPEC v0.21 §8.3a). The offer needed somewhere to live and the only place was the top of the rail — which is a margin index, and a panel pinned above it is a banner. The question it asked was one nobody benefits from being asked, since the whole reason a diagonal is cheap at an empty queue is that there is nothing else to do. The panel is gone; the card arrives.
- **A diagonal had a progress bar** (Ed, 2026-08-17): *if salience has no ratification threshold, why does the diagonal card have a progress bar?* It should not have. The fill is closeness-to-resolution and salience never resolves — no threshold, no quorum, which is exactly what lets it be advisory. Its rail entry washes flat now, for the same reason an unproposed draft does: there is nothing to be close to. The rule is written into §8.3a so no surface draws one again.
- **The cable's shadow clipped 3px low** (Ed, 2026-08-17: *how is it you managed to join two things that share a shadow with the tabs, but not with the cables?* — and he was right that it is the same technique, applied wrongly). A `clip-path` resolves in the user space of the referencing element **after** its own transform, so putting the clip and the shadow offset on one group moved the holes down with the shadow and left a 3px sliver along the top edge of every card the cable met. Invisible on a decision card, where the cable arrives well below the top; obvious on a rail entry, where it arrives at one. Two groups now: clip on the outer, offset on the inner.
- **291** — where the diagonal lives now that §8.3a has taken it out of the stream: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **(b)**, build the empty-queue state, and built the same day as `end-of-queue`. Until the offer is taken the diagonal is nowhere on the surface — not in the rail, not in the gutter — because it has not been put to you. The three states are reachable in the mockup by judging everything, which is what Ed expected: the queue empties, the offer appears with the live-question count in it, taking it puts the diagonal in the rail, and judging that returns the panel saying the priorities are as clear as more answers would make them. The **three-in-a-row cap** is in the spec but unexercised here, because the fixture holds one diagonal.
- **A locked card dimmed your own answer** (Ed, 2026-08-17, fixed the same day): every radio on a locked card faded to 55%, including the one you chose, so a ⏳ card you had judged looked unanswered. The greying says *you cannot change this*; it should not also dim the record of what you said. The unchosen options fade now and your answer does not.
- **290** — when a salience diagonal is served: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **both gates**, and folded into SPEC v0.20 §8.3a. The flat ~1-in-10 rate is gone. Volume first — at least E **live questions**, with a race counting once because all its candidates prioritise to the same place — then audience: only a participant with nothing else to judge, since everyone else's time is better spent judging. Ed's own constraint closed the obvious hole (*I don't want them to just get an endless stream of prioritisations either*), so it terminates on the same active-sampling rule races use, with a hard ceiling of three in a row. The sampling bias that follows — salience measured on the most active members — is recorded in the spec as a known limitation rather than corrected, because the ranking is advisory and never touches adoption. **Open, in the mockup**: under this rule the session-view fixture's member, who has a full queue, would see **no diagonal at all** — see 291.
- **274** — redlines: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **result-only**, with a helper in place when a proposal removes the text entirely. That is option (a) plus a patch for the case option (a) fails worst at: a partial cut is left to be found by reading against the clause at the head, but a *total* cut would have rendered as blank space, which cannot be told from unchanged, so it says *(all text removed)*. The recommendation had been (b), a machine-drafted sentence saying what changed; Ed's answer is cheaper and does not depend on generating good prose, which was (b)'s whole risk. The rendering was already there — `resultOnly` had been written for 91 and abandoned when 274 brought redlines back — so nothing about the data changed either time, which is the argument for keeping the fixture as a full diff. `Q92` inherits the marking floor. **Note**: nothing in the fixture is a total deletion, so the new helper is unexercised on screen; adding a candidate that deletes a clause outright would show it.
- **289** — *raised to the height of the cards they join*: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 as **elevation**, the reading I had guessed but not built. The cable now casts the same three-layer light as `--shadow-xl`, on the container so the union of the white underlay and the colour over it casts one shadow. The other two readings were vertical position (it should meet the cards at their mid-height) and z-order (which it already had).
- **The QA of 2026-08-17, second pass**, all built the same day. **The sealed record** no longer prints the adopted wording twice: the clause stands at the head, the wording it displaced sits under it in a dashed band of its own, and the winning proposal states no text at all because it *is* the head. **Cables** take the entry's hue at full opacity, 6px, with a 10px cap at the document end only — the rail-end dot said nothing the card it left did not already say. **Gutter marks** are objects rather than glyphs: a resting ground in their own hue, the contact shadow, 30x27 with the glyph at document size, and a real press. **Radios line up down the left edge of every card**, locked ones included — the alignment is what says the three are answers to one question. **Closing a card** animates its whole box back onto its paragraph rather than stopping at head height and jumping the last 93px; and the card's own mark, which had been inert behind a stale guard, closes it again. **The composer** accepts spaces (the lane rewrites its markup on every keystroke, and a trailing space collapsed to nothing in the re-render), its rationale placeholder no longer sits in the text flow pushing the caret ahead of it, and it reads *We should change this because…*.
- **284–287** — the rail-and-radio pass of 2026-08-17, all four built the same day. **284**, the diagonal's title over four lines: the entry carries the two names as `prio` and keeps the flat string for tooltips, so the rail can stack `Prioritise:` / name / `vs` / name while anywhere that needs one line still gets one. **285**, the invisible right edge: settled on Ed's first option — every card now carries a **ground** in its own hue, so the card *is* its colour and the bar reads as how far through that colour you are. Derived from the bar's own colour by swapping the alpha rather than passed in beside it, so a ground can never end up a different hue from its bar. It needed one thing Ed's note did not anticipate: at the bottom of the urgency scale a 0.07 bar over a 0.06 ground has almost no edge, and the *edge* is the whole of what a progress bar says — so the bar's leading edge is drawn a second time in its own colour and composites to roughly double alpha. Ed's second option (stronger edges) was not built: it would have added a rule the palette has spent three passes removing. **286**, the radio: now UI size at weight 600 on a 1.5px edge with a 15px dot, which puts it above the ✏️ beside it and makes the trio the loudest thing on the card. **287**, the wire in the entry's own hue, mixed 28% toward the ink because a hue tuned for a 0.06 wash is not a 1.5px line — and the accent is not missed, because only the open judgment ever draws a wire, so its existence already says *this is the open one*. A filed decision has no hue, and takes the grey its mark and its dot already wear rather than falling back to the blue.
- **283** — the "rationale box" fragment: Ed 2026-08-17, *it should be as high as the text edit box (and maybe the user avatar too, since they are connected) — this is the whole editing surface, as you are expected to fill in both.* Built: the rationale row now sits **inside** the drafting box rather than under it, so the wording, the speaker's disc and the reason are one lifted surface separated by a hairline, and the field needs no elevation of its own. None of my three readings had it — the answer was not about the box's depth but about where its edges are.
- **265** — the composer replacing its clause while a proposed card did not: dissolved by the card rebuild of 2026-08-16, which made every card replace its clause. The question has no subject left.
- **262** — a draft with no rationale: Ed 2026-08-17, *no problem with that*. Nothing enforces one and nothing will; a typo fix genuinely has nothing to say, and a required field would be met with "typo" fourteen times a session, which looks like an argument and is worse than silence.
- **272** — a lane's control: settled by Ed 2026-08-16, *Prefer this*, as built. The alternatives were "This one" / "Chosen" and the bare radio with no label. Worth remembering if the copy is revisited: on a `salience-diagonal` the radios sit under questions rather than wordings, so "prefer" there means something closer to *this deserves the room's time*.
- **273** — the lost open/close motion: settled by Ed 2026-08-16 (*animate the gap in the document sliding open and the card appearing*). Built 2026-08-17. The card no longer grows from nothing; it grows from **exactly the height at which only its own head is showing** — which is the clause, at the size and place it already occupied — so frame one looks like the document with the paragraph still in it, the gap then slides open beneath the clause, and the field fades in 60ms behind the motion. Measured: the clause moves 0px on every card type, and the gap that opens is 283–480px. Closing runs it backwards to head height, where a card and a paragraph are the same shape, so the substitution is never seen.
- **276** — the salience diagonal: settled by Ed 2026-08-17 (*it should be the same as the others*). Its head is the one head on the surface that is not a clause, because a diagonal has none — it spans two, which is what makes it a diagonal — so the head holds the question being put, which is what the slot is actually for: the thing you need before the field makes sense. No `sealed-speaker` on either block, and that is the mechanism rather than an omission: the line under each question describes the dispute, it is not somebody's argument for it, and drawing a person behind it would claim an author the thing does not have. Left behind: 277.
- **268** — the shape of the card: settled by Ed 2026-08-16 (*stacked is better*). The `paired` code paths and the `layout-lab` are deleted rather than left switchable — the whole reason for a lab is that the answer is not known, and it is. Reasoning in the design notes (§ Rebuilding the card as a post and its replies).
- **270 and 275**, both closed by Ed's follow-up of 2026-08-16 (*why is the editing card still paired?*). There was no good answer. It had stayed a side-by-side pair on the argument that while writing you want the original level with what you are typing, which does not survive contact: the original is one line up, your own additions are marked green as you type (263), and a full-width lane is a far better place to write a paragraph of prose than a 300px column. It is now clause-at-the-head like every card, with your draft as the single reply — and its rationale field moved into the speaker's own slot beneath the wording, which is 270: you now write the sentence in the place, and at the weight, that everybody else will read it in.
- **202, 204, 209 and 213**, all reversed by Ed 2026-08-16 in one instruction about the commit row. Indifference is the *last radio in the trio*, labelled rather than drawn as 🤷, because every candidate now carries a radio and *neither of these* is plainly one more answer to the same question. Submit is always rendered and greyed until something is chosen, rather than absent until then — 202's argument was that a disabled button is a thing you are being told off by; against it, an absent one gives the row no shape and the reader no idea what finishing looks like. The two no longer share a slot: indifference sits at the left of the row, the tick at the right. 🤷 has left the surface entirely.
- **269** — boxes or a hairline: settled by Ed 2026-08-16 (*hairline is nice*). Baked into the base rules rather than left switchable; the `editing-card` is the only place boxes survive, because its right lane is a text editor. The dividend was unplanned: with the boxes gone the rule between two lanes lands on the same pixel as the rule between the two rationales below them.
- **271** — the lifecycle mark reading as a label on the left-hand rationale: dissolved by the card rebuild of 2026-08-16 rather than answered. There is one rationale per proposal now and the mark sits on the clause at the head, one per card, which is what it was always about.
- **87 and 197, both reversed by Ed's QA of 2026-08-16.** 87 put the rationale above the lanes so you met the reason before the wording; the card now reads status quo → texts → rationale → buttons, so you form a view of the wording before being told what to think of it. 197 made the lane itself the button; the choosing has moved to a control at the foot of each lane, because clicking a paragraph of the charter is the gesture the composer now means by *put a caret here* (224), and because Ed's own reading was that he had been wrong about it. Neither number is reopened — they are recorded here so the reasoning in the design notes is findable from the numbers it overturns.
- **222–223** — the open entry's absolute claim on its clause's line, and ranking a deadlocked race by its bounty score. Both built 2026-08-16; reasoning in the design notes.
- **224–243** — the composing-surface design conversation of 2026-08-16 (Ed answered every one in chat), **built 2026-08-16**. The UI decisions and the reasoning behind the build are in `design/session-view.notes.md` (§ The composer); the three mechanism changes they forced — the propose-C forfeit dropped, the author counting toward their own floor, and withdrawal semantics — are folded into SPEC v0.16 at §3.3, §3.3a, §3.5 and §8.2. What the build itself raised is 260–266 above.
- **Mechanism decisions** are folded into SPEC.md — including 8–10 and 13, resolved on sim evidence (see `packages/sim-harness/REPORT-deferred-evidence.md`).
- **Session-view UX decisions, 61–91, 93, 95, 104–108, 110, 112, 114–121, 123, 147–157, 160, 162, 164–167, 172–173, 177–181, 183, 184, 186, 188, 190, 194–195, 197–206, 209–210, 213–217**, are recorded in `design/session-view.notes.md` alongside the reasoning. They are not repeated here.
- **126–146** were the inventory of queue-card kinds Ed asked for on 2026-08-16 to choose symbols against; the choices they fed are 147–157, and the inventory itself is in the notes' lifecycle section.
- **323** dissolved by Ed’s answer-card design of 2026-08-18: delegation no longer moves any tab between piles — the setting card stays in the convenor’s pile and a question card is *born* in the membership pile — so there is no jump left for an open card to make.
- **351** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18: (a) the 👑 question is a **new kind of task** — news-pinned, its commit row Accept / Reject; (b) one pending at crown lapse **passes automatically**; (c) no timeout — **the crown has unilateral control over reserved matters** (direct change, no motion), and proposals on reserved matters require assent to pass. SPEC §9.7, v0.45.
- **327** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18: **one 🏛️ per member at a time** — a constitutional motion is free, any member may raise one, and the limit is the member rather than the setting: withdraw it and the 🏛️ comes back whole; it also returns when the motion settles. Supersedes the per-setting/seconding recommendations. Surface gesture: the `assembly-press`, a full ten-second hold in which the members’ avatars convene in a circle around the button. SPEC §9.6, v0.43.
- **347** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18: a **card** is the UX element; a **task** is the general term for *a thing that gets tracked by sidebar cards and opens a document card with the controls*. Glossary updated. **348** answered: (a) via the join policy — *applications must be proposed* is a policy rung, and a second IS a proposed application, the seconding member staking the ✏️; direct applications (where the policy allows them) are free, the tasks their price and the bar their filter. (b) the holder/carve-out reading confirmed and now policy-expressed. (c) stands as recommended unless Ed objects: one class of membership — §9.6a applies in full however you came in.
- **345** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18 with the sunset option: memberships may **lapse**, a constitutional setting — *do they, and after how long a quiet spell* — with warnings by email first and the package (document + record) sent at the lapse itself. SPEC §9.5a; 💤 card on both surfaces.
- **344** closed by Ed, 2026-08-18: 🎩 decides *whether* the convenor owes answers (a clerk owes none), which determines *where* — once it says yes they are a member, so the delegated card on their own surface carries the member’s answering body above the watching half. Built; the consent controls and answer bodies moved into setup.js as shared vocabulary, which also surfaced and fixed a rung that never lit (the machines ladder compared a boolean to a string).
- **338** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18: quorum is a fixed count or a share of the membership. Folded with the floor unification — F = max(Q, min(⌈E/3⌉, F_max)), §4.2/§8.2 — which is the reading of the card copy Ed had already approved (*how many must weigh in before it can change the charter*); flagged for veto in chat. **339** answered: membership begins at first arrival; an invitation is an invitation to become a member, invitees listed as *invited*; the convenor removes freely before the start. **340** resolved by the principle Ed’s answer pointed at: *before the start nothing is amended, only set* — §9.6a; roster changes during answering are a ground shift (notify, may revise); after the start both directions are constitutional motions, and removal needs the removed member’s own consent, so expulsion is effectively impossible (stated plainly; the residue is Q345). **341** rethought from scratch at Ed’s word: *delegate the decision, not the field* — the close bar is consent and the ramp is pacing (ordinary!), the quorum number is consent and its form is the convenor’s wording; ✒️ split into two cards, seventeen became eighteen. **342** answered fine: the ramp and the drip run from judging-open. **343** answered fine: the proposal rate settles prospectively only.
- **334** answered yes by Ed, 2026-08-18: the founding questions and motions must be simulable, so the §9 layer goes in **its own package** beside engine-core, drivable by sim-harness, consumed by the server.
- **336** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18: a new domain, **docs.vote**, with **Resend** as the transactional sender — option (a), ours. The host follows (any managed platform; no interesting difference at this size).
- **329** answered (a) by Ed, 2026-08-18: a motion’s route is read off the value at compose time, so the ⏰ card offers `routeOf` and the price and the note flip as you type. **330** answered (b): the Proposing and Judging gate cards stand in the members’ pile. **332** kept: the approval-threshold slider moves in fives.
- **321** answered by Ed, 2026-08-18 and folded into SPEC §9.6: a setting is changed by a **motion**, and the route it takes is a fact about the setting — ordinary at the bar, constitutional by the consent rule. **322** answered (b): the economy defaults to the convenor, which generalised into §9.7 (*constitutional settings default to the roster, ordinary ones to the convenor*).
- **316** answered (b) by Ed, 2026-08-18 — the tabs pile like clause-tabs, in two piles by holder. **317** answered: a name and a picture are two cards. **318** answered: personal cards sit in the participants’ pile, which is *everything the participants hold, collectively or individually*. **320** closed by grouping the rail by pile in pile order, so nothing moves on confirm and the only thing that does move is a genuine change of holder.
- **Ephemeral chat items** that never became project decisions: 30, 33–41, 44–45, 47, 51, 57–60, 83–84.

Two exceptions worth knowing. **46 is parked, not spent** — diagonal-card visual distinction, to be decided with the palette work. And **73** was raised in chat but folded into 72(b), so it stands for nothing on its own.
